Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.3.1.1, PDF p.9

3.3.1.1 The chapter 3, Principles, specifies the system principles of ETCS/ERTMS. These principles apply to on-board and trackside subsystems.

Clause 3.3.1.2 — The principles define the operational and technical behaviour of the system in general

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.3.1.2, PDF p.9

3.3.1.2 The principles define the operational and technical behaviour of the system in general and functional terms.

Clause 3.3.1.3 — The chapter is divided into subchapters. In each subchapter normally several

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.3.1.3, PDF p.9

3.3.1.3 The chapter is divided into subchapters. In each subchapter normally several requirements are defined. Each requirement is identified with a unique identification number.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.3.1.4, PDF p.9

3.3.1.4 Notes, Justifications and Examples are only informative and shall not be regarded as requirements.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.1.1, PDF p.9

3.4.1.1 A balise group shall consist of between one and eight balises.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.1.2, PDF p.9

3.4.1.2 In every balise shall at least be stored: a) The internal number (from 1 to 8) of the balise b) The number of balises inside the group c) The balise group identity.

Clause 3.4.1.3 — The internal number of the balise describes the relative position of the balise in the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.1.3, PDF p.9

3.4.2.2.2 The nominal direction of each balise group is defined by increasing internal balise numbers.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.13

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.4.3 — Balise Information Types and Usage

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.3, PDF p.14

3.4.3 Balise Information Types and Usage

Clause 3.4.3.1 — In level 1, all information to the on-board system is given from balise groups or

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.3.1, PDF p.14

3.4.3.1 In level 1, all information to the on-board system is given from balise groups or additionally from EUROLOOPS or Radio Infill Units (see section 3.9). In level 2/3, balise groups are mostly used for location information.

Clause 3.4.3.2 — A balise may contain directional information, i.e. valid either for nominal or for reverse

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.3.2, PDF p.14

3.4.3.2 A balise may contain directional information, i.e. valid either for nominal or for reverse direction, or may contain information valid for both directions. In level 1, this information can be of the following type (please refer to section 3.8.5): a) Non-infill b) Intentionally deleted c) Infill.

Clause 3.4.3.2.1 — Intentionally deleted.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.3.2.1, PDF p.14

3.4.3.2.1 Intentionally deleted.

Clause 3.4.3.2.2 — Note: Infill information is referring to the location reference of an announced balise

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.3.2.2, PDF p.14

3.4.3.2.2 Note: Infill information is referring to the location reference of an announced balise group.

Clause 3.4.3.3 — Some information shall be read also in sleeping mode and when no linking information

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.3.3, PDF p.14

3.4.3.3 Some information shall be read also in sleeping mode and when no linking information is available (see Chapter 4 Use of received information). If such information is transmitted by balises, and if the information is directional, balise groups consisting of at least two balises shall be used.

Clause 3.4.4.1.1 — Aim of linking:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.4.1.1, PDF p.14

3.4.4.1.1 Aim of linking:  To determine whether a balise group has been missed or not found within the expectation window (see section 3.4.4.4) and take the appropriate action.  To assign a co-ordinate system to balise groups consisting of a single balise.  To correct the confidence interval due to odometer inaccuracy (see section 3.6.4).

Clause 3.4.4.1.2 — A balise group is linked when its linking information (see section 3.4.4.2) is known in

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.4.1.2, PDF p.14

3.4.4.1.2 A balise group is linked when its linking information (see section 3.4.4.2) is known in advance.

Clause 3.4.4.1.2.1 — Note: In cases where a balise group contains repositioning information, the term

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.4.1.2.1, PDF p.14

3.4.4.1.2.1 Note: In cases where a balise group contains repositioning information, the term linked also applies since the balise group is announced, marked as linked and contains repositioning information marked accordingly.

Clause 3.4.4.2 — Content of linking information

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.4.2, PDF p.14

3.4.4.2 Content of linking information

Clause 3.4.4.2.1 — Linking information shall be composed of:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.4.2.1, PDF p.14

3.4.4.2.1 Linking information shall be composed of: a) The identity of the linked balise group. b) Where the location reference of the group has to be found.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.14

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP c) The accuracy of this location. Note: If the reference balise is duplicated, it is the trackside responsibility to define the location accuracy to cover at least the location of the two duplicated balises. d) The direction with which the linked balise group will be passed over (nominal or reverse). e) The reaction required if a data consistency problem occurs with the expected balise group.

Clause 3.4.4.2.1.1 — "Linking information is used" shall be interpreted as when balise group(s) are

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.4.2.1.1, PDF p.15

3.4.4.2.1.1 "Linking information is used" shall be interpreted as when balise group(s) are announced and the minimum safe antenna position has not yet passed the expectation window of the furthest announced balise group.

Clause 3.4.4.2.2 — Instead of the identity of a linked balise group it shall be possible to identify a following

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.4.2.2, PDF p.15

3.4.4.2.2 Instead of the identity of a linked balise group it shall be possible to identify a following linked balise group as unknown but containing repositioning information

Clause 3.4.4.2.2.1 — Intentionally deleted.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.4.2.2.1, PDF p.15

3.4.4.2.2.1 Intentionally deleted.

Clause 3.4.4.2.2.2 — Note 1: Regarding the repositioning information, see chapter 3.8.5.3.5 and 3.8.5.2.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.4.2.2.2, PDF p.15

3.4.4.2.2.2 Note 1: Regarding the repositioning information, see chapter 3.8.5.3.5 and 3.8.5.2.

Clause 3.4.4.2.2.3 — Note 2: In case the identity of the next balise group is not unambiguously known

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.4.2.2.3, PDF p.15

3.4.4.2.2.3 Note 2: In case the identity of the next balise group is not unambiguously known because the route is not known by the trackside, this feature allows to link this balise group.

Clause 3.4.4.2.3 — For each linked balise group, the trackside shall select one of the following reactions to

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.4.2.3, PDF p.15

3.4.4.2.3 For each linked balise group, the trackside shall select one of the following reactions to be used in case of data inconsistencies: a) Train trip (Trip mode, see Chapter 4) b) Command service brake c) No reaction For further details see section 3.16.2.

Clause 3.4.4.3 — Unlinked Balise Groups

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.4.3, PDF p.15

3.4.4.3 Unlinked Balise Groups

Clause 3.4.4.3.1 — A balise group, which contains information that must be considered even when the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.4.3.1, PDF p.15

3.4.4.3.1 A balise group, which contains information that must be considered even when the balise group is not announced by linking, is called an unlinked balise group.

Clause 3.4.4.3.2 — Unlinked balise groups shall consist at minimum of two balises.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.4.3.2, PDF p.15

3.4.4.3.2 Unlinked balise groups shall consist at minimum of two balises.

Clause 3.4.4.3.3 — Unlinked balise groups shall always contain the unlinked balise group qualifier.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.4.3.3, PDF p.15

3.4.4.3.3 Unlinked balise groups shall always contain the unlinked balise group qualifier.

Clause 3.4.4.4 — Rules related to linking

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.4.4, PDF p.15

3.4.4.4 Rules related to linking

Clause 3.4.4.4.1 — When no linking information is used on-board, all balise groups shall be taken into

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.4.4.1, PDF p.15

3.4.4.4.1 When no linking information is used on-board, all balise groups shall be taken into account.

Clause 3.4.4.4.2 — When linking information is used on-board, only balise groups marked as linked and

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.4.4.2, PDF p.15

3.4.4.4.2 When linking information is used on-board, only balise groups marked as linked and included in the linking information and balise groups marked as unlinked shall be taken into account.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.15

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.4.4.4.2.1 — When linking information is used on-board and the expected balise group is referred

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.4.4.2.1, PDF p.16

3.4.4.4.2.1 When linking information is used on-board and the expected balise group is referred in the linking information with a balise group with ID “unknown”, a balise group marked as linked shall only be taken into account if: a) the on-board equipment can determine the orientation of the linked balise group by information from the balise group itself (therefore excluding for example single balise groups), AND b) the balise group contains repositioning information valid for the train orientation, AND c) the balise group is crossed with the direction announced in the linking information.

Clause 3.4.4.4.3 — The on-board equipment shall accept a balise group marked as linked and included in

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.4.4.3, PDF p.16

3.4.4.4.3 The on-board equipment shall accept a balise group marked as linked and included in the linking information (i.e. the balise giving the location reference) from  when the max safe front end of the train has passed the first possible location of the balise group until  the min safe front end of the train has passed the last possible location of the balise group taking the offset between the front of the train and the balise antenna into account.

Clause 3.4.4.4.3.1 — Note: The first possible location and the last possible location of the balise group

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.4.4.3.1, PDF p.16

3.4.4.4.3.1 Note: The first possible location and the last possible location of the balise group are defined by the linking distance and the location accuracy.

Clause 3.4.4.4.3.2 — Note: The interval between the outer limits to accept the balise group defines the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.4.4.3.2, PDF p.16

3.4.4.4.3.2 Note: The interval between the outer limits to accept the balise group defines the expectation window.

Clause 3.4.4.4.4 — In case of a balise group containing repositioning information, the first possible location

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.4.4.4, PDF p.16

3.4.4.4.4 In case of a balise group containing repositioning information, the first possible location shall start from the previously linked balise group.

Clause 3.4.4.4.5 — The on-board equipment shall expect balise groups one by one (i.e., it shall supervise

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.4.4.5, PDF p.16

3.4.4.4.5 The on-board equipment shall expect balise groups one by one (i.e., it shall supervise only one expectation window at a time) according to the order given by linking information.

Clause 3.4.4.4.6 — The ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall stop expecting a balise group and shall

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.4.4.6, PDF p.16

3.4.4.4.6 The ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall stop expecting a balise group and shall expect the next one announced in the linking information (if any) when: a) the balise group is found inside its expectation window b) a linking consistency error is found, see 3.16.2.3.1

Clause 3.4.4.4.6.1 — Linking consistency error due to early reception of balise group expected later (see

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.4.4.4.6.1, PDF p.16

3.4.4.4.6.1 Linking consistency error due to early reception of balise group expected later (see

Clause 3.16.2.3.1 — c)): if the balise group found is the next one announced in the linking

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.3.1, PDF p.16

3.16.2.3.1 c)): if the balise group found is the next one announced in the linking information, the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall check its linking consistency and apply again clause 3.4.4.4.6, i.e. it will immediately expect the further next balise group announced in the linking information.

Clause 3.5 — Management of Radio Communication

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5, PDF p.17

3.5 Management of Radio Communication

Clause 3.5.1.1 — Note: the following section refers to the behaviour of the user application interacting

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.1.1, PDF p.17

3.5.1.1 Note: the following section refers to the behaviour of the user application interacting with Euroradio protocols. How the messages are actually transported from the sender to the receiver user application is not relevant for this description.

Clause 3.5.2.1 — Each communication session managed by an entity allows the exchange of data with

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.2.1, PDF p.17

3.5.2.1 Each communication session managed by an entity allows the exchange of data with only one other entity.

Clause 3.5.2.2 — Note: in the following sections reference is made to safe radio connections, whose

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.2.2, PDF p.17

3.5.2.2 Note: in the following sections reference is made to safe radio connections, whose definition and management is contained in Euroradio specification.

Clause 3.5.2.3 — Note: The information Initiation of a Communication Session and Version not

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.2.3, PDF p.17

3.5.2.3 Note: The information Initiation of a Communication Session and Version not Compatible (see sections 3.5.2.4 and 3.17) are the same in every system version.

Clause 3.5.2.4 — The ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall be able to manage simultaneous

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.2.4, PDF p.17

3.5.2.4 The ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall be able to manage simultaneous communication sessions with at least two different entities.

Clause 3.5.3 — Establishing a communication session

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.3, PDF p.17

3.5.3 Establishing a communication session

Clause 3.5.3.1 — Only the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment can initiate a communication session.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.3.1, PDF p.17

3.5.3.1 Only the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment can initiate a communication session.

Clause 3.5.3.2 — Intentionally deleted.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.3.2, PDF p.17

3.5.3.2 Intentionally deleted.

Clause 3.5.3.3 — Note: Only communication sessions between an ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.3.3, PDF p.17

3.5.3.3 Note: Only communication sessions between an ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment and a trackside equipment (RBC or Radio Infill Unit) are considered here.

Clause 3.5.3.4 — The on-board shall establish a communication session

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.3.4, PDF p.17

3.5.3.4 The on-board shall establish a communication session a) At Start of Mission (only if level 2 or 3). b) If ordered from trackside. c) If a mode change, neither considered as an End of Mission nor triggered from condition g) below, has to be reported to the RBC (only if level 2 or 3) d) If the driver has manually changed the level to 2 or 3 e) When the train front reaches the end of an announced radio hole f) When the previous communication session is considered as terminated due to loss of safe radio connection (refer to 3.5.4.2.1) g) When a Start of Mission procedure, during which no communication session could be established, is completed in level 2 or 3 h) When outside the Start of Mission procedure, the driver has manually selected the RBC contact information (only if level 2 or 3)

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.17

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.5.3.4.1 — In respect of a), b), c), d), e) and h) of 3.5.3.4, the on-board shall not establish a new

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.3.4.1, PDF p.18

3.5.3.4.1 In respect of a), b), c), d), e) and h) of 3.5.3.4, the on-board shall not establish a new communication session with an RBC/RIU in case a communication session is currently being established or is already established with this RBC/RIU.

Clause 3.5.3.5 — The order to contact an RBC shall include

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.3.5, PDF p.18

3.5.3.5 The order to contact an RBC shall include a) The identity of the RBC. b) The telephone number of the RBC. c) The action to be performed (establish/terminate the session). d) Whether this applies also to Sleeping units.

Clause 3.5.3.5.1 — See table at the end of section 3.5.3.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.3.5.1, PDF p.18

3.5.3.5.1 See table at the end of section 3.5.3.

Clause 3.5.3.5.2 — If the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment has to establish a communication session

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.3.5.2, PDF p.18

3.5.3.5.2 If the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment has to establish a communication session with an RBC whilst in session with another RBC, the existing communication session shall be terminated (see 3.5.5.2 for details) and the new one shall be established. Exception: the order to contact an Accepting RBC shall not terminate the communication session with the Handing Over RBC.

Clause 3.5.3.5.3 — The order to contact an Accepting RBC shall be part of the RBC transition order and

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.3.5.3, PDF p.18

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.3.6, PDF p.18

3.5.3.6 The order to contact a Radio Infill Unit shall include a) The identity of the Radio Infill Unit b) The telephone number of the Radio Infill Unit c) The action to be performed (establish/terminate the session).

Clause 3.5.3.7 — The establishment of a communication session shall be performed according to the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.3.7, PDF p.18

3.5.3.7 The establishment of a communication session shall be performed according to the following steps: a) The on-board shall request the set-up of a safe radio connection with the trackside. If this request is part of an ongoing Start of Mission procedure or is related to the establishment of a communication session due to condition 3.5.3.4 c), it shall be repeated until successful or a defined number of times (see Appendix A.3.1). If this request is not part of an ongoing Start of Mission procedure and is not related to the establishment of a communication session due to condition 3.5.3.4 c), it shall be repeated until successful. A request shall be repeated immediately after EURORADIO has indicated that setting up the safe radio connection has failed.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.18

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP b) As soon as the safe radio connection is set-up, the on-board shall send the message Initiation of communication session to the trackside. c) As soon as the trackside receives the information, it shall send the system version. d) When the on-board receives the system version it shall consider the communication session established and:  If one of its supported system versions is compatible with the one sent by trackside, it shall send a session established report, including its supported system versions, to the trackside.  If none of its supported system versions is compatible with the one sent by trackside, it shall send a version independent message indicating “No compatible version supported”. It shall inform the driver and shall terminate the communication session. e) When the trackside receives the session established report or the information that no compatible system version is supported by the on-board, it shall consider the communication session established. On-Board Trackside Set-up of the safe connection According to EURORADIO specifications Initiation of a communication session Communication RBC/RIU session System Version established for on-board Session Communication session established established for trackside Figure 3: Establishment initiated by on-board

Clause 3.5.4 — Maintaining a communication session

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.4, PDF p.21

3.5.4 Maintaining a communication session

Clause 3.5.4.1 — When a communication session is established, in case of a loss of the safe radio

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.4.1, PDF p.21

3.5.4.1 When a communication session is established, in case of a loss of the safe radio connection, i.e., if the disconnection has not been ordered (see 3.5.5.1), the involved entities shall consider the communication session still established for a defined time. The defined time shall start as soon as EURORADIO has indicated the loss of the safe radio connection.

Clause 3.5.4.2 — When EURORADIO indicates the loss of the safe radio connection, the ERTMS/ETCS

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.4.2, PDF p.21

3.5.4.2 When EURORADIO indicates the loss of the safe radio connection, the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall immediately try to set-up a new safe radio connection.

Clause 3.5.4.2.1 — If the safe radio connection is not re-established after the defined time (as defined in

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.4.2.1, PDF p.21

3.5.4.2.1 If the safe radio connection is not re-established after the defined time (as defined in A.3.1), both, on-board equipment and trackside, shall consider the session as terminated.

Clause 3.5.4.3 — The attempts shall be repeated, until at least one of the following conditions is met:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.4.3, PDF p.21

3.5.4.3 The attempts shall be repeated, until at least one of the following conditions is met:  The safe radio connection is set-up.  The session is considered as terminated.  The train passes the location indicated in the RIU order “Terminate the communication session”

Clause 3.5.4.3.1 — Note: if the session is considered as terminated due to 3.5.4.2.1, the attempts will be

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.4.3.1, PDF p.21

3.5.4.3.1 Note: if the session is considered as terminated due to 3.5.4.2.1, the attempts will be resumed immediately according to 3.5.3.4 f).

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.21

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.5.4.4 — Exception to 3.5.4.2 and 3.5.4.3: the on-board equipment shall not try to set up a new

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.4.4, PDF p.22

3.5.4.4 Exception to 3.5.4.2 and 3.5.4.3: the on-board equipment shall not try to set up a new safe radio connection and shall stop any ongoing attempts if the train front is inside an announced radio hole (see 3.12.1.3). The on-board equipment shall try to set it up again when the train front reaches the end of the radio hole.

Clause 3.5.4.5 — In case a message has to be sent during the loss of the safe radio connection, this

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.4.5, PDF p.22

3.5.4.5 In case a message has to be sent during the loss of the safe radio connection, this message shall be considered as sent.

Clause 3.5.5 — Terminating a communication session

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.5, PDF p.22

3.5.5 Terminating a communication session

Clause 3.5.5.1 — The termination of a communication session shall be initiated only by the on-board and

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.5.1, PDF p.22

3.5.5.1 The termination of a communication session shall be initiated only by the on-board and in the following cases: a) If an order is received from trackside (RBC, RIU or balise groups) (see sections

Clause 3.5.3.5 — and 3.5.3.6 concerning the content of the order).

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.3.5, PDF p.22

3.5.3.5 and 3.5.3.6 concerning the content of the order). b) If a condition requiring the termination of the communication session is fulfilled without any explicit trackside order. The situations in which such condition is met are detailed in other sections of this specification. c) Intentionally deleted. d) Intentionally deleted. e) Intentionally deleted. f) Intentionally deleted.

Clause 3.5.5.2 — In case a session is established, the on-board equipment shall terminate the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.5.2, PDF p.22

3.5.5.2 In case a session is established, the on-board equipment shall terminate the communication session according to the following steps: a) The on-board equipment shall send a Termination of communication session message. b) As soon as this information is received, the trackside shall consider the communication session terminated and send an acknowledgement to the on-board. c) When the acknowledgement is received the on-board shall consider the communication session terminated and request the release of the safe radio connection with trackside.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.22

Clause 3.5.5.3 — No further message shall be sent by the on-board after the message Termination of

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.5.3, PDF p.23

3.5.5.3 No further message shall be sent by the on-board after the message Termination of communication session.

Clause 3.5.5.3.1 — Exception: In case a communication session is established and no acknowledgement

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.5.3.1, PDF p.23

3.5.5.3.1 Exception: In case a communication session is established and no acknowledgement is received within a fixed waiting time (see Appendix A.3.1) after sending the “Termination of communication session” message, the message shall be repeated with the fixed waiting time after each repetition.

Clause 3.5.5.3.2 — After a defined number of repetitions (see Appendix A.3.1), and if no acknowledgment

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.5.3.2, PDF p.23

3.5.5.3.2 After a defined number of repetitions (see Appendix A.3.1), and if no acknowledgment is received within the fixed waiting time from the time of the last sending of “Termination of communication session”, the ERTMS/ETCS onboard equipment shall consider the communication session terminated.

Clause 3.5.5.4 — No further message shall be sent by the trackside after the message

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.5.4, PDF p.23

3.5.5.4 No further message shall be sent by the trackside after the message Acknowledgement of the termination of communication session.

Clause 3.5.5.5 — Note: The information Termination of Communication Session and corresponding

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.5.5, PDF p.23

3.5.5.5 Note: The information Termination of Communication Session and corresponding Acknowledgement are the same in every system version.

Clause 3.5.5.6 — Messages from the RBC received onboard after the message “Termination of

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.5.6, PDF p.23

3.5.5.6 Messages from the RBC received onboard after the message “Termination of communication session” has been sent shall be ignored with the exception of the Acknowledgement of the termination of communication session.

Clause 3.5.6 — Registering to the Radio Network

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.6, PDF p.23

3.5.6 Registering to the Radio Network

Clause 3.5.6.1 — ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall order the registration of its connected Mobile

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.6.1, PDF p.23

3.5.6.1 ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall order the registration of its connected Mobile Terminal(s) to a Radio Network: a) At power-up

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.23

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP b) Following driver entry of a new Radio Network identity c) If ordered from the trackside

Clause 3.5.6.2 — When powered-off, ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall memorize the last

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.6.2, PDF p.24

3.5.6.2 When powered-off, ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall memorize the last received Radio Network identity (from trackside or from driver) and shall use it when powered-up again.

Clause 3.5.6.3 — If no Radio Network identity received from trackside or from driver could have been

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.6.3, PDF p.24

3.5.6.3 If no Radio Network identity received from trackside or from driver could have been memorized by ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment (e.g. after a System Failure or at very first power-up), this latter shall nevertheless order the registration of its Mobile Terminal(s) to a default Radio Network.

Clause 3.5.6.3.1 — Note 1: the source used to retrieve the default Radio Network identity (on-board

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.6.3.1, PDF p.24

3.5.6.3.1 Note 1: the source used to retrieve the default Radio Network identity (on-board equipment permanent storage, Mobile Terminal itself, or other external source) is implementation dependent.

Clause 3.5.6.3.2 — Note 2: if ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment is powered-up in an area not covered by

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.6.3.2, PDF p.24

3.5.6.3.2 Note 2: if ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment is powered-up in an area not covered by the memorized or default Radio Network, attempts to register to this Radio Network will be repeated unconditionally by the Mobile Terminal(s) until either an attempt is successful or a new Radio Network identity is received from trackside or from driver, preventing Mobile Terminal(s) from registering to any unwanted Radio Network.

Clause 3.5.6.4 — Intentionally deleted.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.6.4, PDF p.24

3.5.6.4 Intentionally deleted.

Clause 3.5.6.5 — On reception of the trackside order, ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.6.5, PDF p.24

3.5.6.5 On reception of the trackside order, ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall immediately order the Radio Network registration of each Mobile Terminal that fulfils the following conditions: a) it is not yet registered to the ordered Radio Network, AND b) it is not used for an established communication session, AND c) no safe radio connection is being set-up

Clause 3.5.6.6 — If a Mobile Terminal is not currently registered to the Radio Network ordered by

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.6.6, PDF p.24

3.5.6.6 If a Mobile Terminal is not currently registered to the Radio Network ordered by trackside and if one of the conditions b) or c) is not fulfilled, ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall initiate the Radio Network registration once communication session is terminated and safe radio connection is released.

Clause 3.5.6.7 — If no Mobile Terminal is duly registered to a Radio Network, any order to contact an

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.6.7, PDF p.24

3.5.6.7 If no Mobile Terminal is duly registered to a Radio Network, any order to contact an RBC or an RIU received from trackside shall be rejected by ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment.

Clause 3.5.7 — Safe Radio Connection Indication

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.7, PDF p.24

3.5.7 Safe Radio Connection Indication

Clause 3.5.7.1 — The ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall inform the driver about the status of the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.7.1, PDF p.24

3.5.7.1 The ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall inform the driver about the status of the safe radio connection. To that purpose, the following indication statuses of the safe radio connection are defined: “No Connection”, “Connection Lost/Set-Up failed”, “Connection Up”.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.24

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.5.7.2 — In addition, the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall use a “connection status”

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.7.2, PDF p.25

3.5.7.2 In addition, the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall use a “connection status” timer (see Appendix A.3.1), in order to manage properly the transitions to the indication status “Connection Lost/Set-Up failed”.

Clause 3.5.7.2.1 — Note: The purpose of the “connection status” timer is to avoid distracting the driver for

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.7.2.1, PDF p.25

3.5.7.2.1 Note: The purpose of the “connection status” timer is to avoid distracting the driver for any short disturbance of the safe radio connection.

Clause 3.5.7.3 — The ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall start the “connection status” timer as

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.7.3, PDF p.25

3.5.7.3 The ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall start the “connection status” timer as soon as the first request to set-up a safe radio connection with the relevant RBC/RIU is sent: a) for what regards the session establishment, see items b), c), d), e) in 3.5.3.4. b) for what regards maintaining a communication session, see 3.5.4.2 and 3.5.4.4

Clause 3.5.7.4 — If the “connection status” timer is ongoing, it shall be stopped if the requests to set-up

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.7.4, PDF p.25

3.5.7.4 If the “connection status” timer is ongoing, it shall be stopped if the requests to set-up a safe radio connection are stopped with the relevant RBC/RIU.

Clause 3.5.7.5 — The ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall execute the transitions between the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.7.5, PDF p.25

3.5.7.5 The ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall execute the transitions between the different indication statuses of the safe radio connection with the relevant RBC/RIU as described in Table 1 according to the conditions in Table 2 (see section 4.6.1 for details about the symbols). Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.25

Clause 3.5.7.6 — For the case of an RBC/RBC transition, the safe radio connection indicated to the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.7.6, PDF p.26

3.5.7.6 For the case of an RBC/RBC transition, the safe radio connection indicated to the driver shall switch from the indication status of the safe radio connection with the Handing over RBC to the one with the Accepting RBC as soon as one of the following conditions is met: a) the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment sends a position report directly to the Accepting RBC with its maximum safe front end having passed the border, see

Clause 3.15.1.3.5 — (i.e. the Accepting RBC becomes the supervising RBC),

3.15.1.3.5 (i.e. the Accepting RBC becomes the supervising RBC), b) the safe radio connection is released with the Handing over RBC and the minimum safe rear end of the train has crossed the border, see 3.15.1.2.7 and 3.15.1.3.9.

Clause 3.5.7.6.1 — Note: During an RBC/RBC handover procedure, an indication status transition table

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.7.6.1, PDF p.26

3.5.7.6.1 Note: During an RBC/RBC handover procedure, an indication status transition table and a connection status timer might have to be managed at the same time, for each RBC.

Clause 3.5.7.7 — For the case of safe radio connection with RIU’s, the safe radio connection indicated to

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.5.7.7, PDF p.26

3.5.7.7 For the case of safe radio connection with RIU’s, the safe radio connection indicated to the driver shall be the one related to the current infill area.

Clause 3.6 — Location Principles, Train Position and Train Orientation

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6, PDF p.26

3.6 Location Principles, Train Position and Train Orientation

Clause 3.6.1.1 — Two types of location based data are defined:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.1.1, PDF p.26

3.6.1.1 Two types of location based data are defined: a) Data that refers only to a given location, referred to as Location data (e.g. level transition orders, linking)

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.26

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP b) Data that remains valid for a certain distance, referred to as Profile data (e.g. SSP, gradient).

Clause 3.6.1.2 — Note: Determination of the Train Position is always longitudinal along the route, even

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.1.2, PDF p.27

3.6.1.2 Note: Determination of the Train Position is always longitudinal along the route, even though the route might be set through a complex track layout. 1 2 3 4 5 6 7 Figure 6: Actual route of the train 1 5 7 Figure 7: Route known by the train

Clause 3.6.1.3 — The Train Position information defines the position of the train front in relation to a

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.1.3, PDF p.27

3.6.1.3 The Train Position information defines the position of the train front in relation to a balise group, which is called LRBG (the Last Relevant Balise Group). It includes:  The estimated train front end position, defined by the estimated distance between the LRBG and the front end of the train  The train position confidence interval (see 3.6.4)  Directional train position information in reference to the balise group orientation (see 3.4.2, also Figure 14) of the LRBG, regarding:  the position of the train front end (nominal or reverse side of the LRBG)  the train orientation  the train running direction In case of an LRBG being a single balise group with no co-ordinate system assigned, directional information is defined in reference to the pair of LRBG and “previous LRBG”, see 3.4.2.3.3  A list of LRBGs, which may alternatively be used by trackside for referencing location dependent information (see 3.6.2.2.2 c)).

Clause 3.6.1.4 — Balise groups, which are marked as unlinked, shall never be used as LRBG.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.1.4, PDF p.27

3.6.1.4 Balise groups, which are marked as unlinked, shall never be used as LRBG.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.27

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.6.1.4.1 — Justification: The location of an unlinked balise group, or the balise group itself, may

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.1.4.1, PDF p.28

3.6.1.4.1 Justification: The location of an unlinked balise group, or the balise group itself, may not be known to the RBC.

Clause 3.6.1.5 — If there is an active cab, this one defines the orientation of the train, i.e. the side of the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.1.5, PDF p.28

3.6.1.5 If there is an active cab, this one defines the orientation of the train, i.e. the side of the active cab shall be considered as the front of the train. If no cab is active, the train orientation shall be as when a cab was last active.

Clause 3.6.1.6 — The “train orientation relative to LRBG” is defined as the train orientation related to the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.1.6, PDF p.28

3.6.1.6 The “train orientation relative to LRBG” is defined as the train orientation related to the orientation of the LRBG, see Figure 14. It can be either “nominal” or “reverse”.

Clause 3.6.1.6.1 — Note: The train orientation cannot be affected by the direction controller position.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.1.6.1, PDF p.28

3.6.1.6.1 Note: The train orientation cannot be affected by the direction controller position.

Clause 3.6.2 — Location of Data Transmitted to the On-Board Equipment

3.6.2.1 Data Transmitted by Balises

Clause 3.6.2.1.1 — All location and profile data transmitted by a balise shall refer to the location reference

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.2.1.1, PDF p.28

3.6.2.1.1 All location and profile data transmitted by a balise shall refer to the location reference and orientation of the balise group to which the balise belongs.

Clause 3.6.2.2 — Data Transmitted by Radio from RBC

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.2.2, PDF p.28

3.6.2.2 Data Transmitted by Radio from RBC

Clause 3.6.2.2.1 — All location and profile data transmitted from the RBC shall refer to the location

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.2.2.1, PDF p.28

3.6.2.2.1 All location and profile data transmitted from the RBC shall refer to the location reference and orientation of the LRBG given in the same message.

Clause 3.6.2.2.2 — For the LRBG the following requirements have to be met:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.2.2.2, PDF p.28

3.6.2.2.2 For the LRBG the following requirements have to be met: a) The on-board equipment shall use the last balise group passed as a reference when reporting its position to the RBC (in the following termed as LRBG ). Only ONB  balise groups marked as linked and contained in the previously received linking information, if linking information is used on-board or  the last balise group not marked as unlinked, if no linking was used when this balise group was passed shall be regarded. b) The RBC shall use a balise group which was reported by the on-board equipment as a reference (in the following termed as LRBG ). At a certain moment LRBG RBC RBC and LRBG can be different. ONB c) The on-board equipment shall be able to accept information referring to one of at least eight LRBG last reported to the RBC. ONB

Clause 3.6.2.2.2.1 — Exception to a): When on-board position is unknown or when position data has

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.2.2.2.1, PDF p.28

3.6.2.2.2.1 Exception to a): When on-board position is unknown or when position data has been deleted during SoM procedure, the on-board equipment shall use an LRBG identifier set to "unknown" until the onboard has validated its position again by passing a balise group.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Clause 3.6.2.2.2.2 — Exception to b): When the RBC has received from the onboard an unknown position

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.2.2.2.2, PDF p.29

3.6.2.2.2.2 Exception to b): When the RBC has received from the onboard an unknown position (as per 3.6.2.2.2.1) or during SoM procedure an invalid position which it is not able to confirm, the RBC shall use an LRBG identifier set to "unknown" until it receives a position report from the onboard having validated its position by passing a balise group.

Clause 3.6.2.2.2.3 — Regarding c): From the time it has reported an unknown position, or an invalid

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.2.2.2.3, PDF p.29

Clause 3.6.3 — Validity direction of transmitted Information

3.6.3 Validity direction of transmitted Information

Clause 3.6.3.1.1 — The direction for which transmitted information is valid shall refer to:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.3.1.1, PDF p.31

3.6.3.1.1 The direction for which transmitted information is valid shall refer to: a) the direction of the LRBG for information sent by radio b) the direction of the balise group sending the information.

Clause 3.6.3.1.2 — Data transmitted to the on-board equipment (by balise or radio) shall be identified as

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.3.1.2, PDF p.31

3.6.3.1.2 Data transmitted to the on-board equipment (by balise or radio) shall be identified as being valid for a) both directions b) the nominal direction c) the reverse direction of the referenced balise group.

Clause 3.6.3.1.3 — When receiving information from any transmission medium, the ERTMS/ETCS on-

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.3.1.3, PDF p.31

3.6.3.1.3 When receiving information from any transmission medium, the ERTMS/ETCS on- board equipment shall only take into account information valid for its orientation. Other information shall be ignored. Exception: for SL, PS and SH engines, balise group crossing direction shall be considered.

Clause 3.6.3.1.3.1 — If the train position is unknown, data received from any transmission medium valid

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.3.1.3.1, PDF p.31

3.6.3.1.3.1 If the train position is unknown, data received from any transmission medium valid for one direction only (nominal or reverse) shall be rejected by the onboard equipment. Data valid for both directions shall be evaluated (see section 4.8).

Clause 3.6.3.1.4 — If no co-ordinate system has been assigned to a single balise group, data transmitted

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.3.1.4, PDF p.31

3.6.3.1.4 If no co-ordinate system has been assigned to a single balise group, data transmitted by trackside, which refers to that balise group requiring the co-ordinate system to be known (i.e. all data which are only valid for one direction (nominal or reverse)) shall be rejected by the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment. Data valid for both directions shall be evaluated (see section 4.8).

Clause 3.6.3.1.4.1 — Exception: if not rejected due to balise group message consistency check (see

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.3.1.4.1, PDF p.31

3.6.3.1.4.1 Exception: if not rejected due to balise group message consistency check (see

Clause 3.16.2.4.4.1 — and 3.16.2.5.1.1), data to be forwarded to a National System (see section

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.4.4.1, PDF p.31

3.16.2.4.4.1 and 3.16.2.5.1.1), data to be forwarded to a National System (see section 3.15.6) shall be accepted. Justification: the co-ordinate system of the balise group might be known to the National System by other means inherent to the National System itself.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.31

Clause 3.6.3.2 — Location, Continuous Profile Data and Non-continuous Profile Data

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.3.2, PDF p.32

3.6.3.2 Location, Continuous Profile Data and Non-continuous Profile Data

Clause 3.6.3.2.1 — Location and profile data shall have the structure shown in Figure 12 below

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.3.2.1, PDF p.32

3.6.3.2.1 Location and profile data shall have the structure shown in Figure 12 below Direction with regard to location reference continuous value(1) value(2) profile and location data distance (1) distance (2) LRBG value(1) value(2) non continuous length (1) length (2) profile data distance (1) distance (2) LRBG Figure 12: General Structure of location and profile data

Clause 3.6.3.2.2 — With regard to Figure 12 the following applies to continuous profile data:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.3.2.2, PDF p.32

3.6.3.2.2 With regard to Figure 12 the following applies to continuous profile data: a) Value (n) shall be valid for distance (n+1) b) For distance (1) the previously received data shall be used (in case of an SSP this includes train length delay, refer to 3.11.3.1.3). c) Distances shall be given as unsigned incremental values representing the distance between value(n) and value(n-1). d) The last value (n) transmitted shall be valid for an unlimited distance unless value(n) represents a special "end of profile" value.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.32

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP e) If distance (n+1) = 0 then the corresponding profile value n shall still be taken into account.

Clause 3.6.3.2.3 — With regard to Figure 12 the following shall apply to location data:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.3.2.3, PDF p.33

3.6.3.2.3 With regard to Figure 12 the following shall apply to location data: a) Distances shall be given as unsigned incremental values representing the distance between value(n) and value(n-1). b) For distance (1) the previously received data shall be used. c) Each value (n) may represent a single value or a set of data.

Clause 3.6.3.2.4 — According to Figure 12 the structure for non-continuous profile data shall allow to

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.3.2.4, PDF p.33

3.6.3.2.4 According to Figure 12 the structure for non-continuous profile data shall allow to contain multiple elements (value(n) for length(n)) inside the profile. a) Distance to the start of each element (value(n) for length(n)) shall be given as unsigned incremental values, each increment representing the distance between starts of element (n) and element (n-1). b) For distance (1) the previously received data (or initial data/default values, see section 3.7) shall be used. c) Each value (n) may represent a single value or a set of data. d) Note: There is no relationship between length of element (n-1) and distance (n), i.e., elements may overlap.

Clause 3.6.3.2.5 — It shall be possible for the RBC to shift the location reference, e.g., after a change of

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.3.2.5, PDF p.33

3.6.3.2.5 It shall be possible for the RBC to shift the location reference, e.g., after a change of train orientation or running direction.

Clause 3.6.3.2.5.1 — Justification: Refer to Figure 13. To make it possible to shift the location reference if

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.3.2.5.1, PDF p.33

3.6.3.2.5.1 Justification: Refer to Figure 13. To make it possible to shift the location reference if – due to the location of the LRBG and the start location – distance (1) would become a negative value.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.33

Clause 3.6.3.2.6 — With regards to Figure 12 the following applies to linking information

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.3.2.6, PDF p.34

3.6.3.2.6 With regards to Figure 12 the following applies to linking information a) The distance (1) shall be given to the first balise group included in the linking information b) The distance (n) shall be given as the distance between two consecutive balise groups c) Each value (n) shall represent the linking information related to that balise group.

Clause 3.6.4 — Train Position Confidence Interval and Relocation

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.4, PDF p.34

3.6.4 Train Position Confidence Interval and Relocation

Clause 3.6.4.1 — All location related information transmitted from trackside equipment shall be used by

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.4.1, PDF p.34

3.6.4.1 All location related information transmitted from trackside equipment shall be used by the on-board equipment taking into account the confidence interval to the train position, if required for safe operation.

Clause 3.6.4.2 — The confidence interval to the train position shall refer to the distance to the LRBG and

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.4.2, PDF p.34

3.6.4.2 The confidence interval to the train position shall refer to the distance to the LRBG and shall take into account a) On-board over-reading amount and under-reading amount (odometer accuracy plus the error in detection of the balise group location reference) b) The location accuracy of the LRBG.

Clause 3.6.4.2.1 — Distance information received from trackside shall be evaluated on-board as nominal

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.4.2.1, PDF p.34

3.6.4.2.1 Distance information received from trackside shall be evaluated on-board as nominal information (without taking into account any tolerances).

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.34

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.6.4.2.2 — Note: The confidence interval increases in relation to the distance travelled from the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.4.2.2, PDF p.35

3.6.4.2.2 Note: The confidence interval increases in relation to the distance travelled from the LRBG depending on the accuracy of odometer equipment until it is reset when another balise group becomes the LRBG.

Clause 3.6.4.2.3 — The value of the Location Accuracy shall be determined by Linking information if

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.4.2.3, PDF p.35

3.6.4.2.3 The value of the Location Accuracy shall be determined by Linking information if available, if not, by the corresponding National Value, or the corresponding Default Value if the National Value is not applicable.

Clause 3.6.4.3 — When another balise group becomes the LRBG or when evaluating (see section 4.8)

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.4.3, PDF p.35

3.6.4.3 When another balise group becomes the LRBG or when evaluating (see section 4.8) location related trackside information, which is referred to a previously received balise group different from the LRBG, all the location related information shall be relocated by subtracting from the distances that are counted from the reference balise group of the location related information: a) the distance between the reference balise group of the location related information and the LRBG, retrieved from linking information if it is available and it includes both the reference balise group and the LRBG, OR b) in all other cases, the estimated travelled distance between the reference balise group of the location related information and the LRBG.

Clause 3.6.4.3.1 — Justification: it is always the trackside responsibility to provide linking in due course,

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.4.3.1, PDF p.35

3.6.4.3.1 Justification: it is always the trackside responsibility to provide linking in due course, knowing this rule; if the location related information is to be used in situations where linking is not provided (e.g. TSR transmitted by balise group marked as unlinked), the trackside can include provisions, if deemed necessary, when engineering the distance information.

Clause 3.6.4.4 — The train front end position shall be identified in the following way

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.4.4, PDF p.37

3.6.4.4 The train front end position shall be identified in the following way a) The estimated front end position. b) The max(imum) safe front end position, differing from the estimated position by the under-reading amount in the distance measured from the LRBG plus the location accuracy of the LRBG. I.e. in relation to the orientation of the train this position is in advance of the estimated position. c) The min(imum) safe front end position, differing from the estimated position by the over-reading amount in the distance measured from the LRBG plus the location accuracy of the LRBG. I.e. in relation to the orientation of the train this position is in rear of the estimated position.

Clause 3.6.4.4.1 — Note: The rear end position is referenced in the same way. However min safe rear end

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.4.4.1, PDF p.37

3.6.4.4.1 Note: The rear end position is referenced in the same way. However min safe rear end is only safe if sent together with train integrity information.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.4.7.2, PDF p.38

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.5.1.2, PDF p.39

3.6.5.1.2 The position report shall contain at least the following position and direction data a) The distance between the LRBG and the estimated front end of the train. b) The distance from the estimated front end position to the min safe front end position and the distance from the estimated front end position to the max safe front end position. c) The identity of the location reference, the LRBG. d) The orientation of the train in relation to the LRBG orientation. Note: Driver selected running direction is only handled by the on-board system. e) The position of the front end of the train in relation to the LRBG (nominal or reverse side of the LRBG). f) The estimated speed g) Train integrity information. h) Direction of train movement in relation to the LRBG orientation i) Optionally, the previous LRBG (see 3.4.2.3.3).

3.6.5.1.4 The on-board equipment shall send position reports as requested by the RBC in the position report parameters. In addition, it shall also send a position report if at least one of the events listed hereafter occurs: a) The train reaches standstill, if applicable to the current mode. b) The mode changes. c) The driver confirms train integrity. d) A loss of train integrity is detected. e) The train passes a RBC/RBC border with its min safe rear end. f) The train passes with its min safe rear end a level transition border which led to a transition from level 2/3 to level 0, NTC or 1. g) The level changes.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.5.1.5, PDF p.41

3.6.5.1.5 For the position report parameters requested by the RBC the following possibilities shall be available, individually or in combination a) Periodically in time. b) Periodically in space. c) When the max safe front end or min safe rear end of the train has passed a specified location. d) At every passage of an LRBG compliant balise group (see 3.6.2.2.2). e) Immediately.

3.6.5.1.7 The given position report parameters shall be valid until new parameters are given from the RBC.

Clause 3.6.6 — Geographical position reporting

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.6, PDF p.42

3.6.6 Geographical position reporting

Clause 3.6.6.1 — The ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall display, only on driver request, the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.6.1, PDF p.42

3.6.6.1 The ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall display, only on driver request, the geographical position of the estimated front end of the train in relation to the track kilometre. The display of the geographical position shall also be stopped on driver request.

Clause 3.6.6.2 — The resolution of the position indication shall be 1 metre (sufficient to allow the driver

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.6.2, PDF p.42

3.6.6.2 The resolution of the position indication shall be 1 metre (sufficient to allow the driver to report the train position when communicating with the signalman).

Clause 3.6.6.3 — When receiving new geographical position information (from radio or from balise

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.6.3, PDF p.42

3.6.6.3 When receiving new geographical position information (from radio or from balise group), the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall replace the currently stored

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.42

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP geographical position information (if any) by this new received one, continuing the ongoing geographical position calculation until at least one of the condition of 3.6.6.9 applies.

Clause 3.6.6.4 — Geographical position information shall always use a balise group as geographical

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.6.4, PDF p.43

3.6.6.4 Geographical position information shall always use a balise group as geographical position reference balise group and if needed an offset from that balise group. A geographical position reference balise group shall be either: a) part of the last reported balise groups memorised on-board, in case the information is transmitted by radio, OR b) the balise group transmitting the information, in case the information is transmitted by balise group, OR c) any balise group not yet passed at the time of reception of the information.

Clause 3.6.6.4.1 — In case the information is received by radio and at least one of the announced

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.6.4.1, PDF p.43

3.6.6.4.1 In case the information is received by radio and at least one of the announced geographical position reference balise group(s) is part of the last reported balise groups memorised on-board, the on-board equipment shall use the data related to the most recently reported balise group.

Clause 3.6.6.4.2 — From the currently stored geographical position information, the track kilometre

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.6.4.2, PDF p.43

3.6.6.4.2 From the currently stored geographical position information, the track kilometre reference given for a geographical reference location shall become applicable if the train has detected the related geographical reference balise group and has travelled the offset distance from this reference balise group.

Clause 3.6.6.4.3 — The announced and not applicable geographical references shall be deleted on-board

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.6.4.3, PDF p.43

3.6.6.4.3 The announced and not applicable geographical references shall be deleted on-board if the train changes orientation.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.6.5, PDF p.43

3.6.6.5 The distance travelled from the geographical reference location shall be taken into account when calculating the geographical position.

Clause 3.6.6.6 — In cases where the track kilometre is not incremental (jumps, changes in counting

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.6.6, PDF p.43

3.6.6.6 In cases where the track kilometre is not incremental (jumps, changes in counting direction, scaling error) the reported position might be wrong between the point of irregularity and the next new reference.

Clause 3.6.6.7 — In cases where single balise groups are used as a reference for geographical position

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.6.7, PDF p.43

3.6.6.7 In cases where single balise groups are used as a reference for geographical position information and where no linking information is available (and therefore no orientation can be assigned to the balise group), the on-board equipment shall ignore the geographical position information related to these single balise groups.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.7.1.1, PDF p.44

3.7.1.1 To control the train movement in an ERTMS/ETCS based system the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall be given information from the trackside system both concerning the route set for the train and the track description for that route. The following information shall be given from the trackside a) Permission and distance to run, the Movement Authority (MA) (see section 3.8) b) When needed, limitations related to the movement authority, i.e. Mode profile for On Sight, Limited Supervision or Shunting and signalling related speed restriction (see

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Mode profile and Signalling related Speed restriction shall always be sent together with the MA to which the information belongs c) Track description covering as a minimum the whole distance defined by the MA. Track description includes the following information  The Static Speed Profile (SSP) (see section 3.11.3).  The gradient profile (see section 3.11.12).  Optionally Axle load Speed Profile (ASP) (see section 3.11.4)  Optionally Speed restriction to ensure a given permitted braking distance (see section 3.11.11)  Optionally track conditions (see section 3.12.1).  Optionally route suitability data (see section 3.12.2).  Optionally areas where reversing is permitted (see section 3.15.4).  Optionally changed adhesion factor (see section 3.18.4.5.5). d) Linking information when available.

Clause 3.7.3 — Extension, replacement of track description and linking information

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.7.3, PDF p.46

3.7.3 Extension, replacement of track description and linking information

Clause 3.7.3.1 — New track description and linking information shall replace (in the ERTMS/ETCS on-

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.7.3.1, PDF p.46

3.7.3.1 New track description and linking information shall replace (in the ERTMS/ETCS on- board equipment) stored information as detailed below: a) New Static Speed Profile information shall replace all stored Static Speed Profile information from the start location of the new information. b) New Gradient Profile information shall replace all stored Gradient Profile information from the start location of the new information. c) New Axle Load Speed Profile information shall replace all stored Axle Load Speed Profile information from the start location of the first element of the new information. d) New Speed Restriction to ensure Permitted Braking Distance information shall replace all stored Speed Restriction to ensure Permitted Braking Distance information from the start location of the first element of the new information. e) New track condition Change of Traction System information shall replace all stored Change of Traction System information. f) New track condition Big Metal Masses information shall replace all stored Big Metal Masses information from the start location of the first element of the new information. g) New track condition information of at least one of the types listed here, i.e., sound horn, non stopping area, tunnel stopping area, powerless section - lower pantograph, powerless section - switch off the main power switch, radio hole, air tightness, switch off regenerative brake, switch off eddy current brake for service brake, switch off eddy current brake for emergency brake, switch off magnetic shoe brake, shall replace all stored track condition information of the listed types from the start location of the first element of the new information. h) New route suitability loading gauge information shall replace all stored route suitability loading gauge information. i) New route suitability traction system information shall replace all stored route suitability traction system information. j) New route suitability axle load information shall replace all stored route suitability axle load information. k) New reversing area information shall replace all stored reversing area information. l) New adhesion factor information shall replace all stored adhesion factor information from the start location of the new information.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.46

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP m) New linking information received as non-infill information shall replace all stored linking information from the LRBG. n) New linking information received as infill information shall replace all stored linking information from the reference location of the infill information (i.e. the balise group at next main signal). o) New track condition Station Platform information shall replace all stored Station Platform information from the start location of the first element of the new information. p) New track condition Allowed Current Consumption information shall replace all stored Allowed Current Consumption information.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.47

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.8.1 — Characteristics of a MA

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.1, PDF p.48

3.8.1 Characteristics of a MA

Clause 3.8.1.1 — The following characteristics can be used in a Movement Authority (see Figure 17:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.1.1, PDF p.48

3.8.1.1 The following characteristics can be used in a Movement Authority (see Figure 17: Structure of an MA): a) The End of Movement Authority is the location to which the train is authorised to move. b) When the Target Speed at the End of Movement Authority is zero, the End of Movement Authority is called EOA (End of Authority); when the target speed is not zero, it is called the LOA (Limit of Authority). This non zero target speed can be time limited. c) If no overlap exists, the Danger Point is a location beyond the End of Movement Authority that can be reached by the front end of the train without a risk for a hazardous situation. d) The end of an overlap (if used in the existing interlocking system) is a location beyond the Danger Point that can be reached by the front end of the train without a risk for a hazardous situation. This additional distance is only valid for a defined time. e) A release speed is a speed limit under which the train is allowed to run in the vicinity of the End of Movement Authority, when the target speed is zero. One release speed can be associated with the Danger Point, and another one with the overlap. Release speed can also be calculated on-board the train (see section 3.13.9.4). f) The MA can be split into several sections, The last one is called End Section.  A first time-out value can be attached to each section. This value will be used for the revocation of the associated route when the train has not entered into it yet. It is called the Section time-out.  In addition, a second time-out value can be attached to the End Section of the MA. This second time-out will be used for the revocation of the last section when it is occupied by the train; it is called the End Section time-out.

Clause 3.8.1.2 — The values of the time-outs possibly given in an MA shall take into account the time

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.1.2, PDF p.48

3.8.1.2 The values of the time-outs possibly given in an MA shall take into account the time elapsed from the start of validity of information to the sending of the message.

Clause 3.8.1.3 — Note: A Danger Point can be (not exhaustive list):

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.1.3, PDF p.48

3.8.1.3 Note: A Danger Point can be (not exhaustive list):  the entry point of an occupied block section (if the line is operated according to fixed block principles)  the position of the safe rear end of a train (if the line is operated according to moving block principles)

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.48

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP  the fouling point of a switch, positioned for a route, conflicting with the current direction of movement of the train (both for fixed and moving block mode of operation)

Clause 3.8.1.4 — Note: Traditionally the overlap is a piece of track (beyond the danger point), that is put

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.1.4, PDF p.49

3.8.1.4 Note: Traditionally the overlap is a piece of track (beyond the danger point), that is put at disposal of a train, to guarantee a non hazardous situation, also in case the driver should misjudge the stopping distance for the train. In ERTMS/ETCS the overlap can be used to improve the efficiency of the braking supervision.

Clause 3.8.1.5 — Note: Time-out values can be given in the MA to cope with the following situations

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.1.5, PDF p.49

3.8.1.5 Note: Time-out values can be given in the MA to cope with the following situations depending on the interlocking operations, i.e. the timers on-board will only reflect the situation trackside and when expired (on-board) the actions taken are restrictive: a) Section time-out or time-out for the speed at the LOA: When a signalman requests a route release of a part of a route not yet entered by the approaching train. b) End Section time-out: When the train has entered the last part of a route, the automatic route release can be delayed to make sure that the train has come to a standstill before any switches inside the route can be moved. c) Time-out for an overlap: When the train has entered the last part of a route, the overlap associated with the route remains valid for a certain time to make sure that the train has successfully stopped before its End of Movement Authority. If the overlap is still unoccupied when the timer expires the interlocking revokes the overlap.

Clause 3.8.1.6 — Note: If the trackside equipment does not have enough information to give the distance

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.1.6, PDF p.49

3.8.1.6 Note: If the trackside equipment does not have enough information to give the distance to the End of Movement Authority with a target speed equal to zero, a target speed higher than zero can be given (LOA, Limit of Authority). It is the responsibility of the trackside to ensure that the safe distance beyond the LOA is long enough to brake the train from the target speed to a stand still without any hazardous situation. It is the responsibility of the on-board equipment to apply the brakes if no new information is received when the Limit of Authority is passed.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.51

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP the real position of the train. The route release and revocation of routes can not be carried out by the interlocking until permitted by the RBC.

Clause 3.8.3.6 — When an MA is transmitted by a balise group, the length of the first section shall refer

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.3.6, PDF p.52

3.8.3.6 When an MA is transmitted by a balise group, the length of the first section shall refer to the balise co-ordinate system of that balise group.

Clause 3.8.3.7 — In case a main signal is at danger in level 1, the first section shall give the distance

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.3.7, PDF p.52

3.8.3.7 In case a main signal is at danger in level 1, the first section shall give the distance from the balise group at the main signal to the location of the main signal, i.e. the distance to EOA is given. Where available, information concerning danger point and overlap for this EOA may also be given.

Clause 3.8.3.7.1 — Justification: The balise group is not necessarily placed at the same location as the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.3.7.1, PDF p.52

3.8.3.7.1 Justification: The balise group is not necessarily placed at the same location as the signal and thus an infill message (which includes the same information as the balise group at the main signal) could change the location of the EOA to a position closer to the train.

Clause 3.8.3.8 — Note: In case the main signal is at danger in level 1, the on-board will supervise the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.3.8, PDF p.52

3.8.3.8 Note: In case the main signal is at danger in level 1, the on-board will supervise the given distance (specified in section 3.8.3.7) as the distance to EOA.

Clause 3.8.3.9 — When an MA is transmitted by radio from the RBC, the length of the first section shall

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.3.9, PDF p.52

3.8.3.9 When an MA is transmitted by radio from the RBC, the length of the first section shall refer to the balise co-ordinate system of the LRBG given in the same message.

Clause 3.8.3.10 — It shall be possible to give the length of a section to any location in the track.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.3.10, PDF p.52

3.8.3.10 It shall be possible to give the length of a section to any location in the track.

Clause 3.8.3.10.1 — Note: A section can cover several blocks and is not restricted to block ends (see

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.3.10.1, PDF p.52

3.8.3.10.1 Note: A section can cover several blocks and is not restricted to block ends (see figures). [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] Section(1) [ ] EOA/LOA: Optional signal Figure 18: Distance to End of Movement Authority when no time-outs are needed

Clause 3.8.3.11 — In moving block operation the MA shall never exceed the min safe rear end of the
