3.8.3.11 In moving block operation the MA shall never exceed the min safe rear end of the preceding train. B A Safe rear end of train A EOA Figure 20: MA in moving block operation.

Clause 3.8.4 — Use of the MA on board the train

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4, PDF p.53

3.8.4 Use of the MA on board the train

Clause 3.8.4.1 — End Section Time-Out

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.1, PDF p.53

3.8.4.1 End Section Time-Out

Clause 3.8.4.1.1 — The End Section timer shall be started on-board when the train passes the End

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.1.1, PDF p.53

3.8.4.1.1 The End Section timer shall be started on-board when the train passes the End Section timer start location given by trackside with its max safe front end.

Clause 3.8.4.1.2 — When the End Section timer value becomes greater than the time-out value given by

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.1.2, PDF p.53

3.8.4.1.2 When the End Section timer value becomes greater than the time-out value given by trackside, the following shall apply: a) The EOA/LOA shall be withdrawn to the current position of the train. Refer to appendix A.3.4 for the exhaustive list of location based information stored on-board, which shall be deleted accordingly; b) if any, a non zero target speed value at the End of Movement Authority shall be set to zero (i.e. an LOA at the End of Movement Authority becomes an EOA withdrawn to the current position of the train).

Clause 3.8.4.1.3 — In case no End Section timer is running when the on-board receives a new MA with an

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.1.3, PDF p.53

3.8.4.1.3 In case no End Section timer is running when the on-board receives a new MA with an End Section timer start location in rear of the max safe front end of the train, the on- board shall consider that the End Section timer value became greater than its time-out value and apply 3.8.4.1.2.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.53

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.8.4.1.3.1 — Justification: in this case the train is already beyond the timer start location,

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.1.3.1, PDF p.54

3.8.4.1.3.1 Justification: in this case the train is already beyond the timer start location, therefore it is impossible to determine when that location was crossed and so what share of the time-out value has elapsed since the crossing event. The safe assumption is to consider that the time-out value has been exceeded and therefore that the End Section will soon be released by the Interlocking.

Clause 3.8.4.1.4 — In case an End Section timer is already running when the on-board receives a new MA

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.1.4, PDF p.54

3.8.4.1.4 In case an End Section timer is already running when the on-board receives a new MA with an End Section timer start location in rear of the max safe front end of the train, the on-board shall keep the End Section timer running but replace the time-out value with the value received with the new MA.

Clause 3.8.4.1.4.1 — Justification: this allows repetition of the MA via RBC (e.g. acknowledgment of

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.1.4.1, PDF p.54

3.8.4.1.4.1 Justification: this allows repetition of the MA via RBC (e.g. acknowledgment of current MA was lost) without unintentionally affecting the End Section timer.

Clause 3.8.4.2 — Section Time-Outs

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.2, PDF p.54

3.8.4.2 Section Time-Outs

Clause 3.8.4.2.1 — The on-board shall start a Section timer for each section:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.2.1, PDF p.54

3.8.4.2.1 The on-board shall start a Section timer for each section: a) For Level 2: at the value of the time stamp of the message including the MA. b) For Level 1: at the time of passage over the first encountered balise of the balise group giving the MA.

Clause 3.8.4.2.1.1 — Justification for b): This is to ensure that the timer is always started before or at the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.2.1.1, PDF p.54

3.8.4.2.1.1 Justification for b): This is to ensure that the timer is always started before or at the same time as the related variable information is received. Thus the timer start is independent of in which balise the variable information is given.

Clause 3.8.4.2.2 — When a Section timer value becomes greater than the time-out value given by

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.2.2, PDF p.54

3.8.4.2.2 When a Section timer value becomes greater than the time-out value given by trackside, the following shall apply: a) the EOA/LOA and the SvL shall be withdrawn to the entry point of the revoked section. Refer to appendix A.3.4 for the exhaustive list of location based information stored on-board, which shall be deleted accordingly; b) the National/ Default Value of the Release Speed shall apply; c) if any, a non zero target speed value at the End of Movement Authority shall be set to zero (i.e. an LOA at the End of Movement Authority becomes an EOA withdrawn to the entry point of the revoked section).

Clause 3.8.4.2.2.1 — Intentionally deleted.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.2.2.1, PDF p.54

3.8.4.2.2.1 Intentionally deleted.

Clause 3.8.4.2.3 — The Section timer shall be stopped when the min safe front end of the train has passed

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.2.3, PDF p.54

3.8.4.2.3 The Section timer shall be stopped when the min safe front end of the train has passed the associated Section timer stop location.

Clause 3.8.4.3 — Time-out of the speed associated with the End of Movement Authority (LOA

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.3, PDF p.54

3.8.4.3 Time-out of the speed associated with the End of Movement Authority (LOA speed time out)

Clause 3.8.4.3.1 — The on-board shall start a timer for the LOA speed:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.3.1, PDF p.54

3.8.4.3.1 The on-board shall start a timer for the LOA speed: a) For Level 2: at the value of the time stamp of the message including the MA.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.54

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP b) For Level 1: at the time of passage over the first encountered balise of the balise group giving the MA.

Clause 3.8.4.3.1.1 — Justification for b): This is to ensure that the timer is always started before or at the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.3.1.1, PDF p.55

3.8.4.3.1.1 Justification for b): This is to ensure that the timer is always started before or at the same time as the related variable information is received. Thus the timer start is independent of in which balise the variable information is given.

Clause 3.8.4.3.2 — When the LOA speed timer value becomes greater than the time-out value given by

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.3.2, PDF p.55

3.8.4.3.2 When the LOA speed timer value becomes greater than the time-out value given by trackside, the speed associated with the LOA shall be set to zero (i.e. the Limit of Authority becomes an End of Authority and the SvL is defined on-board according to 3.8.4.5). Refer to appendix A.3.4 for the exhaustive list of location based information stored on-board, which shall be deleted accordingly.

Clause 3.8.4.4 — Time-out of Overlap

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.4, PDF p.55

3.8.4.4 Time-out of Overlap

Clause 3.8.4.4.1 — The Overlap timer shall be started on-board when the train passes the Overlap timer

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.4.1, PDF p.55

3.8.4.4.1 The Overlap timer shall be started on-board when the train passes the Overlap timer start location given by trackside with its max safe front end. The timer shall be considered as started even if a time-out value “infinite” is given.

Clause 3.8.4.4.2 — When the Overlap timer value becomes greater than the time-out value given by

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.4.2, PDF p.55

3.8.4.4.2 When the Overlap timer value becomes greater than the time-out value given by trackside, the following shall apply: a) the overlap information shall be deleted and the Supervised Location shall be determined in accordance with 3.8.4.5. Refer to appendix A.3.4 for the exhaustive list of location based information stored on-board, which shall be deleted accordingly. b) the release speed associated with the Overlap shall be deleted c) if any, a non zero target speed value at the End of Movement Authority shall be set to zero (i.e. an LOA at the End of Movement Authority becomes an EOA).

Clause 3.8.4.4.3 — If the train comes to a standstill after the Overlap timer has been started, the on-board

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.4.3, PDF p.55

3.8.4.4.3 If the train comes to a standstill after the Overlap timer has been started, the on-board shall consider that the Overlap timer value became greater than its time-out value and shall apply 3.8.4.4.2.

Clause 3.8.4.4.4 — In case no Overlap timer is running when the on-board receives a new MA with an

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.4.4, PDF p.55

3.8.4.4.4 In case no Overlap timer is running when the on-board receives a new MA with an Overlap timer start location in rear of the max safe front end of the train, the on-board shall consider that the Overlap timer value became greater than its time-out value and shall apply 3.8.4.4.2.

Clause 3.8.4.4.4.1 — Justification: in this case the train is already beyond the timer start location,

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.4.4.1, PDF p.55

3.8.4.4.4.1 Justification: in this case the train is already beyond the timer start location, therefore it is impossible to determine when that location was crossed and so what share of the time-out value has elapsed since the crossing event. The safe assumption is to consider that the time-out value has been exceeded and therefore that the Overlap will soon be released by the Interlocking.

Clause 3.8.4.4.5 — In case an Overlap timer is already running when the on-board receives a new MA with

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.4.5, PDF p.55

3.8.4.4.5 In case an Overlap timer is already running when the on-board receives a new MA with an Overlap timer start location in rear of the max safe front end of the train, the on-

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.55

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP board shall keep the Overlap timer running but replace the Overlap time-out value with the value received with the new MA.

Clause 3.8.4.4.5.1 — Justification: this allows repetition of the MA via RBC (e.g. acknowledgment of

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.4.5.1, PDF p.56

3.8.4.4.5.1 Justification: this allows repetition of the MA via RBC (e.g. acknowledgment of current MA was lost) without unintentionally affecting the Overlap timer.

Clause 3.8.4.5 — Supervised Location

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.5, PDF p.56

3.8.4.5 Supervised Location

Clause 3.8.4.5.1 — The Supervised Location (SvL) shall be defined on-board as:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.5.1, PDF p.56

3.8.4.5.1 The Supervised Location (SvL) shall be defined on-board as: a) the end of overlap (if any and before time-out). b) if not, the Danger Point (if any). c) if not, the End Of Authority.

Clause 3.8.4.5.2 — As long as a Limit of Authority is supervised, no SvL shall be defined on-board.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.5.2, PDF p.56

3.8.4.5.2 As long as a Limit of Authority is supervised, no SvL shall be defined on-board.

Clause 3.8.4.6 — Infill MA (level 1 only)

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.6, PDF p.56

3.8.4.6 Infill MA (level 1 only)

Clause 3.8.4.6.1 — An MA given by an infill device is called an infill MA.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.6.1, PDF p.56

3.8.4.6.1 An MA given by an infill device is called an infill MA.

Clause 3.8.4.6.2 — An infill MA shall be evaluated on-board only if the on-board equipment is in FS or LS

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.6.2, PDF p.56

3.8.4.6.2 An infill MA shall be evaluated on-board only if the on-board equipment is in FS or LS mode.

Clause 3.8.4.6.3 — The infill information shall include the identity of the balise group at the next main

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.6.3, PDF p.56

3.8.4.6.3 The infill information shall include the identity of the balise group at the next main signal i.e. the identity of the balise group giving the information that is transmitted in advance by the infill device.

Clause 3.8.4.6.4 — An infill MA shall be evaluated on-board only if the linking information, regarding the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.6.4, PDF p.56

3.8.4.6.4 An infill MA shall be evaluated on-board only if the linking information, regarding the main signal balise group to which it refers, is available.

Clause 3.8.4.6.5 — The on-board shall start a Section timer for each section beyond the next main signal:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.4.6.5, PDF p.56

3.8.4.6.5 The on-board shall start a Section timer for each section beyond the next main signal: a) When the infill information is received from a balise group at the time of passing the first encountered balise of the infill balise group. b) When the infill information is received from a loop at the time of receiving the loop message. c) When the infill information is received from a radio infill unit at the value of the time stamp of the radio infill message including the MA.

Clause 3.8.5.1 — A new MA shall replace a previously received MA in the following ways:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.5.1, PDF p.56

3.8.5.1 A new MA shall replace a previously received MA in the following ways: a) When the new MA is given from a balise group at a main signal (i.e. not infill information) or from the RBC all data included in the previous MA shall be replaced by the new data. b) When the new MA is given as infill information all data beyond the announced balise group at the next main signal shall be replaced.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.56

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.8.5.1.1 — Note: This refers to all information included in the MA as listed in section 3.8.1.1 and

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.5.1.1, PDF p.57

3.8.5.1.1 Note: This refers to all information included in the MA as listed in section 3.8.1.1 and the Signalling related speed restriction (see section 3.11.6).

Clause 3.8.5.1.2 — When an infill MA is received, the on-board shall start a new MA section at the infill

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.5.1.2, PDF p.57

3.8.5.1.2 When an infill MA is received, the on-board shall start a new MA section at the infill location reference, i.e. the balise group at the next main signal (see 3.6.2.3.1).

Clause 3.8.5.1.3 — If the SvL defined from the new MA is closer than the one supervised with the former

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.5.1.3, PDF p.57

3.8.5.1.3 If the SvL defined from the new MA is closer than the one supervised with the former MA, this shall be considered by the on-board equipment as an MA shortening. Refer to appendix A.3.4 for the exhaustive list of location based information stored on-board, which shall be deleted accordingly.

Clause 3.8.5.1.4 — If a new MA defines an SvL while the on-board was supervising an LOA, this shall

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.5.1.4, PDF p.57

3.8.5.1.4 If a new MA defines an SvL while the on-board was supervising an LOA, this shall always be considered by the on-board equipment as an MA shortening regardless of the SvL location. Refer to appendix A.3.4 for the exhaustive list of location based information stored on-board, which shall be deleted accordingly.

Clause 3.8.5.2 — It shall be possible to update the length of an MA section by means of repositioning

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.5.2, PDF p.57

3.8.5.2 It shall be possible to update the length of an MA section by means of repositioning information contained in a balise group message (see section 3.8.5.3).

Clause 3.8.5.2.1 — Note: The concerned MA section need not be the end section.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.5.2.1, PDF p.57

3.8.5.2.1 Note: The concerned MA section need not be the end section.

Clause 3.8.5.2.2 — Upon reception of repositioning information and only if linking information has

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.5.2.2, PDF p.57

3.8.5.2.2 Upon reception of repositioning information and only if linking information has announced a following balise group as unknown but containing repositioning information, the on-board shall update the length of the current MA section in which the train front end is.

Clause 3.8.5.2.3 — A balise group message containing a movement authority shall not contain

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.5.2.3, PDF p.57

3.8.5.2.3 A balise group message containing a movement authority shall not contain repositioning information for the same direction.

Clause 3.8.5.2.3.1 — Note: It is possible to combine repositioning with an infill MA.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.5.2.3.1, PDF p.57

3.8.5.2.3.1 Note: It is possible to combine repositioning with an infill MA.

Clause 3.8.5.2.4 — The reception of repositioning information or of a new MA with an LOA shall not be

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.5.2.4, PDF p.57

3.8.5.2.4 The reception of repositioning information or of a new MA with an LOA shall not be considered as an MA shortening by the on-board equipment.

Clause 3.8.5.3.5.3 — In balise group A the following information is given:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.5.3.5.3, PDF p.62

3.8.5.3.5.3 In balise group A the following information is given: a) The most restrictive track description from all routes (which could be a combination from the routes); b) The linking distance given to the farthest balise group containing repositioning information, the identification of the repositioning balise group is not known; c) For a given aspect of signal A, the most restrictive MA from all routes (the shortest sections from the routes and the lowest target speed at the End of Movement Authority); d) If some sections are time limited, the most restrictive timer.

Clause 3.8.6 — Co-operative shortening of MA (Level 2 and 3 only)

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.6, PDF p.63

3.8.6 Co-operative shortening of MA (Level 2 and 3 only)

Clause 3.8.6.1 — It shall be possible to shorten a given MA using a special procedure between on-board

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.8.6.1, PDF p.63

3.8.6.1 It shall be possible to shorten a given MA using a special procedure between on-board equipment and RBC. The procedure is as follows: a) The RBC proposes a new MA with an EOA closer to the train than the current EOA/LOA, optionally with a mode profile.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.63

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP b) The ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall check the train front end position versus the Indication supervision limit of the proposed shortened MA.  If it is in rear, the on-board equipment shall accept the new MA.  If it is in advance, the request shall be rejected and the previously received MA remains valid c) The RBC shall be informed about the decision.

Clause 3.9 — Means to transmit Infill information (Level 1 only)

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9, PDF p.64

3.9 Means to transmit Infill information (Level 1 only)

Clause 3.9.1.1 — It shall be possible to transmit infill information to the on-board equipment using

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.1.1, PDF p.64

3.9.1.1 It shall be possible to transmit infill information to the on-board equipment using a) Balise groups b) Euroloops c) Radio infill units.

Clause 3.9.1.1.1 — If the information transmitted by Balise groups, Euroloops, and Radio includes infill

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.1.1.1, PDF p.64

3.9.1.1.1 If the information transmitted by Balise groups, Euroloops, and Radio includes infill information, those devices are also identified as infill devices.

Clause 3.9.1.2 — The principle used for the infill information shall be the same independent of

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.1.2, PDF p.64

3.9.1.2 The principle used for the infill information shall be the same independent of transmission media.

Clause 3.9.1.3 — If the on-board system is not equipped with the infill transmission media as requested

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.1.3, PDF p.64

3.9.1.3 If the on-board system is not equipped with the infill transmission media as requested by the announcement balise group, the announcement information shall be ignored by the on-board equipment and the train shall proceed according to the previously received information.

Clause 3.9.1.4 — Note: No additional description is needed for infill by balise group (other than already

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.1.4, PDF p.64

3.9.1.4 Note: No additional description is needed for infill by balise group (other than already covered in previous chapters).

Clause 3.9.2.1 — An End Of Loop Marker (EOLM) is by definition a device to mark the beginning or the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.2.1, PDF p.64

3.9.2.1 An End Of Loop Marker (EOLM) is by definition a device to mark the beginning or the end of a loop. When receiving this information, the on-board equipment knows that it is entering/leaving a track equipped with a loop. In unidirectional applications it is possible to have an EOLM only at the entry side of a loop.

Clause 3.9.2.2 — Balise groups shall be used as EOLMs. They act as an EOLM by sending the EOLM

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.2.2, PDF p.64

3.9.2.2 Balise groups shall be used as EOLMs. They act as an EOLM by sending the EOLM information to the passing train.

Clause 3.9.2.3 — EOLMs have an orientation that is identical to the balise group orientation. The general

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.2.3, PDF p.64

3.9.2.3 EOLMs have an orientation that is identical to the balise group orientation. The general rules for balise orientation therefore also apply to EOLMs.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.64

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.9.2.4 — EOLM information might be contained in a balise group that contains other information.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.2.4, PDF p.65

3.9.2.4 EOLM information might be contained in a balise group that contains other information.

Clause 3.9.2.5 — The EOLM shall send the identity of the announced loop.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.2.5, PDF p.65

3.9.2.5 The EOLM shall send the identity of the announced loop.

Clause 3.9.2.6 — The on-board shall only accept information coming from the loop with the announced

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.2.6, PDF p.65

3.9.2.6 The on-board shall only accept information coming from the loop with the announced identity.

Clause 3.9.2.9 — The following information shall be sent in advance by the EOLM to prepare for

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.2.9, PDF p.65

3.9.2.9 The following information shall be sent in advance by the EOLM to prepare for reception of the loop information:  Loop identity used to identify the loop.  Key to select the spread spectrum key necessary to receive the loop telegrams.  Distance to the loop giving the distance from the EOLM to the location from where on loop messages can be received.  Length of the loop giving the length of the loop over which messages can be received.  Indicator telling the on-board whether the orientation of the loop is identical or reverse to the orientation of the announcing EOLM.

Clause 3.9.2.10 — The on-board shall be prepared to receive messages from the Euroloop after passing

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.2.10, PDF p.65

3.9.2.10 The on-board shall be prepared to receive messages from the Euroloop after passing the EOLM.

Clause 3.9.2.11 — When the on-board equipment reads the next main signal balise group or when it

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.2.11, PDF p.65

3.9.2.11 When the on-board equipment reads the next main signal balise group or when it detects that the next main signal balise group was missed, new infill information possibly received from the loop shall be ignored.

Clause 3.9.2.12 — The distances given in an EOLM (distance to loop, length of loop) are used for

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.2.12, PDF p.65

3.9.2.12 The distances given in an EOLM (distance to loop, length of loop) are used for diagnostic purpose only. They shall therefore not be used to restrict reception of loop telegrams to specific locations.

Clause 3.9.3.1 — In level 1 areas it shall be possible to send to the on-board equipment orders to

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.3.1, PDF p.65

3.9.3.1 In level 1 areas it shall be possible to send to the on-board equipment orders to establish/terminate a communication session with a radio infill unit.

Clause 3.9.3.2 — The orders shall be sent via balise groups or via Radio Infill units.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.3.2, PDF p.65

3.9.3.2 The orders shall be sent via balise groups or via Radio Infill units.

Clause 3.9.3.3 — The order to establish a communication session shall be ignored:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.3.3, PDF p.65

3.9.3.3 The order to establish a communication session shall be ignored: a) Intentionally deleted. b) If the on-board equipment does not include radio.

Clause 3.9.3.4 — If the on-board equipment includes radio, the communication session shall be

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.3.4, PDF p.65

3.9.3.4 If the on-board equipment includes radio, the communication session shall be established using the same protocols and interfaces as for Level 2/3 operations.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.65

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.9.3.5 — If the order to establish a communication session with a radio infill unit sent via balise

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.3.5, PDF p.66

3.9.3.5 If the order to establish a communication session with a radio infill unit sent via balise groups is received, the on-board equipment shall, once the location indicated in the order is reached: a) terminate any existing communication session(s) with RIU(s) not indicated in the order b) as soon as a new communication session can be handled, establish a communication session with the RIU indicated in the order

Clause 3.9.3.5.2 — Intentionally deleted.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.3.5.2, PDF p.66

3.9.3.5.2 Intentionally deleted.

Clause 3.9.3.6 — If the order to establish a communication session with a radio infill unit sent via Radio

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.3.6, PDF p.66

3.9.3.6 If the order to establish a communication session with a radio infill unit sent via Radio Infill unit is received, the on-board equipment shall: a) If only one RIU communication session is ongoing, maintain the existing communication session and establish a new one with the RIU indicated in the order. b) If two RIU communication sessions are ongoing, maintain the communication session related to the current infill area, terminate the other one and establish a new one with the RIU indicated in the order.

Clause 3.9.3.6.1 — Exception (degraded situation): if the on-board can handle only one communication

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.3.6.1, PDF p.66

3.9.3.6.1 Exception (degraded situation): if the on-board can handle only one communication session, the order shall be ignored.

Clause 3.9.3.7 — A Radio Infill Unit shall not initiate a communication session with an on-board

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.3.7, PDF p.66

3.9.3.7 A Radio Infill Unit shall not initiate a communication session with an on-board equipment.

Clause 3.9.3.8 — The order to establish/terminate a communication session sent via balise groups shall

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.3.8, PDF p.66

3.9.3.8 The order to establish/terminate a communication session sent via balise groups shall be sent together with the following radio infill area information: a) Location where to perform the action (referred to the balise group containing the order).Note: if the action is to establish a communication session, this location marks the beginning of the Radio Infill Area. b) Next main signal balise group identifier (ignored by the on-board if the action is Terminate communication session).Note: the reference location of this balise group marks the end of the Radio Infill Area.

Clause 3.9.3.8.1 — The order to establish/terminate a communication session sent via Radio Infill units

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.3.8.1, PDF p.66

3.9.3.8.1 The order to establish/terminate a communication session sent via Radio Infill units (see 3.5.3.6) shall not be sent together with any radio infill area information.

Clause 3.9.3.9 — The establishment of a communication session for radio infill shall not change the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.3.9, PDF p.66

3.9.3.9 The establishment of a communication session for radio infill shall not change the operational level of the on-board i.e. the information in the balise group shall be taken into account as usual in level 1.

Clause 3.9.3.10 — The on-board equipment shall inform the radio infill unit

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.3.10, PDF p.66

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.66

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP a) As soon as the location indicated in the order sent via balise groups is passed (i.e. entry of the train in the infill area) b) As soon as the next main signal balise group indicated in the order sent via balise groups is read or the on-board equipment detects that it was missed.

Clause 3.9.3.11 — The information sent to the radio infill unit by the on-board equipment shall include

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.3.11, PDF p.67

3.9.3.11 The information sent to the radio infill unit by the on-board equipment shall include a) Train identity (ETCS-ID of the on-board equipment) b) Position report c) Identifier of the next main signal balise group d) Time stamp

Clause 3.9.3.11.1 — Justification:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.3.11.1, PDF p.67

3.9.3.11.1 Justification: a) The train identity is used for conformity with other train to track messages b) The identifier of the next main signal balise group allows the radio infill unit to identify safely where the train is going, even in the case of a points area

Clause 3.9.3.12 — As soon as the radio infill unit is informed that a train has entered an infill area under

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.3.12, PDF p.67

3.9.3.12 As soon as the radio infill unit is informed that a train has entered an infill area under its responsibility, it shall a) Terminate a possible previous sending of infill information to the on-board equipment, AND b) Send cyclically the infill information corresponding to the message currently sent by the next main signal balise group indicated in the information from the on-board equipment.

Clause 3.9.3.12.1 — Justification: case a) refers to the possibility that a report from the on-board equipment,

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.9.3.12.1, PDF p.67

3.9.3.12.1 Justification: case a) refers to the possibility that a report from the on-board equipment, after having passed the previous main signal, was lost.

Clause 3.10.2.1 — It shall be possible to stop a train with a conditional or an unconditional emergency

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.10.2.1, PDF p.69

3.10.2.1 It shall be possible to stop a train with a conditional or an unconditional emergency stop message.

Clause 3.10.2.2 — A conditional emergency stop message shall contain the information of a new stop

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.10.2.2, PDF p.69

3.10.2.2 A conditional emergency stop message shall contain the information of a new stop location, referred to the LRBG. In case, when receiving this message  the train has already passed with its min safe front end the new stop location, the emergency stop message shall be rejected and the RBC shall be informed.  the train has not yet passed with its min safe front end the new stop location, the emergency stop message shall be accepted, however this location shall be used by the onboard to define a new EOA/SvL only if not beyond the current EOA/LOA. Refer to appendix A.3.4 for the exhaustive list of location based information stored on-board, which shall be deleted accordingly.

Clause 3.10.2.3 — When receiving an unconditional emergency stop message the train shall be tripped

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.10.2.3, PDF p.69

3.10.2.3 When receiving an unconditional emergency stop message the train shall be tripped immediately.

Clause 3.10.3.3 — The revocation of an emergency message shall have no effect on the management of

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.10.3.3, PDF p.70

3.10.3.3 The revocation of an emergency message shall have no effect on the management of other emergency messages possibly received.

Clause 3.11 — Static Speed Restrictions and Gradients

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11, PDF p.70

3.11 Static Speed Restrictions and Gradients

Clause 3.11.1.1 — The permitted speed at which the train is allowed to travel shall be limited to different

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.1.1, PDF p.70

3.11.1.1 The permitted speed at which the train is allowed to travel shall be limited to different kinds of Static Speed Restrictions.

Clause 3.11.1.2 — A Static Speed Restriction shall be handled in the same way independent of ETCS

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.1.2, PDF p.70

3.11.1.2 A Static Speed Restriction shall be handled in the same way independent of ETCS level.

Clause 3.11.2 — Definition of Static Speed Restriction

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.2, PDF p.70

3.11.2 Definition of Static Speed Restriction

Clause 3.11.2.1 — Static Speed Restrictions are imposed by the trackside infrastructure, the train

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.2.1, PDF p.70

3.11.2.1 Static Speed Restrictions are imposed by the trackside infrastructure, the train characteristics, the signalling and the mode of the on-board equipment.

Clause 3.11.2.2 — There are eleven categories of Static Speed Restrictions:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.2.2, PDF p.70

3.11.2.2 There are eleven categories of Static Speed Restrictions: a) Static Speed Profile (SSP) b) Axle load Speed Profile (ASP) c) Temporary Speed Restrictions (TSR) d) Maximum Train Speed e) Signalling related speed restriction (only level 1) f) Mode related Speed Restriction. g) STM Max speed (for details refer to Subset-035) h) STM System speed (for details refer to Subset-035) i) Level Crossing speed restriction (LX SR) j) Override function related Speed Restriction k) Speed restriction to ensure a given permitted braking distance (PBD SR) (see 3.11.11)

Clause 3.11.2.3 — The Static Speed Restriction categories are independent of each other. This means

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.2.3, PDF p.70

3.11.2.3 The Static Speed Restriction categories are independent of each other. This means that one speed restriction category cannot affect, nor be affected by, any other category of Static Speed Restrictions.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.70

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP v d Maximum Train Speed Static Speed Profile Temporary Speed Restriction Mode related Speed Restriction Figure 26: Example of Static Speed Restriction categories on a piece of track.

Clause 3.11.2.4 — Depending on the type of Static Speed Restriction train length may have to be used to

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.2.4, PDF p.71

3.11.2.4 Depending on the type of Static Speed Restriction train length may have to be used to ensure that the full length of the train has passed a Static Speed Restriction discontinuity before a speed increase shall be taken into account.

Clause 3.11.3 — Static Speed Profile (SSP)

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.3, PDF p.71

3.11.3 Static Speed Profile (SSP)

Clause 3.11.3.1.1 — The Static Speed Profile (SSP) is a description of the fixed speed restrictions of a

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.3.1.1, PDF p.71

3.11.3.1.1 The Static Speed Profile (SSP) is a description of the fixed speed restrictions of a given piece of track. The speed restrictions can be related to e.g. maximum line speed, curves, points, tunnel profiles, bridges.

Clause 3.11.3.1.2 — The Static Speed Profile is based on factors, which are both track and train dependent.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.3.1.2, PDF p.71

3.11.3.1.2 The Static Speed Profile is based on factors, which are both track and train dependent. The relationship between track and train characteristics determines the individual Static Speed Profile for each train.

Clause 3.11.3.1.3 — It shall be possible for every element (distance between two discontinuities) of a static

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.3.1.3, PDF p.71

3.11.3.1.3 It shall be possible for every element (distance between two discontinuities) of a static speed profile to define, if a transition to a higher speed limit than the speed limit specified for this element is permitted before the complete train has left the element.

Clause 3.11.3.2 — Static Speed Profile Categories

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.3.2, PDF p.71

3.11.3.2 Static Speed Profile Categories

Clause 3.11.3.2.1 — It shall be possible to transmit several Static Speed Profile Categories; one Basic SSP

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.3.2.1, PDF p.71

3.11.3.2.1 It shall be possible to transmit several Static Speed Profile Categories; one Basic SSP category and specific SSP categories related to the international train categories.

Clause 3.11.3.2.1.1 — The specific SSP categories are decomposed into two types:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.3.2.1.1, PDF p.71

3.11.3.2.1.1 The specific SSP categories are decomposed into two types:

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.71

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP a) The “Cant Deficiency” SSP categories: the cant deficiency value assigned to one category shall define the maximum speed, determined by suspension design, at which a particular train can traverse a curve and thus can be used to set a specific speed limit in a curve with regards to this category. b) The “other specific” SSP categories: it groups all other specific SSP categories corresponding to the other international train categories

Clause 3.11.3.2.1.2 — Whenever the type of specific SSP category is not explicitly specified in the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.3.2.1.2, PDF p.72

3.11.3.2.1.2 Whenever the type of specific SSP category is not explicitly specified in the following requirements, it shall be interpreted as being applicable for both types of specific SSP categories.

Clause 3.11.3.2.2 — For each part of the Static Speed Profile, the ERTMS/ETCS trackside shall:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.3.2.2, PDF p.72

3.11.3.2.2 For each part of the Static Speed Profile, the ERTMS/ETCS trackside shall: a) always give the Basic SSP, which shall be considered as the default “Cant Deficiency” SSP b) optionally give one or more specific SSPs c) specify, for each “other specific” SSP, whether it replaces or not the “Cant Deficiency” SSP as selected by the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment according to 3.11.3.2.3

Clause 3.11.3.2.3 — For each part of the Static Speed Profile, the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.3.2.3, PDF p.72

3.11.3.2.3 For each part of the Static Speed Profile, the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall select the SSP best suiting its “Cant Deficiency” train category, according to the following order of preference: a) if available, the “Cant Deficiency” SSP matching its “Cant Deficiency” train category, OR b) if available, the “Cant Deficiency” SSP with the highest Cant Deficiency value below the value of its “Cant Deficiency” train category, OR c) the Basic SSP

Clause 3.11.3.2.3.1 — Intentionally deleted.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.3.2.3.1, PDF p.72

3.11.3.2.3.1 Intentionally deleted.

Clause 3.11.3.2.4 — Intentionally deleted.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.3.2.4, PDF p.72

3.11.3.2.4 Intentionally deleted.

Clause 3.11.3.2.5 — “Other Specific” SSP categories not relevant to the current train shall be ignored.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.3.2.5, PDF p.72

3.11.3.2.5 “Other Specific” SSP categories not relevant to the current train shall be ignored.

Clause 3.11.3.2.6 — For each part of the Static Speed Profile, the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment in a

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.3.2.6, PDF p.72

3.11.3.2.6 For each part of the Static Speed Profile, the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment in a train belonging to at least one or more “other international” train categories shall use the most restrictive speed amongst: a) the ” Cant Deficiency” SSP as selected in 3.11.3.2.3, only if none of the “other specific” SSP categories matching the train categories replaces the ” Cant Deficiency” SSP, AND b) all the “other specific” SSP categories matching the “other international” train categories.

Clause 3.11.4 — Axle load Speed Profile

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.4, PDF p.73

3.11.4 Axle load Speed Profile

Clause 3.11.4.1 — It shall be possible to define an Axle load Speed Profile as a non-continuous profile.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.4.1, PDF p.73

3.11.4.1 It shall be possible to define an Axle load Speed Profile as a non-continuous profile.

Clause 3.11.4.2 — For each section with a speed restriction due to axle load, the different speed value(s)

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.4.2, PDF p.73

3.11.4.2 For each section with a speed restriction due to axle load, the different speed value(s) and for which minimum axle load category this speed value(s) applies shall be specified.

Clause 3.11.4.2.1 — Note: Different speed restrictions depending on the axle load category can be

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.4.2.1, PDF p.73

3.11.4.2.1 Note: Different speed restrictions depending on the axle load category can be applicable to the same distance.

Clause 3.11.4.3 — The ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall consider the most restrictive speed

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.4.3, PDF p.73

3.11.4.3 The ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall consider the most restrictive speed restriction that is associated with any axle load category lower than, or equal to that of the train.

Clause 3.11.4.4 — For trains with an axle load category lower than the minimum axle load category given

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.4.4, PDF p.73

3.11.4.4 For trains with an axle load category lower than the minimum axle load category given in the profile, the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall not consider any speed restriction due to axle load.

Clause 3.11.4.5 — The initial state for Axle load Speed Profile shall be “no restriction due to axle load”.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.4.5, PDF p.73

3.11.4.5 The initial state for Axle load Speed Profile shall be “no restriction due to axle load”.

Clause 3.11.4.6 — Whether a speed increase after the axle load speed restriction shall be delayed with

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.4.6, PDF p.73

3.11.4.6 Whether a speed increase after the axle load speed restriction shall be delayed with train length, shall be determined by the axle load speed profile information sent to the on-board equipment.

Clause 3.11.5 — Temporary Speed Restrictions

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.5, PDF p.73

3.11.5 Temporary Speed Restrictions

Clause 3.11.5.1 — The temporary speed restriction is defined in order to enable a separate category of

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.5.1, PDF p.73

3.11.5.1 The temporary speed restriction is defined in order to enable a separate category of track infrastructure speed restriction, which can be used for working areas etc.

Clause 3.11.5.2 — All Temporary Speed Restrictions are independent of each other. This means that an

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.5.2, PDF p.73

3.11.5.2 All Temporary Speed Restrictions are independent of each other. This means that an individual Temporary Speed Restriction cannot affect, nor be affected by, any other individual Temporary Speed Restriction.

Clause 3.11.5.3 — Whether a speed increase after the temporary speed restriction shall be delayed with

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.5.3, PDF p.73

3.11.5.3 Whether a speed increase after the temporary speed restriction shall be delayed with train length, shall be determined by the temporary speed restriction information sent to the on-board equipment.

Clause 3.11.5.4 — When two or more temporary speed restrictions overlap, the most restrictive speed of

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.5.4, PDF p.73

3.11.5.4 When two or more temporary speed restrictions overlap, the most restrictive speed of the overlapping temporary speed restrictions shall be used in the area of overlap.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.73

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.11.5.5 — Each Temporary Speed Restriction shall have an identity to make it possible to revoke

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.5.5, PDF p.74

3.11.5.5 Each Temporary Speed Restriction shall have an identity to make it possible to revoke the Temporary Speed Restriction using its identity. The speed restriction shall be revoked immediately when revocation is received from trackside, without delay for the train length.

Clause 3.11.5.6 — It shall be possible to identify whether a Temporary Speed Restriction is possible to

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.5.6, PDF p.74

3.11.5.6 It shall be possible to identify whether a Temporary Speed Restriction is possible to revoke or not.

Clause 3.11.5.7 — A new Temporary Speed Restriction shall not replace a previously received Temporary

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.5.7, PDF p.74

3.11.5.7 A new Temporary Speed Restriction shall not replace a previously received Temporary Speed Restriction with another identity.

Clause 3.11.5.8 — Temporary Speed Restrictions shall only be revoked on request from the trackside.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.5.8, PDF p.74

3.11.5.8 Temporary Speed Restrictions shall only be revoked on request from the trackside.

Clause 3.11.5.9 — If the on-board equipment receives a new Temporary Speed Restriction (TSR) with the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.5.9, PDF p.74

3.11.5.9 If the on-board equipment receives a new Temporary Speed Restriction (TSR) with the same identity as an already received TSR, the new Temporary Speed Restriction shall replace the previous one, except when the Temporary Speed Restriction is identified as non revocable in which case this shall be considered as an additional TSR.

Clause 3.11.5.10 — In case the train has changed its orientation any Temporary Speed Restriction shall be

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.5.10, PDF p.74

3.11.5.10 In case the train has changed its orientation any Temporary Speed Restriction shall be deleted (operational requirement: will be executed due to the mode change).

Clause 3.11.5.11 — Intentionally deleted.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.5.11, PDF p.74

3.11.5.11 Intentionally deleted.

Clause 3.11.5.12 — It shall be possible for the RBC to order an ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment in Level

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.5.12, PDF p.74

3.11.5.12 It shall be possible for the RBC to order an ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment in Level 2 or 3 to reject revocable TSRs from balises.

Clause 3.11.5.13 — When ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment has accepted an order to reject revocable

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.5.13, PDF p.74

3.11.5.13 When ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment has accepted an order to reject revocable TSRs from balises, this inhibition shall be stored and shall be effective immediately, but only for revocable TSRs received from balises thereafter.

Clause 3.11.5.14 — The inhibition of revocable TSRs from balises shall be deleted if any of the following

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.5.14, PDF p.74

3.11.5.14 The inhibition of revocable TSRs from balises shall be deleted if any of the following occurs:  the communication session established with the RBC that ordered the inhibition is terminated, OR  in case of RBC/RBC handover, the train front end crosses the RBC/RBC border.

Clause 3.11.5.15 — Note: this inhibition may be useful in Level 1 / Level 2 mixed signalling applications

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.5.15, PDF p.74

3.11.5.15 Note: this inhibition may be useful in Level 1 / Level 2 mixed signalling applications when the RBC has more precise information about restrictions than can be given from balises. The RBC may then order inhibition of revocable TSRs from balises and instead send more precise TSRs to the train.

3.11.6.5.1 Note: The infill information will also include an EOA at the next main signal that will be supervised according to the normal rules.

Clause 3.11.7 — Mode related speed restrictions

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.7.1.1, PDF p.75

3.11.7.1.1 Exception 1: For the modes On-sight, Limited Supervision and Shunting the speed limit can also be given from the trackside. The speed limit given from the trackside shall prevail over the National value and the default value.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.7.1.2, PDF p.75

3.11.7.1.2 Exception 2: For the mode Reversing there is no National/Default value. The speed limit is always given from trackside.

3.11.7.1.3 Exception 3: For the mode Staff Responsible the speed limit can also be entered by the driver. The speed limit given by the driver shall prevail over the National/Default value.

Clause 3.11.11 — Speed restriction to ensure permitted braking distance

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.11, PDF p.76

3.11.11 Speed restriction to ensure permitted braking distance

Clause 3.11.11.1 — It shall be possible for trackside to request the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment to

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.11.1, PDF p.76

3.11.11.1 It shall be possible for trackside to request the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment to calculate a speed restriction based on a permitted braking distance given by trackside.

Clause 3.11.11.2 — The order shall be given by means of a non-continuous profile defining:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.11.2, PDF p.76

3.11.11.2 The order shall be given by means of a non-continuous profile defining:  The start and end location for the speed restriction  The permitted braking distance (PBD) used to calculate the speed restriction value  Whether the permitted braking distance is to be achieved with the Service Brake or Emergency Brake  A single gradient value applicable for the calculation

Clause 3.11.11.3 — The speed restriction shall be calculated when the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.11.3, PDF p.76

3.11.11.3 The speed restriction shall be calculated when the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment receives the permitted braking distance information from trackside, and shall be re- calculated only if any of the inputs taken into account for the calculation of the speed restriction changes.

Clause 3.11.11.4 — The calculation of the speed restriction by the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.11.4, PDF p.76

3.11.11.4 The calculation of the speed restriction by the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall take into account that:  The single gradient value received from trackside shall be compensated in value according to the rotating mass as defined in 3.13.4.3.  The safe deceleration shall be computed as in 3.13.6.2.1 but without considering the adhesion profiles, the track conditions related to brake inhibition and the track conditions related to powerless section given by trackside.  The expected deceleration shall be computed as in 3.13.6.3.1 but without considering the track conditions related to brake inhibition and the track conditions related to powerless section given by trackside.  The ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall calculate an Emergency Brake Deceleration (EBD) curve based on the safe deceleration and that reaches zero speed at a distance equal to the permitted braking distance.  If the permitted braking distance is to be achieved with the service brake, the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall also calculate a Service Brake Deceleration (SBD) curve based on the expected deceleration and that reaches zero speed at a distance equal to the permitted braking distance.  The estimated acceleration shall be set to “zero”.  If not inhibited by National Value, the compensation of the inaccuracy of the speed measurement shall be set to a value calculated from the PBD speed, as defined in SUBSET-041 § 5.3.1.2: V_delta0PBD = f (V_PBD + dV_EBI(V_PBD)) if the 41

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.76

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP permitted braking distance is to be achieved with the emergency brake; V_delta0PBD = f (V_PBD + dV_SBI(V_PBD)) if the permitted braking distance is 41 to be achieved with the service brake.  The train will travel a distance from the last encountered balise of a group that provides restrictive information until initiating the brake command. This travelled distance shall be set to a value calculated from the PBD speed considering a processing delay (T ) equal to SUBSET-041 § 5.2.1.1. 41  Regardless of how the service brake feedback is actually configured (see 3.13.2.2.7.2), T_bs1 and T_bs2 (see 3.13.9.3.3) shall be defined as if the service brake feedback was not implemented.  Regardless of how the traction cut-off interface is actually configured (see 3.13.2.2.8), T_traction (see 3.13.9.3.2) shall be defined as if the traction cut-off was not implemented.

Clause 3.11.11.4.1 — Note: Knowing how the PBD speed restriction is computed by the ERTMS/ETCS on-

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.11.4.1, PDF p.77

3.11.11.4.1 Note: Knowing how the PBD speed restriction is computed by the ERTMS/ETCS on- board equipment, it is the responsibility of the trackside to set the appropriate permitted braking distance with regard to the risk of not initiating the brake command in due time when encountering a further balise group providing restrictive information. In other words, if deemed necessary, the trackside can include provisions based on the characteristics of the balise group providing restrictive information e.g. distances between balises in that group with regards to validity direction of transmitted information and balise group orientation, accuracy of balise location.

Clause 3.11.11.4.2 — Note: If the permitted braking distance is to be achieved with the service brake, it is the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.11.4.2, PDF p.77

3.11.11.4.2 Note: If the permitted braking distance is to be achieved with the service brake, it is the responsibility of the trackside to also consider an estimation of the on-board over- reading and under-reading amounts at the time the brake command is initiated in order to lower the likelihood of the max safe front end of the train reaching the EBI supervision limit.

Clause 3.11.11.5 — Note: Throughout the following formulas, all the distances marked with “d” (lower case)

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.11.5, PDF p.77

3.11.11.5 Note: Throughout the following formulas, all the distances marked with “d” (lower case) are counted from a single arbitrary reference location.

Clause 3.11.11.6 — If the permitted braking distance is to be achieved with the emergency brake, the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.11.6, PDF p.77

Clause 3.11.11.7 — If the permitted braking distance is to be achieved with the service brake, the PBD

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.11.7, PDF p.78

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.11.10, PDF p.79

3.11.11.10 Note: The method chosen (e.g. iterative algorithm) to compute the PBD speed restriction(s) is an implementation issue.

Clause 3.11.11.11 — The initial state for Speed Restrictions to Ensure Permitted Braking Distance shall be

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.11.11, PDF p.79

3.11.11.11 The initial state for Speed Restrictions to Ensure Permitted Braking Distance shall be “no speed restriction”.

Clause 3.11.12.1 — The gradient information for a given piece of track shall be transmitted to the on-board

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.12.1, PDF p.79

3.11.12.1 The gradient information for a given piece of track shall be transmitted to the on-board equipment in form of a gradient profile.

Clause 3.11.12.2 — The gradient profile shall be continuous, i.e., give a gradient value for each location

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.12.2, PDF p.79

3.11.12.2 The gradient profile shall be continuous, i.e., give a gradient value for each location within the piece of track covered by the profile.

Clause 3.11.12.3 — A gradient value shall be identified as a positive value for an uphill slope, and with a

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.12.3, PDF p.79

3.11.12.3 A gradient value shall be identified as a positive value for an uphill slope, and with a negative value for a downhill slope.

Clause 3.11.12.4 — The gradient profile shall contain the gradient information as a sequence of gradient

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.12.4, PDF p.79

3.11.12.4 The gradient profile shall contain the gradient information as a sequence of gradient values, constant between two defined locations each, see Figure 27.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.12.4.1, PDF p.80

3.11.12.4.1 Note: The figure above symbolises the engineering process to provide the values of gradients. Following the track height, the track must be split in segments giving for each segment a gradient value.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.11.12.5, PDF p.80

3.11.12.5 It shall be possible via balise groups to send to the on-board equipment a default gradient for TSR, to be used for the parts of the track not covered by the gradient profile.

3.11.12.6 The Default Gradient for TSR stored on-board shall be valid until a new Default Gradient for TSR is received.

Clause 3.12.1.1 — The Track Condition function is used to inform the driver and/or the train of a condition

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.12.1.1, PDF p.80

3.12.1.1 The Track Condition function is used to inform the driver and/or the train of a condition in front of the train.

Clause 3.12.1.2 — A Track Condition shall be given as profile data (e.g. non-stopping area), i.e. start and

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.12.1.2, PDF p.80

3.12.1.2 A Track Condition shall be given as profile data (e.g. non-stopping area), i.e. start and end of the data is given, or location data (e.g. change of traction system) i.e. start location given, depending on the type of track condition.

Clause 3.12.1.2.1 — The starting point of a profile type track condition shall be evaluated taking into

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.12.1.2.1, PDF p.80

3.12.1.2.1 The starting point of a profile type track condition shall be evaluated taking into account the max safe front end of the train, the end of the profile taking into account the min safe rear end of the train. Location type data shall be evaluated taking into account the max safe front of the train.

Clause 3.12.1.2.1.1 — Note: The timing of output data to control train equipment (e.g. pantograph) is

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.12.1.2.1.1, PDF p.80

3.12.1.2.1.1 Note: The timing of output data to control train equipment (e.g. pantograph) is application specific.

Clause 3.12.1.2.1.2 — Exception 1: The starting point of a Big Metal Mass type track condition shall be

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.12.1.2.1.2, PDF p.80

3.12.1.2.1.2 Exception 1: The starting point of a Big Metal Mass type track condition shall be evaluated taking into account the max safe antenna position, the end of the profile taking into account the min safe antenna position.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.80

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.12.1.2.1.3 — Exception 2: The end of the Powerless section and the end of the Station Platform

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.12.1.2.1.3, PDF p.81

3.12.1.2.1.3 Exception 2: The end of the Powerless section and the end of the Station Platform shall be evaluated taking into account the min safe front end of the train.

Clause 3.12.1.2.1.4 — Exception 3: The start and end of a tunnel stopping area and of a sound horn track

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.12.1.2.1.4, PDF p.81

3.12.1.2.1.4 Exception 3: The start and end of a tunnel stopping area and of a sound horn track condition shall be evaluated taking into account the estimated front end of the train.

Clause 3.12.1.3 — The types of track conditions to be covered by this function are:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.12.1.3, PDF p.81

3.12.1.3 The types of track conditions to be covered by this function are:  Powerless section, lower pantograph (initial state: no powerless section, i.e. pantograph not to be lowered)  Powerless section, switch off main power switch (initial state: no powerless section, i.e. main power switch not to be switched off)  Air tightness (initial state: no request for air tightness)  Sound horn (initial state: no request for sound horn)  Non stopping area (initial state: stopping permitted)  Tunnel stopping area (initial state: no tunnel stopping area)  Change of traction system, switch traction system on-board, used for train capable of handling several traction systems (initial state: no initial state – keep the current setting)  Change of allowed current consumption, limit current consumed by the train, used to adapt the maximum current consumption of the train to the maximum current allowed by the trackside (initial state: no initial state – keep the current setting)  Big metal masses, ignore onboard integrity check alarms of balise transmission. (initial state: alarms not ignored)  Radio hole, stop supervision of the loss of safe radio connection (initial state: loss of safe radio connection supervised)  Switch off regenerative brake (initial state: regenerative brake on)  Switch off eddy current brake for service brake (initial state: eddy current brake on for service brake)  Switch off eddy current brake for emergency brake (initial state: eddy current brake on for emergency brake)  Switch off magnetic shoe brake (initial state: magnetic shoe brake on).  Station platform, enable passenger doors with or without steps according to platform location, side and height (initial state: no platform, i.e. passenger doors not enabled).

Clause 3.12.1.3.1 — Note: In case of regenerative brake switch off or magnetic shoe brake switch off, the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.12.1.3.1, PDF p.81

3.12.1.3.1 Note: In case of regenerative brake switch off or magnetic shoe brake switch off, the deceleration of the emergency brake might be affected if the effect of these brakes was included in the calculation of the deceleration value.

Clause 3.12.1.3.2 — Note: In case of eddy current brake switch off the deceleration of the service brake or

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.12.1.3.2, PDF p.82

3.12.1.3.2 Note: In case of eddy current brake switch off the deceleration of the service brake or emergency brake might be affected if the effect of these brakes was included in the calculation of the deceleration value.

Clause 3.12.1.3.3 — Note: in case of powerless section the deceleration of the service brake or emergency

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.12.1.3.3, PDF p.82

3.12.1.3.3 Note: in case of powerless section the deceleration of the service brake or emergency brake might be affected if the effect of a regenerative brake not independent from the presence of voltage in the catenary was included in the calculation of the deceleration value.

Clause 3.12.1.5 — The following actions shall be performed once a track condition has been received:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.12.1.5, PDF p.82

3.12.1.5 The following actions shall be performed once a track condition has been received: a) Indicate on DMI (see chapter 5, procedure “Indication of track conditions”), except “Station platform”, “Change of allowed current consumption” and “Big metal masses”. b) Send information with the remaining distance to an ERTMS/ETCS external function (see chapter 5, procedure “Generation of track conditions related information to an ERTMS/ETCS external function”), with the exception of big metal mass track condition, sound horn track condition, non stopping area, tunnel stopping area and supervision of radio transmission which are handled inside the ERTMS/ETCS on- board equipment.

Clause 3.12.1.5.2 — Note: The ERTMS/ETCS external function must be able to handle new track condition

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.12.1.5.2, PDF p.82

3.12.1.5.2 Note: The ERTMS/ETCS external function must be able to handle new track condition of the same type as previously received and covering the same distance.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.12.1.6, PDF p.82

3.12.1.6 The train is permitted to run without any track condition information given from the trackside. The initial state shall then be used by the on-board equipment.

Clause 3.12.4.1 — It shall be possible for trackside to send a Mode Profile. The Mode Profile can request

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.12.4.1, PDF p.86

Clause 3.12.4.2 — For OS and LS mode the mode profile defines the entry and the length of the On

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.12.4.2, PDF p.86

3.12.4.3.1 Exception: When receiving a new MA by infill, any currently supervised Mode Profile shall be deleted only beyond the reference location of the infill information.

Clause 3.12.4.4 — In case the mode profile information for shunting is overwritten by a new shunting

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.12.4.4, PDF p.86

3.12.4.4 In case the mode profile information for shunting is overwritten by a new shunting profile, before the on-board equipment switches to SH mode, a previous list of balise groups for SH area shall be deleted or replaced by a new list of balise groups for SH area.

3.12.4.6 The end of the mode profile relates to the min safe front end of the train.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.12.5.1, PDF p.86

3.12.5.1 It shall be possible for trackside to inform the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment about the conditions under which a Level Crossing (LX) must be passed.

Clause 3.12.5.2 — Each Level Crossing shall have an identity, so that all LX information is independent of

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.12.5.2, PDF p.86

3.12.5.2 Each Level Crossing shall have an identity, so that all LX information is independent of each other. This means that an individual LX information cannot affect, nor be affected by, any other individual LX information.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.12.5.3, PDF p.86

3.12.5.3 If the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment receives a new LX information with the same identity as an already received LX information, the new LX information shall replace the previous one.

3.12.5.4 Level Crossing information shall be given as profile data, corresponding to the LX start location and the length of the LX area.

Clause 3.12.5.5 — Level Crossing information shall indicate whether the LX is protected or not.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.12.5.5, PDF p.86

3.12.5.5 Level Crossing information shall indicate whether the LX is protected or not.

Clause 3.12.5.6 — In case the LX is not protected, ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall be informed:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.12.5.6, PDF p.86

3.12.5.6 In case the LX is not protected, ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall be informed: a) at which speed the LX is allowed to be passed b) whether the stopping of the train in rear of the LX start location is required or not

Clause 3.13 — Speed and distance monitoring

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13, PDF p.87

3.13 Speed and distance monitoring

Clause 3.13.1.1 — The speed and distance monitoring is the supervision of the speed of the train versus

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.1.1, PDF p.87

3.13.1.1 The speed and distance monitoring is the supervision of the speed of the train versus its position, in order to assure that the train remains within the given speed and distance limits.

Clause 3.13.1.1.1 — Note: The speed and distance monitoring of the on-board can only assure this when

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.1.1.1, PDF p.87

3.13.1.1.1 Note: The speed and distance monitoring of the on-board can only assure this when the following necessary conditions are fulfilled:  Brake system of the train functions as specified  wheel/rail adhesion is sufficient for the required safe deceleration  Brake characteristics (and other Train related inputs) are correctly entered into the on-board

Clause 3.13.1.2 — Note: The ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment triggers brake commands and revokes

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.1.2, PDF p.87

3.13.1.2 Note: The ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment triggers brake commands and revokes them, it may also receive status information if the brakes are applied or released. However, it cannot be made responsible if brake control circuits outside the equipment fail. Also the way the brakes are released by the driver after a revocation of a brake command is an implementation issue.

Clause 3.13.1.3 — Figure 28 gives an overview of the main elements contributing to the speed and

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.1.3, PDF p.87

3.13.1.3 Figure 28 gives an overview of the main elements contributing to the speed and distance monitoring. These elements (inputs, functions and outputs) are detailed in the following chapters.

Clause 3.13.1.4 — Throughout the following sections, all the distances marked with “d” (lower case),

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.1.4, PDF p.88

3.13.1.4 Throughout the following sections, all the distances marked with “d” (lower case), which are referred in parameters, formulas and figures, are counted from the current reference location of the on-board equipment (e.g. the LRBG).

Clause 3.13.2 — Inputs for speed and distance monitoring

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2, PDF p.88

3.13.2 Inputs for speed and distance monitoring

Clause 3.13.2.1.1 — The traction / braking models, the brake position / brake percentage are used for the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.1.1, PDF p.88

3.13.2.1.1 The traction / braking models, the brake position / brake percentage are used for the definition of the kinematic behaviour of the train after a service brake command or an emergency brake command has been initiated.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.88

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.13.2.1.2 — However, railway brakes have a statistical behaviour and braking distances vary within

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.1.2, PDF p.89

3.13.2.1.2 However, railway brakes have a statistical behaviour and braking distances vary within the typical distribution for a given condition. Correction factors are therefore incorporated for the speed and distance monitoring.

Clause 3.13.2.1.3 — The correction factors will allow obtaining, from the nominal emergency braking

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.1.3, PDF p.89

3.13.2.1.3 The correction factors will allow obtaining, from the nominal emergency braking performance of the train, the minimum emergency braking performances that are required for reference conditions set by trackside.

Clause 3.13.2.2 — Train related inputs

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2, PDF p.89

3.13.2.2 Train related inputs

Clause 3.13.2.2.1.1 — The train related inputs to be considered for the speed and distance monitoring are:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.1.1, PDF p.89

3.13.2.2.1.1 The train related inputs to be considered for the speed and distance monitoring are: a) Traction model b) Braking models (brake build up time and speed dependent deceleration) or brake percentage c) Brake position d) Special brakes (interface configuration and status) e) Service brake (interface configuration and application) f) Traction cut-off interface g) On-board correction factors h) Nominal rotating mass i) Train length j) Fixed values related to speed and distance monitoring k) Train related speed restriction (i.e. the maximum train speed)

Clause 3.13.2.2.1.2 — These train related inputs are acquired as Train Data (see 3.18.3.2 items b) c) and

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.1.2, PDF p.89

3.13.2.2.1.2 These train related inputs are acquired as Train Data (see 3.18.3.2 items b) c) and d)), except:  the configuration of the special brakes, service brake and traction cut-off interfaces which are not affected by the Train Data acquisition,  the service brake application and the special brakes statuses which are continuously acquired on the Train Interface,  the fixed values.

Clause 3.13.2.2.1.3 — The speed and distance monitoring shall use braking models acquired as Train

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.1.3, PDF p.89

3.13.2.2.1.3 The speed and distance monitoring shall use braking models acquired as Train Data, unless the brake percentage is acquired as Train Data and the conversion model is applicable (see 3.13.3.2 for its validity limits).

Clause 3.13.2.2.3 — Braking Models
