3.13.2.2.3 Braking Models

Clause 3.13.2.2.3.1 — Speed Dependent Deceleration

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.3.1, PDF p.90

3.13.2.2.3.1 Speed Dependent Deceleration

Clause 3.13.2.2.3.1.1 — The deceleration due to braking shall be given as a step function of the speed.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.3.1.1, PDF p.90

3.13.2.2.3.1.1 The deceleration due to braking shall be given as a step function of the speed.

Clause 3.13.2.2.3.1.2 — It shall be possible to define up to seven steps for each speed dependent

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.3.1.2, PDF p.90

3.13.2.2.3.1.2 It shall be possible to define up to seven steps for each speed dependent deceleration model.

Clause 3.13.2.2.3.1.3 — Note: An example with 4 steps is given in Figure 30. A_brake(V) is calculated as

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.3.1.3, PDF p.90

3.13.2.2.3.1.3 Note: An example with 4 steps is given in Figure 30. A_brake(V) is calculated as follows:  A_brake = AD_0 when 0 ≤ speed ≤ V1  A_brake = AD_1 when V1 < speed ≤ V2  A_brake = AD_2 when V2 < speed ≤ V3  A_brake = AD_3 when V3 < speed A_brake AD_1 AD_0 AD_2 AD_3 0 V1 V2 V3 speed

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.90

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP Figure 30: Speed Dependent Deceleration Model

Clause 3.13.2.2.3.1.4 — The last step of A_brake(V) shall by definition be considered as open ended, i.e. it

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.3.1.4, PDF p.91

3.13.2.2.3.1.4 The last step of A_brake(V) shall by definition be considered as open ended, i.e. it has no upper speed limit.

Clause 3.13.2.2.3.1.5 — The model shall be applicable only after full build up of the braking effort (see a_full

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.3.1.5, PDF p.91

3.13.2.2.3.1.5 The model shall be applicable only after full build up of the braking effort (see a_full in Figure 31)

Clause 3.13.2.2.3.1.6 — The model shall be used for the emergency brake nominal deceleration

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.3.1.6, PDF p.91

3.13.2.2.3.1.6 The model shall be used for the emergency brake nominal deceleration (A_brake_emergency(V)), for the full service brake deceleration (A_brake_service(V)) and for the normal service brake deceleration (A_brake_normal_service(V)).

Clause 3.13.2.2.3.1.7 — It shall be possible to define individual speed dependent deceleration models of

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.3.1.7, PDF p.91

3.13.2.2.3.1.7 It shall be possible to define individual speed dependent deceleration models of A_brake_emergency(V) and A_brake_service(V) for each combination of use of regenerative brake, eddy current brake and magnetic shoe brake.

Clause 3.13.2.2.3.1.8 — Note: Individual deceleration models may be equal, thereby avoiding the influence

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.3.1.8, PDF p.91

3.13.2.2.3.1.8 Note: Individual deceleration models may be equal, thereby avoiding the influence of a specific brake on A_brake_emergency(V) or A_brake_service(V). However, the choice to take into account or not the contribution of a specific brake for A_brake_emergency(V) or A_brake_service(V) is only rolling stock dependent, not an ETCS implementation issue.

Clause 3.13.2.2.3.1.9 — It shall be possible to define up to two sets of three models of

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.3.1.9, PDF p.91

3.13.2.2.3.1.9 It shall be possible to define up to two sets of three models of A_brake_normal_service(V): a) one set applicable when the brake position is in “Freight train in G” b) one set applicable when the brake position is in “Passenger train in P” or “Freight train in P”

Clause 3.13.2.2.3.1.10 — A set of A_brake_normal_service(V) shall be defined as a function of the full

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.3.1.10, PDF p.91

3.13.2.2.3.1.10 A set of A_brake_normal_service(V) shall be defined as a function of the full service brake deceleration at zero speed, A_brake_service(V=0): If A_brake_service(V = 0)  A_SB01 A_brake_normal_service(V) = A_brake_normal_service_0(V) if A_SB01 < A_brake_service(V = 0)  A_SB12 A_brake_normal_service(V) = A_brake_normal_service_1(V) if A_SB12 < A_brake_service (V = 0) A_brake_normal_service(V) = A_brake_normal_service_2(V)

Clause 3.13.2.2.3.1.11 — Note: the two pivot values A_SB01 and A_SB12 are part of the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.3.1.11, PDF p.91

3.13.2.2.3.1.11 Note: the two pivot values A_SB01 and A_SB12 are part of the A_brake_normal_service model, i.e. they are train related input data for the speed and distance monitoring function.

Clause 3.13.2.2.3.2 — Brake build up time

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.3.2, PDF p.91

3.13.2.2.3.2 Brake build up time

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.91

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.13.2.2.3.2.1 — The deceleration A_brake is not available immediately after the on-board

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.3.2.1, PDF p.92

3.13.2.2.3.2.1 The deceleration A_brake is not available immediately after the on-board commands the brake. There is a time lag between brake command and the start of the brake force build-up. There is also time needed to build up the full brake force.

Clause 3.13.2.2.3.2.2 — The model for the brake build up time shall be given as a step function as explained

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.3.2.2, PDF p.92

3.13.2.2.3.2.2 The model for the brake build up time shall be given as a step function as explained in Figure 31. A_brake a_full 95% t0 t1 t3 t2 time Figure 31: Brake Build Up Time Model

Clause 3.13.2.2.3.2.3 — In Figure 31, the following time intervals are defined:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.3.2.3, PDF p.92

3.13.2.2.3.2.3 In Figure 31, the following time intervals are defined: a) T_brake_react (t0…t1) is the interval between the command of the brake by the on- board and the moment the brake force starts to build up. b) T_brake_increase (t1...t2) is the interval in which the brake force increases from the zero to the moment when 95% of full brake power is reached. c) T_brake_build_up (t0...t3) is the equivalent brake build up time.

Clause 3.13.2.2.3.2.4 — The equivalent brake build up time (T_brake_build_up) is defined as

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.3.2.4, PDF p.92

3.13.2.2.3.2.4 The equivalent brake build up time (T_brake_build_up) is defined as T_brake_build_up = T_brake_react + 0.5*T_brake_increase.

Clause 3.13.2.2.3.2.5 — This model for T_brake_build_up shall be used for the emergency brake

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.3.2.5, PDF p.92

3.13.2.2.3.2.5 This model for T_brake_build_up shall be used for the emergency brake (T_brake_emergency) and for the full service brake (T_brake_service).

Clause 3.13.2.2.3.2.6 — Note: The equivalent brake build up time is a safe approximation. In the beginning

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.3.2.6, PDF p.92

3.13.2.2.3.2.6 Note: The equivalent brake build up time is a safe approximation. In the beginning of the build-up time the model assumes a smaller deceleration, in the later part this is compensated by a higher deceleration.

Clause 3.13.2.2.3.2.7 — Note: T_brake_react and T_brake_increase are indicated in Figure 31 for

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.3.2.7, PDF p.92

3.13.2.2.3.2.7 Note: T_brake_react and T_brake_increase are indicated in Figure 31 for completeness reasons; only T_brake_build_up is to be considered as an input for the speed and distance monitoring function.

Clause 3.13.2.2.3.2.8 — It shall be possible to define individual values of T_brake_emergency and

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.3.2.8, PDF p.92

3.13.2.2.3.2.8 It shall be possible to define individual values of T_brake_emergency and T_brake_service for each combination of use of regenerative brake, eddy current brake, magnetic shoe brake and Ep brake.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.92

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.13.2.2.3.2.9 — Note: Individual values of T_brake_emergency and T_brake_service may be equal,

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.3.2.9, PDF p.93

3.13.2.2.3.2.9 Note: Individual values of T_brake_emergency and T_brake_service may be equal, thereby avoiding the influence of a specific brake. However, the choice to take into account or not the contribution of a specific brake for T_brake_emergency and T_brake_service is only rolling stock dependent, not an ETCS implementation issue.

Clause 3.13.2.2.3.2.10 — Note: In general, T_brake_emergency and T_brake_service are determined by

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.3.2.10, PDF p.93

3.13.2.2.3.2.10 Note: In general, T_brake_emergency and T_brake_service are determined by the pneumatic brake therefore avoiding to take into account of the influence of the regenerative brake, eddy current brake or magnetic shoe brake. However, if the Electro-pneumatic brake system is used, it is possible that T_brake_emergency and T_brake_service are determined by another special brake.

Clause 3.13.2.2.4 — Brake Position

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.4, PDF p.93

3.13.2.2.4 Brake Position

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.4.1, PDF p.93

3.13.2.2.4.1 The brake position shall be set to one of the following three values: a) Passenger train in P b) Freight train in P c) Freight train in G

Clause 3.13.2.2.4.2 — Note: The brake position defines the behaviour of the brake for specific train types.

3.13.2.2.4.2 Note: The brake position defines the behaviour of the brake for specific train types.

3.13.2.2.5 Brake Percentage

Clause 3.13.2.2.5.1 — If the brake percentage is captured as Train Data and the conversion model is

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.5.1, PDF p.93

3.13.2.2.5.1 If the brake percentage is captured as Train Data and the conversion model is applicable (see 3.13.3.2), they are used to derive A_brake_emergency(V), A_brake_service(V), T_brake_emergency and T_brake_service.

Clause 3.13.2.2.5.2 — Note: the conversion model has been designed assuming that all the provisions laid

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.5.2, PDF p.93

3.13.2.2.5.2 Note: the conversion model has been designed assuming that all the provisions laid down in the UIC leaflet 544-1 6th edition, with the exception of sections 9.1.2, 9.2.2 and 9.3.3, apply for the acquired brake percentage.

3.13.2.2.6 Special Brakes

Clause 3.13.2.2.6.1 — For each special brake (regenerative brake, eddy current brake, magnetic shoe

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.6.1, PDF p.94

3.13.2.2.6.1 For each special brake (regenerative brake, eddy current brake, magnetic shoe brake and electro-pneumatic brake), the on-board shall be configured to define one of the following possibilities marked with an “X” in Table 3 configuration possibilities No interface Interface Interface Interface exists exists and exists and exists and status affects status affects status affects the the service both emergency brake model emergency brake model only and service only brake models regenerative x x x x brake e a k eddy current x x x x r b brake al ci magnetic shoe x x e p S brake Ep brake x x x Table 3: On-board Configuration in relation to special brakes

Clause 3.13.2.2.6.2 — When an interface exists with the regenerative brake, eddy current brake, magnetic

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.6.2, PDF p.94

3.13.2.2.6.2 When an interface exists with the regenerative brake, eddy current brake, magnetic shoe brake system and/or the Ep brake on-board system and depending whether their status affects the concerned brake parameter(s), the speed and distance monitoring shall take into account their status “active” or “not active” to select the appropriate brake parameter(s) captured as Train Data, according to Table 4: When interface exists and if status affects the brake parameter, selection of brake parameter according to status of: regenerative eddy magnetic Ep brake brake current shoe brake brake A_brake_emergency(V) x x x r e e t e T_brake_emergency x x x x k m a r a A_brake_service(V) x x B r a p T_brake_service x x x Table 4: Selection of brake parameters according to status of special brakes

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.94

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.13.2.2.6.3 — When the brake percentage is captured as Train Data and the conversion model is

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.6.3, PDF p.95

3.13.2.2.6.3 When the brake percentage is captured as Train Data and the conversion model is applicable, A_brake_emergency(V), T_brake_emergency and A_brake_service(V) shall not be influenced by the status of a special brake. However, the conversion model offers the possibility that T_brake_service can be affected by the status of the regenerative brake, eddy current brake or Ep brake (see A.3.9).

Clause 3.13.2.2.6.4 — The on-board equipment shall be configured to define whether it is allowed to take

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.6.4, PDF p.95

3.13.2.2.6.4 The on-board equipment shall be configured to define whether it is allowed to take into account the contribution of a special/additional brake, which is independent from wheel/rail adhesion, for the selection of the maximum emergency brake deceleration under reduced adhesion conditions (see 3.13.6.2.1.6).

Clause 3.13.2.2.6.5 — Note: the choice to set to “allowed” the contribution of such special/additional brake

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.6.5, PDF p.95

3.13.2.2.6.5 Note: the choice to set to “allowed” the contribution of such special/additional brake in the selection of the maximum emergency braking effort, is rolling stock dependent.

Clause 3.13.2.2.6.6 — If it is allowed to take into account the contribution of a special/additional brake,

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.6.6, PDF p.95

3.13.2.2.6.6 If it is allowed to take into account the contribution of a special/additional brake, which is independent from wheel/rail adhesion, the speed and distance monitoring function shall take into account the status “active” or “not active” of the special/additional brake to select the appropriate National Value under reduced adhesion conditions (see 3.13.2.3.7.7).

Clause 3.13.2.2.7.1 — The on-board shall be configured to define whether the service brake command is

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.7.1, PDF p.95

3.13.2.2.7.1 The on-board shall be configured to define whether the service brake command is implemented or not, i.e. whether a service brake interface is implemented to command a full service brake effort.

Clause 3.13.2.2.7.2 — The on-board shall be configured to define whether the service brake feedback is

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.7.2, PDF p.95

3.13.2.2.7.2 The on-board shall be configured to define whether the service brake feedback is implemented or not, i.e. whether it is able to acquire from the service brake interface the information that the service brake is currently applied.

Clause 3.13.2.2.7.3 — If the service brake feedback is implemented and if not inhibited by National Value,

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.7.3, PDF p.95

3.13.2.2.7.3 If the service brake feedback is implemented and if not inhibited by National Value, the speed and distance monitoring function shall take into account either the main brake pipe pressure or the brake cylinder pressure to adjust in real time the expected brake build up time (see 3.13.9.3.3.4 and Appendix A.3.10).

Clause 3.13.2.2.8 — Traction cut-off interface

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.8, PDF p.95

3.13.2.2.8 Traction cut-off interface

Clause 3.13.2.2.8.1 — The on-board shall be configured to define whether the traction cut-off command is

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.8.1, PDF p.95

3.13.2.2.8.1 The on-board shall be configured to define whether the traction cut-off command is implemented, i.e. whether the interface to the traction system is implemented or not.

Clause 3.13.2.2.9 — On-board Correction Factors

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.9, PDF p.95

3.13.2.2.9 On-board Correction Factors

Clause 3.13.2.2.9.1 — Correction factors for the emergency deceleration

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.9.1, PDF p.95

3.13.2.2.9.1 Correction factors for the emergency deceleration

Clause 3.13.2.2.9.1.1 — If the braking models are captured as Train Data, rolling stock correction factors

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.9.1.1, PDF p.95

3.13.2.2.9.1.1 If the braking models are captured as Train Data, rolling stock correction factors shall be defined in the ETCS on-board equipment. If the brake percentage is captured as Train Data and the conversion model is used (see 3.13.3.2 for its validity limits), no rolling stock correction factor shall apply.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.95

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.13.2.2.9.1.2 — For each defined individual speed dependent deceleration model of

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.9.1.2, PDF p.96

3.13.2.2.9.1.2 For each defined individual speed dependent deceleration model of A_brake_emergency(V) (i.e. corresponding to each combination of use of regenerative brake, eddy current brake and magnetic shoe brake), one set of rolling stock correction factors Kdry_rst(V, EBCL) and Kwet_rst(V) shall be defined in the on-board equipment.

Clause 3.13.2.2.9.1.3 — For a given confidence level on emergency brake safe deceleration (EBCL), the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.9.1.3, PDF p.96

3.13.2.2.9.1.3 For a given confidence level on emergency brake safe deceleration (EBCL), the rolling stock correction factor Kdry_rst(V) shall be given as a step function of speed, with the same steps as the ones of A_brake_emergency(V).

Clause 3.13.2.2.9.1.4 — The confidence level on emergency brake safe deceleration represents the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.9.1.4, PDF p.96

3.13.2.2.9.1.4 The confidence level on emergency brake safe deceleration represents the probability of the following individual event: the rolling stock emergency brake subsystem of the train does ensure a deceleration at least equal to A_brake_emergency(V) * Kdry_rst(V), when the emergency brake is commanded on dry rails.

Clause 3.13.2.2.9.1.5 — The rolling stock correction factor Kwet_rst(V) shall be given as a step function of

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.9.1.5, PDF p.96

3.13.2.2.9.1.5 The rolling stock correction factor Kwet_rst(V) shall be given as a step function of speed, with the same steps as the ones of A_brake_emergency(V). It represents the loss of deceleration with regards to emergency braking on dry rails, when the emergency brake is commanded on wet rails, according to wheel/rail adhesion reference conditions.

Clause 3.13.2.2.9.2 — Correction factor for gradient on normal service deceleration

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.2.2.9.2, PDF p.96

3.13.2.3.7.14 The correction factor for brake build up time (Kt_int) shall be a single parameter.

Clause 3.13.3.1.1 — For trains with variable composition (loco hauled trains), the brake characteristics can

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.3.1.1, PDF p.101

3.13.3.1.1 For trains with variable composition (loco hauled trains), the brake characteristics can vary together with the composition of the train. In this case, it is not convenient to pre- program the brake parameters necessary to calculate the braking curves. The only practical way to obtain the correct values for the current train composition is to include them into the data entry process by the driver. However, it cannot be expected from the driver to know deceleration values and brake build up times. Conversion models are therefore defined to convert the parameters entered by the driver (brake percentage and brake position) into the parameters of the corresponding brake model.

Clause 3.13.3.1.2 — Note: The process for defining the input parameters for the conversion model (brake

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.3.1.2, PDF p.101

3.13.3.1.2 Note: The process for defining the input parameters for the conversion model (brake percentage and brake position) is outside the scope of the ERTMS/ETCS specifications.

Clause 3.13.3.2 — Applicability of the conversion models

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.3.2, PDF p.101

3.13.3.2 Applicability of the conversion models

Clause 3.13.3.2.1 — The conversion models shall be used by the on-board equipment if the brake

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.3.2.1, PDF p.101

3.13.3.2.1 The conversion models shall be used by the on-board equipment if the brake percentage is acquired as part of Train Data, and if the maximum train speed, the brake percentage and the train length are all within the following validity limits of the conversion models: a) 0 ≤ V ≤ 200, where V is the maximum train speed in km/h b) 30 ≤ λ ≤ 250, where λ is the brake percentage in % c) 0 ≤ L ≤ Lmax, where L is the train length in m and where Lmax = 900 m if the brake position is “Passenger train in P” or Lmax = 1500 m if the brake position is “Freight train in P” or “Freight train in G”

Clause 3.13.4.2 — Train length compensation

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.4.2, PDF p.104

3.13.4.2 Train length compensation

Clause 3.13.4.2.1 — Assuming that a fictive train front end would be at any location between the current

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.4.2.1, PDF p.104

3.13.4.2.1 Assuming that a fictive train front end would be at any location between the current (actual) train front end location and the SvL, the acceleration due to the gradient shall be determined using the lowest (taking the sign into account) gradient value given by the gradient profile between the location of the fictive train front end and the location of the fictive train rear end (see Figure 35).

Clause 3.13.4.3.1 — The influence of gradients shall be compensated for the rotating mass of the train (see

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.4.3.1, PDF p.104

3.13.4.3.1 The influence of gradients shall be compensated for the rotating mass of the train (see Figure 35).

Clause 3.13.4.3.1.1 — Note: Since the rotating mass works like a flywheel (rotating inertia), the effect of

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.4.3.1.1, PDF p.104

3.13.4.3.1.1 Note: Since the rotating mass works like a flywheel (rotating inertia), the effect of the gradient is reduced. Assume for instance a (theoretical) train without any rotating mass, not braking, on a downhill gradient from height 1 to height 2. All the energy added to the train when it goes from H1 to H2 is converted into linear forward motion. This can be observed as an acceleration due to the gradient. Now assume the same train with part of the weight rotating. If this train travels the same distance from H1 to H2, the same amount of energy is added to the train. But now a part of that energy is converted into rotational motion and only the remaining part is converted into linear forward motion. The latter can be observed as an acceleration which is less than for the train without rotating mass.

Clause 3.13.4.3.1.2 — Note: For the influence of the rotating mass on the deceleration due to the brake, it

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.4.3.1.2, PDF p.104

3.13.4.3.1.2 Note: For the influence of the rotating mass on the deceleration due to the brake, it is already taken into account in the values for the brake parameters.

Clause 3.13.4.3.2 — The following formulas shall be used:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.4.3.2, PDF p.104

3.13.4.3.2 The following formulas shall be used: a) If M_rotating_nom is unknown:  Uphill: A_gradient = g * grad / (1000+10*M_rotating_max)  Downhill: A_gradient = g * grad / (1000+10*M_rotating_min) b) If M_rotating_nom is known:  Uphill: A_gradient = g * grad / (1000+10*M_rotating_nom)  Downhill: A_gradient = g * grad / (1000+10*M_rotating_nom) Legend: A_gradient = acceleration/deceleration due to gradient

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.104

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP g = 9.81 m/s2 - acceleration of gravity in m/s2 grad = gradient values in ‰ (positive = uphill) M_rotating_nom = nominal rotating mass (part of train data) as a percentage of the total train weight M_rotating_max = maximum possible rotating mass (see A.3.1) as a percentage of the total train weight M_rotating_min = minimum possible rotating mass (see A.3.1) as a percentage of the total train weight

Clause 3.13.5 — Determination of locations without special brake contribution and with

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.5, PDF p.105

3.13.5 Determination of locations without special brake contribution and with reduced adhesion conditions

Clause 3.13.5.1 — As long as it uses a track condition profile given by trackside, the on-board shall

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.5.1, PDF p.105

3.13.5.1 As long as it uses a track condition profile given by trackside, the on-board shall consider locations without special brake contribution over a distance going from the start location of the profile to the foot of the deceleration curve (EBD, SBD or GUI, see sections 3.13.8.3, 3.13.8.4 and 3.13.8.5).

Clause 3.13.5.2 — If the status of a special brake is “not active”, all locations shall be considered without

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.5.2, PDF p.105

3.13.5.2 If the status of a special brake is “not active”, all locations shall be considered without the contribution of this special brake.

Clause 3.13.5.2.1 — Note: in such case, a track condition profile implying the inhibition of this special brake

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.5.2.1, PDF p.105

3.13.5.2.1 Note: in such case, a track condition profile implying the inhibition of this special brake will have no effect.

Clause 3.13.5.3 — From the adhesion profile given by trackside, the on-board shall consider locations with

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.5.3, PDF p.105

3.13.5.3 From the adhesion profile given by trackside, the on-board shall consider locations with reduced adhesion conditions over a distance going from the start location of the profile to the location derived by adding the train length to the end location of the profile.

Clause 3.13.5.4 — When slippery rail is selected by the driver, all locations shall be considered with

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.5.4, PDF p.105

3.13.5.4 When slippery rail is selected by the driver, all locations shall be considered with reduced adhesion conditions.

Clause 3.13.5.5 — The speed and distance monitoring shall use, as resulting reduced adhesion

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.5.5, PDF p.105

3.13.5.5 The speed and distance monitoring shall use, as resulting reduced adhesion conditions, the most restrictive value of the adhesion conditions selected by the driver and the adhesion conditions calculated from the trackside profile.

Clause 3.13.6 — Calculation of the deceleration and brake build up time

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.6, PDF p.105

3.13.6 Calculation of the deceleration and brake build up time

Clause 3.13.6.1.1 — This chapter describes how the safe emergency brake, the expected and the normal

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.6.1.1, PDF p.105

3.13.6.1.1 This chapter describes how the safe emergency brake, the expected and the normal service brake decelerations and the time intervals due to brake build up time are calculated.

Clause 3.13.6.2.1 — Safe deceleration

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.6.2.1, PDF p.105

3.13.6.2.1 Safe deceleration

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.105

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.13.6.2.1.1 — The safe deceleration, A_safe(V,d), is safety relevant. This means that for the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.6.2.1.1, PDF p.106

3.13.6.2.1.1 The safe deceleration, A_safe(V,d), is safety relevant. This means that for the calculation of the safe deceleration, all necessary track and train characteristics shall be taken into account.

Clause 3.13.6.2.1.2 — The train and track related characteristics to be considered are:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.6.2.1.2, PDF p.106

3.13.6.2.1.2 The train and track related characteristics to be considered are: a) The speed dependent deceleration model(s) for the emergency brake either acquired as part of Train Data (see 3.13.2.2.3.1) or derived from the brake percentage using the conversion model (see 3.13.3.3) b) The acceleration/deceleration due to gradient i.e. A_gradient(d) (see 3.13.4) c) The locations with reduced adhesion conditions (see 3.13.5) d) The National Values for reduced adhesion condition (see 3.13.2.3.7.7) e) The locations without special brake contribution (see 3.13.5), only if the speed dependent deceleration model(s) for the emergency brake are acquired as part of Train Data f) The rolling stock correction factors Kdry_rst(V, EBCL) and Kwet_rst(V) (see 3.13.2.2.9.1), only if the speed dependent deceleration model(s) for the emergency brake are acquired as part of Train Data g) The National Values for confidence level on emergency brake safe deceleration and for the available wheel/rail adhesion (see 3.13.2.3.7.5 & 3.13.2.3.7.6), only if the speed dependent deceleration model(s) for the emergency brake are acquired as part of Train Data h) The integrated correction factors Kv_int(V) (with the two pivot deceleration values for passenger trains) and Kr_int(l) (see 3.13.2.3.7), only if the conversion model is used i) The brake position (see 3.13.2.2.4) j) The acquired train length L_TRAIN (see 3.13.2.2.11), only if the conversion model is used

Clause 3.13.6.2.1.3 — A_safe(V,d) shall be equal to:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.6.2.1.3, PDF p.106

3.13.6.2.1.3 A_safe(V,d) shall be equal to: For locations with normal adhesion conditions and for locations with reduced adhesion conditions when A_MAXREDADH does not limit to a maximum value the speed dependent deceleration for the emergency brake: A_safe(V,d) = A_brake_safe(V,d) + A_gradient(d) For locations with reduced adhesion conditions when A_MAXREDADH limits to a maximum value the speed dependent deceleration for the emergency brake: A_safe(V,d) = MIN(A_brake_safe(V,d), A_MAXREDADH) + A_gradient(d)

Clause 3.13.6.2.1.4 — A_brake_safe(V,d) shall be the safe emergency brake deceleration.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.6.2.1.4, PDF p.106

3.13.6.2.1.4 A_brake_safe(V,d) shall be the safe emergency brake deceleration. A_brake_safe(V,d) shall be equal to:

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.106

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP If the speed dependent deceleration model(s) for the emergency brake are acquired as part of Train Data: A_brake_safe(V,d) = Kdry_rst(V, M_NVEBCL) * (Kwet_rst(V) + M_NVAVADH *(1- Kwet_rst(V))) * A_brake_emergency(V,d) If the conversion model is used: A_brake_safe(V) = Kv_int(V) * Kr_int(L_TRAIN) * A_brake_emergency(V)

Clause 3.13.6.2.1.5 — A_brake_emergency(V,d) shall be the emergency brake deceleration as a function

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.6.2.1.5, PDF p.107

3.13.6.2.1.5 A_brake_emergency(V,d) shall be the emergency brake deceleration as a function of the speed, of the locations with change of special brake(s) contribution encountered between the train front and the foot of the EBD curve. A_brake_emergency(V,d) shall be equal to: A_brake_emergency (V) when d ≤ d ≤ d 1 estfront 1 A_brake_emergency (V) when d < d ≤ d 2 1 2 A_brake_emergency (V) when d < d ≤ d 3 2 3.... Where d,d,d,... arethelocations with change of special brake(s) contribution 1 2 3 A_brake_emergency (V) is equal to the emergency brake model, A_brake_emergency, x applicable for the concerned combination of brake. A_brake_emergency regenerative brake off Pneumatic brake plus regenerative brake Pneumatic brake only d EBD foot distance 1 Figure 36: Influence of track conditions on A_brake_emergency(V,d)

Clause 3.13.6.2.1.6 — A_MAXREDADH shall be the value, out of the three related National Values,

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.6.2.1.6, PDF p.107

3.13.6.2.1.6 A_MAXREDADH shall be the value, out of the three related National Values, applicable for this train according to: a) its brake position b) whether special/additional brakes independent from wheel/rail adhesion are active and it is allowed to take into account their contribution to the emergency braking effort.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.107

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.13.6.2.1.7 — Kdry_rst(V, M_NVEBCL) shall be the rolling stock correction factor, as a function of

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.6.2.1.7, PDF p.108

3.13.6.2.1.7 Kdry_rst(V, M_NVEBCL) shall be the rolling stock correction factor, as a function of speed (with speed steps identical with the ones of A_brake_emergency(V)), corresponding to the confidence level on emergency brake safe deceleration required by trackside (National Value).

Clause 3.13.6.2.1.8 — Kv_int(V) shall be the integrated correction factor applicable for the train, selected

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.6.2.1.8, PDF p.108

3.13.6.2.1.8 Kv_int(V) shall be the integrated correction factor applicable for the train, selected according to the brake position.

Clause 3.13.6.2.1.8.1 — If the brake position is “Passenger train in P”, the set of Kv_int shall be calculated

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.6.2.1.8.1, PDF p.108

3.13.6.2.1.8.1 If the brake position is “Passenger train in P”, the set of Kv_int shall be calculated as a function of the maximum emergency brake deceleration (A_ebmax) in the following way (see also figure 10): Kv_int_x = Kv_int_x_a when A_ebmax ≤ A_P12. Kv_int_x = Kv_int_x_b when A_ebmax ≥ A_P23. Kv_int_x = Kv_int_x_a + (A_ebmax - A_P12)/(A_P23 - A_P12) * (Kv_int_x_b - Kv_int_x_a) when A_P12 < A_ebmax < A_P23.” Kv_int Kv_int_0_a Kv_int_0_b Kv_int_1_a Kv_int_3_a Kv_int_2_a Kv_int_1_b Kv_int_2_b Kv_int_4_a Kv_int_3_b Kv_int_4_b A_P12 A_P23 A_ebmax Figure 37: Kv_int structure for conventional passenger trains

Clause 3.13.6.2.1.8.2 — The maximum EB deceleration A_ebmax shall be the maximum of

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.6.2.1.8.2, PDF p.108

3.13.6.2.1.8.2 The maximum EB deceleration A_ebmax shall be the maximum of A_brake_emergency between 0 km/h and the maximum speed of the train.

3.13.6.2.1.9 Note: Figure 38 gives an example of the influence of the various track/train characteristics on A_safe(V,d) and consequently on the EBD curve (see 3.13.8.3).

Clause 3.13.6.2.2 — Safe brake build up time

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.6.2.2, PDF p.109

3.13.6.2.2 Safe brake build up time

Clause 3.13.6.2.2.1 — The safe brake build up time, T_be, is safety relevant. This means that for the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.6.2.2.1, PDF p.109

3.13.6.2.2.1 The safe brake build up time, T_be, is safety relevant. This means that for the calculation of the safe brake build up time, all necessary track and train characteristics shall be taken into account.

Clause 3.13.6.2.2.2 — The train and track related characteristics to be considered are:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.6.2.2.2, PDF p.109

3.13.6.2.2.2 The train and track related characteristics to be considered are: a) The values of T_brake_emergency acquired as part of Train Data (see 3.13.2.2.3.2.8) or the value of T_brake_emergency derived from the conversion model (see 3.13.3.4) using the brake position and train length acquired as Train Data. b) The integrated correction factor Kt_int, only if the conversion model is used (see 3.13.2.3.7) c) The status of the regenerative brake, eddy current brake, magnetic shoe brake and Ep brake system (see 3.13.2.2.6), only if the values of T_brake_emergency are acquired as part of Train Data

Clause 3.13.6.2.2.3 — The safe brake build up time T_be shall be equal to:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.6.2.2.3, PDF p.109

3.13.6.2.2.3 The safe brake build up time T_be shall be equal to: If values of T_brake_emergency are acquired as part of Train Data: T_be = T_brake_emergency, with T_brake_emergency corresponding to the combination of special brakes currently in use If the conversion model is used: T_be = Kt_int * T_brake_emergency

Clause 3.13.6.3.1.1 — Since the expected deceleration is not safety relevant, no worst case conditions

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.6.3.1.1, PDF p.110

3.13.6.3.1.1 Since the expected deceleration is not safety relevant, no worst case conditions (e.g. correction factors, adhesion conditions) need to be taken into account for its calculation.

Clause 3.13.6.3.1.2 — The train and track related characteristics to be considered are:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.6.3.1.2, PDF p.110

Clause 3.13.6.3.1.3 — A_expected(V,d) shall be equal to:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.6.3.1.3, PDF p.110

3.13.6.3.1.3 A_expected(V,d) shall be equal to: A_expected(V,d) = A_brake_service(V,d) +A_gradient(d)

Clause 3.13.6.3.1.4 — A_brake_service(V,d) shall be the full deceleration of the service brake as a

A_brake_service(V,d) shall be equal to: A_brake_service (V) when d ≤ d ≤ d 1 estfront 1 A_brake_service (V) when d < d ≤ d 2 1 2 A_brake_service (V) when d < d ≤ d 3 2 3.... Where d,d,d,... arethelocations with change of special brake(s) contribution 1 2 3 A_brake_service (V) is equal to the full service brake model, A_brake_service, x applicable for the concerned combination of brake.

3.13.6.4 Normal service brake deceleration

Clause 3.13.6.4.1 — Since the normal service brake deceleration is not safety relevant, no worst case

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.6.4.1, PDF p.111

3.13.6.4.1 Since the normal service brake deceleration is not safety relevant, no worst case conditions (e.g. correction factors, adhesion conditions) need to be taken into account for its calculation.

Clause 3.13.6.4.2 — The train and track related characteristics to be considered are:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.6.4.2, PDF p.111

3.13.6.4.2 The train and track related characteristics to be considered are: a) The speed dependent deceleration model(s) for the full service brake either acquired as part of Train Data (see 3.13.2.2.3.1) or derived from the brake percentage using the conversion model (see 3.13.3.3) b) The speed dependent deceleration model(s) for the normal service brake acquired as part of Train Data (see 3.13.2.2.3.1) c) The acceleration/deceleration due to gradient i.e. A_gradient(d) (see 3.13.4) d) The brake position (see 3.13.2.2.4) e) The on-board correction factors Kn+(V) and Kn-(V) (see 3.13.2.2.9.2) f) The locations without special brake contribution (see 3.13.5)

Clause 3.13.6.4.3 — The normal service brake deceleration shall be equal to:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.6.4.3, PDF p.111

3.13.6.4.3 The normal service brake deceleration shall be equal to: For positive gradient values (uphill): A_normal_service(V,d) = A_brake_normal_service(V,d) + A_gradient(d) – Kn+(V)*grad/1000 For negative gradient values (downhill): A_normal_service(V,d) = A_brake_normal_service(V,d) + A_gradient(d) – Kn-(V)*grad/1000 Where grad = gradient values in ‰ (positive = uphill)

Clause 3.13.8 — Determination of targets and brake deceleration curves

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.8, PDF p.112

3.13.8 Determination of targets and brake deceleration curves

Clause 3.13.8.1.1 — A target is defined by a target location and a target speed, to which the train must

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.8.1.1, PDF p.112

3.13.8.1.1 A target is defined by a target location and a target speed, to which the train must decelerate before reaching the target location.

Clause 3.13.8.1.2 — For that purpose, the on-board equipment shall use brake deceleration curves related

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.8.1.2, PDF p.112

3.13.8.1.2 For that purpose, the on-board equipment shall use brake deceleration curves related to the supervised targets, from the deceleration values as specified in sections 3.13.6.2.1, 3.13.6.3.1and 3.13.6.4.

Clause 3.13.8.1.3 — These deceleration values being speed and distance dependent, a brake deceleration

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.8.1.3, PDF p.112

3.13.8.1.3 These deceleration values being speed and distance dependent, a brake deceleration curve shall be calculated piecewise, i.e. it shall be composed of interconnected arcs of

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.112

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP parabola, each one being based on one of the speed/distance dependent deceleration values (see Figure 38).

Clause 3.13.8.2 — Determination of the supervised targets

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.8.2, PDF p.113

3.13.8.2 Determination of the supervised targets

Clause 3.13.8.2.1 — The on-board shall continuously supervise a list of targets, which may include the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.8.2.1, PDF p.113

3.13.8.2.1 The on-board shall continuously supervise a list of targets, which may include the following types of target: a) the locations corresponding to a speed decrease of the MRSP (if any), which are in advance of the max safe front end of the train b) the Limit of Authority (LOA) c) the End of Authority (EOA) and the Supervised Location (SvL) d) the location deduced from the maximum permitted distance to run in Staff Responsible, with a target speed zero

Clause 3.13.8.2.1.1 — Note: depending on the information received from trackside and the position of the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.8.2.1.1, PDF p.113

3.13.8.2.1.1 Note: depending on the information received from trackside and the position of the train, the list of supervised targets may be empty.

Clause 3.13.8.2.2 — The list of supervised targets shall be re-evaluated when any of the elements it is built

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.8.2.2, PDF p.113

3.13.8.2.2 The list of supervised targets shall be re-evaluated when any of the elements it is built of is changed (e.g. new MA and/or track description accepted on-board, EOA and/or SvL temporarily supervised at the start location of a mode profile, update of stored information in specific situations (see sections A.3.4 and 4.10)).

Clause 3.13.8.2.3 — A target corresponding to a speed decrease of the MRSP shall be removed from the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.8.2.3, PDF p.113

3.13.8.2.3 A target corresponding to a speed decrease of the MRSP shall be removed from the list of supervised targets when the max safe front end of the train has passed the target location.

Clause 3.13.8.3 — Emergency Brake Deceleration curves (EBD)

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.8.3, PDF p.113

3.13.8.3 Emergency Brake Deceleration curves (EBD)

Clause 3.13.8.3.1 — If a target belongs to the MRSP or is an LOA, the on-board shall calculate an EBD

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.8.3.1, PDF p.113

3.13.8.3.1 If a target belongs to the MRSP or is an LOA, the on-board shall calculate an EBD curve based on the safe deceleration A_safe(V,d), that crosses the ceiling speed EBI supervision limit (see 3.13.9.2) at the target location, and that extends up to the location where the target speed is reached (EBD foot).

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.113

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP Speed EBD reference EBD curve EBI Target speed Ceiling speed limits (from MRSP or LoA) Permitted target location EBD foot Distance Figure 40: Calculation of the EBD curve with regards to MRSP or LOA target

Clause 3.13.8.3.2 — If a target is an SvL, the on-board shall calculate an Emergency Brake Deceleration

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.8.3.2, PDF p.114

3.13.8.3.2 If a target is an SvL, the on-board shall calculate an Emergency Brake Deceleration (EBD) curve based on the safe deceleration A_safe(V,d) and that reaches zero speed at the SvL.

Clause 3.13.8.3.3 — If a target is the location at the end of the maximum permitted distance to run in Staff

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.8.3.3, PDF p.114

3.13.8.3.3 If a target is the location at the end of the maximum permitted distance to run in Staff Responsible, the on-board shall calculate an Emergency Brake Deceleration (EBD) curve based on the safe deceleration A_safe(V,d) and that reaches zero speed at this staff responsible end location. Speed EBD curve EBD EBD = reference foot SvL or Location from max SR distance Distance Figure 41: Calculation of the EBD curve with regards to SvL or SR distance

Clause 3.13.8.4 — Service Brake Deceleration curves (SBD)

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.8.4, PDF p.114

3.13.8.4 Service Brake Deceleration curves (SBD)

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.114

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.13.8.4.1 — If a target is an EOA, the on-board shall calculate an Service Brake Deceleration

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.8.4.1, PDF p.115

3.13.8.4.1 If a target is an EOA, the on-board shall calculate an Service Brake Deceleration (SBD) curve based on the expected deceleration A_expected(V,d) and that reaches zero speed at this EOA location. Speed SBD curve SBD SBD = reference foot EOA Distance Figure 42: Calculation of the SBD curve with regards to EOA

Clause 3.13.8.5 — Guidance curves (GUI)

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.8.5, PDF p.115

3.13.8.5 Guidance curves (GUI)

Clause 3.13.8.5.1 — The purpose of the guidance curve (GUI) is to provide a comfortable way of braking for

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.8.5.1, PDF p.115

3.13.8.5.1 The purpose of the guidance curve (GUI) is to provide a comfortable way of braking for the driver, to avoid excessive wear of the brakes and to save traction energy.

Clause 3.13.8.5.2 — If the National Value does not inhibit them, the on-board shall calculate a guidance

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.8.5.2, PDF p.115

3.13.8.5.2 If the National Value does not inhibit them, the on-board shall calculate a guidance curve (GUI) for each supervised target, based on the normal service brake deceleration A_normal_service(V,d). The foot of a GUI curve (i.e. the location where the GUI speed is equal to the target speed) shall be: a) the target location, in case of EOA/SvL b) the location defined in 3.13.9.3.5.9, for others targets

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9, PDF p.115

3.13.9 Supervision limits

Clause 3.13.9.1.1 — In this chapter the following supervision limits are defined:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.1.1, PDF p.115

3.13.9.1.1 In this chapter the following supervision limits are defined:  Emergency brake intervention (EBI)  Service brake intervention (SBI)  Warning (W)  Permitted speed (P)  Indication (I)

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.115

Clause 3.13.9.1.2 — The purpose of the emergency brake intervention supervision limit is to assure that the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.1.2, PDF p.116

3.13.9.1.2 The purpose of the emergency brake intervention supervision limit is to assure that the train will remain within the various limits (in distance/speed) imposed by the trackside.

Clause 3.13.9.1.3 — The purpose of all other supervision limits is to assist the driver in preventing an

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.1.3, PDF p.116

3.13.9.1.3 The purpose of all other supervision limits is to assist the driver in preventing an emergency brake intervention by maintaining the speed of the train within the appropriate limits.

Clause 3.13.9.3.2.2 — The time elapsed between the Emergency brake intervention and the full application

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.3.2.2, PDF p.119

3.13.9.3.2.2 The time elapsed between the Emergency brake intervention and the full application of the braking effort is reached (EBD) shall be split into two parts: a) Time during which the traction effort is still present: T_traction b) Remaining time during which the traction effort is not present: T_berem

Clause 3.13.9.3.2.3 — The traction time (T_Traction) shall be defined as follows:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.3.2.3, PDF p.119

3.13.9.3.2.3 The traction time (T_Traction) shall be defined as follows: a) when the traction cut-off is implemented: T_traction = MAX((T_traction_cut_off - (T_warning + T_bs2)); 0). b) when the traction cut-off is not implemented: T_traction = T_traction_cut_off

Clause 3.13.9.3.2.4 — Note: When the traction cut-off is implemented, the traction cut-off command is

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.3.2.4, PDF p.119

3.13.9.3.2.4 Note: When the traction cut-off is implemented, the traction cut-off command is triggered when passing the warning limit. The term (T_warning + T_bs2) in the equation above takes this into account, assuming that the warning limit is derived from the EBD.

Clause 3.13.9.3.2.5 — T_bs2 and T_warning are defined in sections 3.13.9.3.3 and 3.13.9.3.4.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.3.2.5, PDF p.119

3.13.9.3.2.5 T_bs2 and T_warning are defined in sections 3.13.9.3.3 and 3.13.9.3.4.

Clause 3.13.9.3.2.6 — The remaining time with no traction (T_berem) shall be equal to MAX(T_be -

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.3.2.6, PDF p.119

3.13.9.3.2.6 The remaining time with no traction (T_berem) shall be equal to MAX(T_be - T_traction; 0).

Clause 3.13.9.3.2.7 — Note: T_Traction exceeding T_be is rather a theoretical case, but is nevertheless

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.3.2.7, PDF p.119

3.13.9.3.2.7 Note: T_Traction exceeding T_be is rather a theoretical case, but is nevertheless included to make the specifications complete.

Clause 3.13.9.3.2.8 — During T_traction, the estimated acceleration (A_est1) shall be the one measured at

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.3.2.8, PDF p.119

3.13.9.3.2.8 During T_traction, the estimated acceleration (A_est1) shall be the one measured at the moment when the calculation is made, but limited to positive or null values.

Clause 3.13.9.3.2.9 — If T_be > T_traction, the estimated acceleration during T_berem (A_est2) shall be

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.3.2.9, PDF p.119

3.13.9.3.2.9 If T_be > T_traction, the estimated acceleration during T_berem (A_est2) shall be the one measured at the moment when the calculation is made, but limited to values between 0 and +0.4m/s2.

Clause 3.13.9.3.3.2 — For an EBD based target, the on-board shall calculate the location of the SBI

Clause 3.13.9.3.3.3 — If the service brake command is available for use and the service brake feedback is

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.3.3.3, PDF p.120

3.13.9.3.3.3 If the service brake command is available for use and the service brake feedback is not available for use, T_bs1 and T_bs2 shall be equal to T_bs.

Clause 3.13.9.3.3.4 — If both the service brake command and the service brake feedback are available for

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.3.3.4, PDF p.120

3.13.9.3.3.4 If both the service brake command and the service brake feedback are available for use, T_bs1 and T_bs2 shall by default be set to T_bs. When the service brake is used by the driver in target speed monitoring or release speed monitoring, they shall be reduced and possibly locked to the respective fixed values of 0s and T_bs2_locked, until the ceiling speed monitoring is entered; they are then reset to T_bs (refer to detailed algorithm in Appendix A.3.10).

Clause 3.13.9.3.3.4.1 — In case T_bs < T_bs2_locked then T_bs2 shall be equal to T_bs2_locked.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.3.3.4.1, PDF p.120

3.13.9.3.3.4.1 In case T_bs < T_bs2_locked then T_bs2 shall be equal to T_bs2_locked.

Clause 3.13.9.3.3.5 — If the service brake command is not available for use, T_bs1 and T_bs2 shall be set

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.3.3.5, PDF p.120

3.13.9.3.3.5 If the service brake command is not available for use, T_bs1 and T_bs2 shall be set to zero.

Clause 3.13.9.3.3.6 — Note: The values T_bs1 and T_bs2 = 0s are defined to achieve the maximum

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.3.3.6, PDF p.120

3.13.9.3.3.6 Note: The values T_bs1 and T_bs2 = 0s are defined to achieve the maximum performance when service brake command is not used.

Clause 3.13.9.3.3.8.1 — Note: the re-use of the same distance travelled and speed increase between the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.3.3.8.1, PDF p.122

3.13.9.3.3.8.1 Note: the re-use of the same distance travelled and speed increase between the SBI2 supervision limit and the EBD, as for the estimated speed (see Figure 48), leads to an overestimation/underestimation of the SBI2 speed to be displayed to the driver. This simplification, which avoids the need of an iterated calculation, is however acceptable and necessary since the error made tends to zero when the train reaches the SBI2 supervision limit.

Clause 3.13.9.3.5 — Permitted speed supervision limit (P)

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.3.5, PDF p.122

3.13.9.3.5 Permitted speed supervision limit (P)

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Clause 3.13.9.3.5.3 — Note: The reference for the Permitted speed supervision limit is the SBI supervision

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.3.5.3, PDF p.123

3.13.9.3.5.3 Note: The reference for the Permitted speed supervision limit is the SBI supervision limit and not the Warning supervision limit. As a result the permitted and warning supervision limits are clearly separated and do not affect each other. In this way it is clear that the warning is not part of the critical performance interval.

Clause 3.13.9.3.5.7.1 — Note: the re-use of the same distance travelled and speed increase between the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.3.5.7.1, PDF p.124

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.126

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.13.9.3.6.2 — If the service brake feedback interface is not available for use, then T_indication

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.3.6.2, PDF p.127

3.13.9.3.6.2 If the service brake feedback interface is not available for use, then T_indication shall be calculated as follows:    T  max 0.8T,5s  T indication bs driver

Clause 3.13.9.3.6.3 — Note: The reduction of T_indication by a factor is intended to improve performance

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.3.6.3, PDF p.127

3.13.9.3.6.3 Note: The reduction of T_indication by a factor is intended to improve performance and the feasibility of this reduction is based on experience with real implementations. To avoid very low values when T_bs is small, a minimum is defined for T_indication, giving the driver always enough time to operate the brake.

Clause 3.13.9.3.6.4 — If the service brake feedback interface is available for use then T_indication shall be

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.3.6.4, PDF p.127

3.13.9.3.6.4 If the service brake feedback interface is available for use then T_indication shall be equal to 5s + T_driver.

Clause 3.13.9.4 — Release speed supervision limits

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.4, PDF p.127

3.13.9.4 Release speed supervision limits

Clause 3.13.9.4.1 — The release speed is a special ceiling speed limit, applicable in the vicinity of the EOA.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.4.1, PDF p.127

3.13.9.4.1 The release speed is a special ceiling speed limit, applicable in the vicinity of the EOA. The EBI supervision limit is equal to the release speed. There is no SBI, W, P, I supervision limit associated to the release speed.

Clause 3.13.9.4.2 — Note: The release speed may be necessary for two reasons. One is that a train has to

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.4.2, PDF p.127

3.13.9.4.2 Note: The release speed may be necessary for two reasons. One is that a train has to be able to approach the EOA where the permitted speed reaches zero and might be too restrictive to permit acceptable driving due to inaccuracy of the measured distance. The other reason is that in a level 1 application the train has to be able to overpass the balise when the signal clears. For these two reasons a (low) release speed may be given from trackside or may be calculated on board, based on the distance from the EOA to the Supervised Location.

Clause 3.13.9.4.3 — With each MA, it shall be possible for the trackside to:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.4.3, PDF p.127

3.13.9.4.3 With each MA, it shall be possible for the trackside to: a) Give the value of the release speed directly to the on-board, OR b) Instruct the on-board to calculate the release speed, OR c) Instruct the on-board to use the national value.

Clause 3.13.9.4.4 — In case the MA does not identify the variant to be used or in case of LOA, no release

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.4.4, PDF p.127

3.13.9.4.4 In case the MA does not identify the variant to be used or in case of LOA, no release speed shall be supervised.

Clause 3.13.9.4.5 — Note: When the release speed is given as a fixed value from trackside, the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.4.5, PDF p.127

3.13.9.4.5 Note: When the release speed is given as a fixed value from trackside, the ERTMS/ETCS system cannot be responsible for stopping the train in rear of the Supervised Location. In this case, it is the full responsibility of the infrastructure manager to set the appropriate release speed with regard to the risk of passing the Supervised Location.

Clause 3.13.9.4.6 — The start location of the release speed monitoring (i.e. where the EBI supervision limit

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.4.6, PDF p.127

3.13.9.4.6 The start location of the release speed monitoring (i.e. where the EBI supervision limit related to EBD is replaced with an EBI supervision limit equal to the release speed value) shall be the location of the most restrictive SBI supervision limit among the SBI1 related to EOA, the SBI2 related to SvL and, when the Release Speed is calculated on-board, the SBI2 supervision limit(s) related to other target(s), if any, between the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.127

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP Trip location related to the EOA and the SvL, calculated for the Release Speed value, taking into account the following assumptions: a) the estimated acceleration shall be set to “zero” b) if not inhibited by National Value, the compensation of the inaccuracy of the speed measurement shall be set to a value calculated from the release speed, as defined in SUBSET-041 § 5.3.1.2: V_delta0rs = f (V_release) 41

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.4.8.2, PDF p.130

3.13.9.4.8.2 The on-board equipment shall seek for each target referred to in clause 3.13.9.4.8, a release speed value which satisfies the two following inequalities:      ABS V  V d  D V 1km/ h release EBD tripEoA bec delta0rsob d  D  d (V ) tripEoA bec EBD target     V  max f V,V V  0 With or (if compensation of speed delta0rsob 41 release ura delta0rsob inaccuracy is inhibited by National Value)     D  V V  T  T With bec release delta0rsob traction berem With d  d  L tripEoA EoA antenna front      max 2Q 10m 10%  d, d  d locacc_refBG EoA max safefront minsafefront 1 And with if level = 1  0 if level = 2 or 3 If no speed value higher than V_target fulfils the above inequalities, then: V V release target

Clause 3.13.9.4.8.2.1 — Note: The above formulas are intended to prevent the calculated release speed

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.4.8.2.1, PDF p.130

3.13.9.4.8.2.1 Note: The above formulas are intended to prevent the calculated release speed from fluctuating, according to the distance, speed and acceleration measurements. It allows calculating the release speed only once, for a given on-board reference location, unless  the distance confidence interval exceeds a predicted one, which is based on the assumption that the whole distance between the current on-board reference location and the EOA would be travelled with SUBSET-041 odometer performance values and without any update of the on-board reference location, or  the speed under reading amount (V_ura) exceeds the SUBSET-041 performance value Whenever the on-board reference location is updated (e.g. new LRBG), the release speed will however be recalculated and will increase with a step. This behaviour is acceptable from an operational point of view.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.9.4.8.2.2, PDF p.131

3.13.9.4.8.2.2 Note: The method chosen (e.g. iterative algorithm) to compute the release speed is an implementation issue.

Clause 3.13.10 — Speed and distance monitoring commands

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10, PDF p.131

3.13.10 Speed and distance monitoring commands

Clause 3.13.10.1.1 — By comparing the train speed and position to the various supervision limits defined in

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.1.1, PDF p.131

3.13.10.1.1 By comparing the train speed and position to the various supervision limits defined in the previous section, the on-board equipment generates braking commands, traction cut-off commands and relevant information to the driver. The information displayed to the driver is selected according to the following supervision statuses of the speed and distance monitoring function: Normal status, Indication status, Overspeed status, Warning status and Intervention status.

Clause 3.13.10.1.2 — The following types of speed and distance monitoring are defined:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.1.2, PDF p.131

3.13.10.1.2 The following types of speed and distance monitoring are defined:  Ceiling speed monitoring (CSM)  Target speed monitoring (TSM)

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.131

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP  Release speed monitoring (RSM) Speed I MRSP release speed monitoring SBI ceiling speed monitoring release speed Distance Start EOA SvL RSM Figure 53: Different types of speed and distance monitoring

Clause 3.13.10.1.3 — Ceiling speed monitoring is the speed supervision in the area where the train can run

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.1.3, PDF p.132

3.13.10.1.3 Ceiling speed monitoring is the speed supervision in the area where the train can run without the need to brake to a target.

Clause 3.13.10.1.4 — Target speed monitoring is the speed and distance supervision in the area where the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.1.4, PDF p.132

3.13.10.1.4 Target speed monitoring is the speed and distance supervision in the area where the specific information related to a target is displayed to the driver and within which the train brakes to a target.

Clause 3.13.10.1.5 — Release speed monitoring is the speed and distance supervision in the area close to

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.1.5, PDF p.132

3.13.10.1.5 Release speed monitoring is the speed and distance supervision in the area close to the EOA where the train is allowed to run with release speed to approach the EOA.

Clause 3.13.10.2 — General requirements

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.2, PDF p.132

3.13.10.2 General requirements

Clause 3.13.10.2.1 — The train speed indicated to the driver shall be identical to the speed used for the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.2.1, PDF p.132

3.13.10.2.1 The train speed indicated to the driver shall be identical to the speed used for the speed monitoring. This shall be the estimated speed.

Clause 3.13.10.2.2 — Once a Train Interface command (traction cut-off, service brake or emergency brake)

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.2.2, PDF p.132

3.13.10.2.2 Once a Train Interface command (traction cut-off, service brake or emergency brake) is triggered, the on-board shall apply it until its corresponding revocation condition is met.

Clause 3.13.10.2.3 — If there is no on-board interface with the service brake or if the use of the service brake

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.2.3, PDF p.132

3.13.10.2.3 If there is no on-board interface with the service brake or if the use of the service brake command is not allowed by a National Value (only in Target speed monitoring), whenever a service brake command is specified, the emergency brake command shall be triggered instead.

Clause 3.13.10.2.4 — The emergency brake command, which is triggered instead of the service brake

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.2.4, PDF p.132

3.13.10.2.4 The emergency brake command, which is triggered instead of the service brake command when an SBI supervision limit is exceeded, shall be revoked according to the requirements specified for the revocation of service brake command, unless the

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.132

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP emergency brake command has been also triggered due to an EBI supervision limit. In such case, the condition for revoking the emergency brake command due to EBI supervision limit shall prevail.

Clause 3.13.10.2.5 — The on-board shall revoke the Intervention status only when no brake command is

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.2.5, PDF p.133

3.13.10.2.5 The on-board shall revoke the Intervention status only when no brake command is applied by the speed and distance monitoring function.

Clause 3.13.10.2.6 — In level 2/3: Train trip shall be initiated if the on-board equipment detects that the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.2.6, PDF p.133

3.13.10.2.6 In level 2/3: Train trip shall be initiated if the on-board equipment detects that the minimum safe front end has passed the EOA/LOA location.

Clause 3.13.10.2.7 — In Level 1: Train Trip shall be initiated if the on-board equipment detects that the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.2.7, PDF p.133

3.13.10.2.7 In Level 1: Train Trip shall be initiated if the on-board equipment detects that the minimum safe antenna position (calculated by subtracting distance between active Eurobalise antenna and the front end of the train from the min safe front end position) has passed the EOA/LOA location.

Clause 3.13.10.2.8 — In the following sections, the term V_MRSP (together with the derived ceiling

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.2.8, PDF p.133

3.13.10.2.8 In the following sections, the term V_MRSP (together with the derived ceiling supervision limits) refers to the lowest MRSP element encountered between the min safe front end and the max safe front end of the train.

Clause 3.13.10.3 — Requirements for Ceiling speed monitoring

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.3, PDF p.133

3.13.10.3 Requirements for Ceiling speed monitoring

Clause 3.13.10.3.1 — The on-board equipment shall display the Permitted speed ceiling supervision limit.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.3.1, PDF p.133

3.13.10.3.1 The on-board equipment shall display the Permitted speed ceiling supervision limit.

Clause 3.13.10.3.2 — When the supervision status is Overspeed, Warning or Intervention, the on-board

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.3.2, PDF p.133

3.13.10.3.2 When the supervision status is Overspeed, Warning or Intervention, the on-board equipment shall display the SBI speed ceiling supervision limit.

Clause 3.13.10.3.7 — In ceiling speed monitoring, only the ceiling supervision limits (described in section

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.3.7, PDF p.135

3.13.10.3.7 In ceiling speed monitoring, only the ceiling supervision limits (described in section 3.13.9.2) are used to determine the commands to the Train Interface and the supervision statuses displayed to the driver. However the braking to target supervision limits and the release speed supervision limits (described in sections 3.13.9.3 and 3.13.9.4) are also used to determine the locations where the transition to target speed monitoring and to release speed monitoring occur respectively.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.135

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall not display any first Indication location information.

Clause 3.13.10.3.9 — If A_MAXREDADH (see 3.13.6.2.1.6) requests the target information as

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.3.9, PDF p.136

3.13.10.3.9 If A_MAXREDADH (see 3.13.6.2.1.6) requests the target information as supplementary DMI information, the on-board equipment shall display to the driver the target information (target speed and distance to target) related to one target at a time: the Most Relevant Displayed Target (MRDT). The MRDT shall be selected amongst the supervised targets as the one whose remaining distance to its Indication supervision limit (as specified in 3.13.10.3.8 and 3.13.10.3.8.1) is the shortest. The indicated distance to the target shall be computed in the same way as for target speed monitoring, i.e. clauses 3.13.10.4.7, 3.13.10.4.8 and 3.13.10.4.8.1 shall apply.

Clause 3.13.10.3.9.1 — Exception: In case the list of supervised targets is empty or in case of LOA with no

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.3.9.1, PDF p.136

3.13.10.3.9.1 Exception: In case the list of supervised targets is empty or in case of LOA with no EBD based target whose speed value is below or equal to the estimated speed, the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall not display any target information.

Clause 3.13.10.3.10 — If A_MAXREDADH (see 3.13.6.2.1.6) requests a time to Indication as

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.3.10, PDF p.136

3.13.10.3.10 If A_MAXREDADH (see 3.13.6.2.1.6) requests a time to Indication as supplementary DMI information, the on-board equipment shall compute the Time to Indication (TTI) as the time to travel at the estimated speed the remaining distance to the first Indication location (as specified in 3.13.10.3.8 and 3.13.10.3.8.1). The on- board equipment shall inform the driver as long as this time is shorter than a fixed value (refer to A.3.1).

Clause 3.13.10.3.10.1 — Exception: In case the list of supervised targets is empty or in case of LOA with no

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.3.10.1, PDF p.136

3.13.10.3.10.1 Exception: In case the list of supervised targets is empty or in case of LOA with no EBD based target whose speed value is below or equal to the estimated speed, the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall not compute any time to Indication.

Clause 3.13.10.4 — Requirements for Target speed monitoring

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.4, PDF p.136

3.13.10.4 Requirements for Target speed monitoring

Clause 3.13.10.4.1 — In target speed monitoring, both the ceiling supervision limits and the braking to target

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.4.1, PDF p.136

3.13.10.4.1 In target speed monitoring, both the ceiling supervision limits and the braking to target supervision limits, described in sections 3.13.9.2 and 3.13.9.3, are used to determine the commands to the Train Interface and the supervision statuses displayed to the driver.

Clause 3.13.10.4.2 — The on-board equipment shall display to the driver the target information (target speed

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.4.2, PDF p.136

3.13.10.4.2 The on-board equipment shall display to the driver the target information (target speed and distance to target) related to one target at a time: the Most Relevant Displayed Target (MRDT). Clause 3.13.10.4.2.1 — Note 1: the above process for the determination of the MRDT ensures that in all

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.4.2.1, PDF p.137

3.13.10.4.2.1 Note 1: the above process for the determination of the MRDT ensures that in all circumstances (especially when several targets are close to each other) it is avoided that a target is displayed after its Indication supervision limit has been reached.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.137

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.13.10.4.2.2 — Note 2: when entering target speed monitoring, there is by definition at least one

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.4.2.2, PDF p.138

3.13.10.4.2.2 Note 2: when entering target speed monitoring, there is by definition at least one target that satisfies the conditions to be selected as MRDT. Afterwards (e.g. due to train braking) it is possible that no target satisfies any more the conditions to be selected as MRDT, which however only means that the target previously selected remains the MRDT (see clause 3.13.10.4.5).

Clause 3.13.10.4.2.3 — Note 3: if MRDT is a target at zero speed (e.g. EOA or SvL), then it is always

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.4.2.3, PDF p.138

3.13.10.4.2.3 Note 3: if MRDT is a target at zero speed (e.g. EOA or SvL), then it is always n-1 selected as MRDT, i.e. no other target can be selected as MRDT as per step n.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.138

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.13.10.4.8.1 — As long as the displayed values are locked due to SB feedback (see Appendix

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.4.8.1, PDF p.139

3.13.10.4.8.1 As long as the displayed values are locked due to SB feedback (see Appendix A.3.10 for details), the on-board equipment shall ensure that the displayed Permitted speed, the displayed SBI speed (if any) and the distance to target never increase (e.g. due to the reduction of T_bs1 and T_bs2 or e.g. due to relocation). In other terms if a concerned displayed value (VP_DMI, VSBI_DMI or target distance) calculated as above has a higher value than the previously displayed value, then the previous value shall remain displayed until a further calculated value is lower than the displayed one.

Clause 3.13.10.4.9 — The on-board shall consider the service brake command as available for use unless:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.4.9, PDF p.139

3.13.10.4.9 The on-board shall consider the service brake command as available for use unless: a) The service brake command is not implemented, OR b) The national value inhibits its use.

Clause 3.13.10.4.10 — The on-board equipment shall compare the estimated speed and train position

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.4.10, PDF p.139

3.13.10.4.10 The on-board equipment shall compare the estimated speed and train position with the ceiling and braking to target supervision limits and shall trigger/revoke commands to the train interface (traction cut-off if implemented, service brake if available for use or emergency brake) and supervision statuses, by evaluating and taking into account the conditions as specified in clause 3.13.10.4.10.1.

Clause 3.13.10.4.10.1 — The conditions in Table 8 and Table 10 shall be evaluated for each target related to

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.4.10.1, PDF p.139

3.13.10.4.10.1 The conditions in Table 8 and Table 10 shall be evaluated for each target related to an MRSP speed decrease or LOA, the conditions in Table 9 and Table 11 shall be evaluated for the targets EOA and SvL and for the end of the maximum permitted distance to run in Staff Responsible.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.4.14, PDF p.146

3.13.10.4.14 A TI command shall be triggered if a corresponding triggering condition is met for at least one target. On the other hand it shall be revoked only if a corresponding revocation condition is met for each supervised target.

Clause 3.13.10.4.16 — When the speed and distance monitoring function becomes active and the target

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.4.16, PDF p.147

3.13.10.4.16 When the speed and distance monitoring function becomes active and the target speed monitoring is the first one entered, the triggering condition t3 defined in Table 8 or Table 9 shall be checked for each target in order to determine whether the Indication status applies. If it is not the case, the on-board shall immediately set the supervision status to the relevant value, applying a transition from the Indication status according to clause 3.13.10.4.15.

Clause 3.13.10.4.17 — The Normal status is not used in target speed monitoring. However, in case the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.4.17, PDF p.147

3.13.10.4.17 The Normal status is not used in target speed monitoring. However, in case the target speed monitoring is entered and the supervision status was previously set to Normal, the on-board equipment shall immediately execute one of the transitions from the Normal status, as specified in clause 3.13.10.4.15.

Clause 3.13.10.4.18 — Note: Depending upon train speed/position it is possible that for some target(s)

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.4.18, PDF p.147

3.13.10.4.18 Note: Depending upon train speed/position it is possible that for some target(s) none of the triggering conditions specified in table 8 and 9 is met. However the conditions to enter the target speed monitoring are such (see condition [1] in table 16) that the clauses 3.13.10.4.16 and 3.13.10.4.17 always allow determining a supervision status.

Clause 3.13.10.6.2 — If a transition of speed and distance monitoring occurs while a brake command is

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.6.2, PDF p.149

3.13.10.6.2 If a transition of speed and distance monitoring occurs while a brake command is already applied, the concerned command shall be maintained until the revocation condition, if specified for the newly entered speed and distance monitoring, is fulfilled.

Clause 3.13.10.6.2.1 — Note: This means that when the service brake is commanded in ceiling speed

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.6.2.1, PDF p.149

3.13.10.6.2.1 Note: This means that when the service brake is commanded in ceiling speed monitoring while it is not available in target speed monitoring, the service brake remains commanded when the on-board switches to target speed monitoring and is only revoked when the Permitted speed supervision limit is no longer exceeded.

3.13.10.6.3 If a transition from target speed monitoring to ceiling speed or release speed monitoring occurs while a traction cut-off command is already applied, the traction cut- off command shall be immediately revoked.

Clause 3.13.10.6.4 — If a transition from target speed monitoring to release speed monitoring occurs while a

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.10.6.4, PDF p.149

3.13.10.6.4 If a transition from target speed monitoring to release speed monitoring occurs while a service brake command is already applied, the service brake command shall be immediately revoked.
