Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.11.1, PDF p.150

3.13.11.1 The purpose of the perturbation location is to trigger the MA request to the RBC in order to renew the Movement Authority in due time before the train would have to brake to an EOA/SvL or LOA target.

Clause 3.13.11.3 — Starting from the first element of the MRSP (i.e. from the start location of the on-board

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.11.3, PDF p.150

T_tractionind and T_beremind shall be defined as in 3.13.9.3.2 for T_traction and T_berem by substituting T_bs2 with T_bs2ind

Clause 3.13.11.9 — If, in exceptional situation (e.g. after a shortening of MA), the EBD, SBD or GUI speed

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.11.9, PDF p.152

Clause 3.13.11.10 — Note: For trackside engineering reasons, the assumptions for the calculation of the EBI

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.13.11.10, PDF p.152

3.13.11.10 Note: For trackside engineering reasons, the assumptions for the calculation of the EBI supervision limit are necessary to obtain a fully predictable perturbation location, i.e. independent from the measured acceleration and speed confidence interval.

Clause 3.14 — Brake Command Handling and Protection against Undesirable

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14, PDF p.152

3.14 Brake Command Handling and Protection against Undesirable Train Movement

Clause 3.14.1 — Brake Command Handling

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.1, PDF p.152

3.14.1 Brake Command Handling

Clause 3.14.1.1 — Note: Whenever the type of brake used is not specified explicitly in the text, it shall be

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.1.1, PDF p.152

3.14.1.1 Note: Whenever the type of brake used is not specified explicitly in the text, it shall be interpreted as not being important for technical interoperability and being a property of the specific implementation.

Clause 3.14.1.2 — In case only the application of (the non-vital) service brake has been commanded and

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.1.2, PDF p.152

3.14.1.2 In case only the application of (the non-vital) service brake has been commanded and the service brake fails to be applied, the emergency brake command shall be given.

Clause 3.14.1.3 — If the emergency brake command was triggered due to a trip condition (see chapter 4)

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.1.3, PDF p.152

3.14.1.3 If the emergency brake command was triggered due to a trip condition (see chapter 4) the emergency brake command shall be released at standstill and after driver acknowledgement of the trip condition.

Clause 3.14.1.4 — For handling of brake commands resulting from the speed and distance monitoring,

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.1.4, PDF p.152

3.14.1.4 For handling of brake commands resulting from the speed and distance monitoring, refer to section 3.13.10.

Clause 3.14.1.5 — If the brake command was triggered due to roll away protection, reverse movement

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.1.5, PDF p.152

3.14.1.5 If the brake command was triggered due to roll away protection, reverse movement protection or standstill supervision the brake command shall be released at standstill and after driver acknowledgement.

Clause 3.14.1.6 — If the brake command was triggered due to linking error, balise group message

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.1.6, PDF p.152

3.14.1.6 If the brake command was triggered due to linking error, balise group message inconsistency or RAMS related supervision error, the brake command shall be released at standstill.

Clause 3.14.1.7 — If the brake command was triggered due to supervision of the safe radio connection

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.1.7, PDF p.152

3.14.1.7 If the brake command was triggered due to supervision of the safe radio connection (T_NVCONTACT) the brake command shall be released at standstill or if a new consistent message has been received from the RBC.

Clause 3.14.1.7.1 — If the brake command was triggered due to an overpassed reversing distance related

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.1.7.1, PDF p.152

3.14.1.7.1 If the brake command was triggered due to an overpassed reversing distance related to a reversing area or due to any further movement in the direction opposite to the train

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.152

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP orientation while the reversing distance is still overpassed, the brake command shall be released if the reversing distance becomes extended so that the reversing distance is no longer overpassed, or at standstill after driver acknowledgement.

Clause 3.14.1.7.2 — If the brake command was triggered due to change of Train Data while running (see

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.1.7.2, PDF p.153

3.14.1.7.2 If the brake command was triggered due to change of Train Data while running (see section 5.17 procedure "Changing Train Data from sources different from the driver”), the brake command shall be released at standstill and after driver acknowledgement.

Clause 3.14.1.7.3 — If the brake command was triggered due to the detection of a train movement while

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.1.7.3, PDF p.153

3.14.1.7.3 If the brake command was triggered due to the detection of a train movement while modifying/revalidating train data or while entering SR speed/distance limits, the brake command shall be released at standstill and after driver acknowledgement..

Clause 3.14.1.7.4 — If the brake command was triggered due to an overpassed distance allowed for moving

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.1.7.4, PDF p.153

3.14.1.7.4 If the brake command was triggered due to an overpassed distance allowed for moving backwards in Post Trip mode or due to any further movement in the direction opposite to the train orientation while the distance allowed for moving backwards in Post Trip mode is still overpassed, the brake command shall be released at standstill and after driver acknowledgement.

Clause 3.14.1.7.5 — If the brake command was triggered due to the driver not having acknowledged a text

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.1.7.5, PDF p.153

3.14.1.7.5 If the brake command was triggered due to the driver not having acknowledged a text message, the brake command shall be released after the driver has acknowledged the text message.

Clause 3.14.1.8 — An indication shall be given to the driver to indicate when the brakes can be released

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.1.8, PDF p.153

3.14.1.8 An indication shall be given to the driver to indicate when the brakes can be released and when an acknowledgement is requested.

Clause 3.14.2 — Roll Away Protection

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.2, PDF p.153

3.14.2 Roll Away Protection

Clause 3.14.2.1 — Note: This protection is only applicable if the required information can be obtained from

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.2.1, PDF p.153

3.14.2.1 Note: This protection is only applicable if the required information can be obtained from the direction controller.

Clause 3.14.2.2 — The Roll Away Protection (RAP) shall prevent the train from moving in a direction,

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.2.2, PDF p.153

3.14.2.2 The Roll Away Protection (RAP) shall prevent the train from moving in a direction, which conflicts with the current position of the direction controller in the active desk.

Clause 3.14.2.3 — If the controller is in neutral position, the RAP shall prevent forward and reverse

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.2.3, PDF p.153

3.14.2.3 If the controller is in neutral position, the RAP shall prevent forward and reverse movements of the train.

Clause 3.14.2.4 — When the system recognises a movement exceeding the national value for the allowed

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.2.4, PDF p.153

3.14.2.4 When the system recognises a movement exceeding the national value for the allowed roll away distance the brakes shall be triggered.

Clause 3.14.2.5 — Refer to section 3.14.1.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.2.5, PDF p.153

3.14.2.5 Refer to section 3.14.1.

Clause 3.14.2.6 — An indication shall be given to the driver showing when the RAP is commanding the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.2.6, PDF p.153

3.14.2.6 An indication shall be given to the driver showing when the RAP is commanding the brakes.

Clause 3.14.2.7 — After revocation of the brake command the RAP shall be re-initialised using the current

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.2.7, PDF p.153

3.14.2.7 After revocation of the brake command the RAP shall be re-initialised using the current position of the train as the new reference location.

Clause 3.14.3 — Reverse Movement Protection

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.3, PDF p.154

3.14.3 Reverse Movement Protection

Clause 3.14.3.1 — The Reverse Movement Protection (RMP) shall prevent the train from moving in the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.3.1, PDF p.154

3.14.3.1 The Reverse Movement Protection (RMP) shall prevent the train from moving in the opposite direction to the permitted one. The permitted movement direction of a train shall be the one of the currently valid MA, if available on-board. See chapter 4 concerning permitted direction for special cases.

Clause 3.14.3.2 — When a reverse movement is detected, the brake command shall be triggered after a

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.3.2, PDF p.154

3.14.3.2 When a reverse movement is detected, the brake command shall be triggered after a distance specified by the national value.

Clause 3.14.3.3 — Refer to section 3.14.1.

3.14.3.3 Refer to section 3.14.1.

Clause 3.14.3.4 — An indication shall be given to the driver showing when the RMP is commanding the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.3.4, PDF p.154

3.14.3.4 An indication shall be given to the driver showing when the RMP is commanding the brakes.

Clause 3.14.3.5 — After revocation of the brake command the RMP shall be reinitialised using the current

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.3.5, PDF p.154

3.14.3.5 After revocation of the brake command the RMP shall be reinitialised using the current position of the train as the new reference location.

Clause 3.14.3.6 — Information received from balises during reverse movement shall be ignored.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.3.6, PDF p.154

3.14.3.6 Information received from balises during reverse movement shall be ignored.

Clause 3.14.4 — Standstill supervision

3.14.4 Standstill supervision

Clause 3.14.4.1 — This function shall prevent the train from moving.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.4.1, PDF p.154

3.14.4.1 This function shall prevent the train from moving.

Clause 3.14.4.2 — When a movement is detected, the brake command shall be triggered after a distance

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.4.2, PDF p.154

3.14.4.2 When a movement is detected, the brake command shall be triggered after a distance specified by the national value.

Clause 3.14.4.3 — Refer to section 3.14.1.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.4.3, PDF p.154

3.14.4.3 Refer to section 3.14.1.

Clause 3.14.4.4 — After revocation of the brake command the standstill supervision shall be re-initialised.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.4.4, PDF p.154

3.14.4.4 After revocation of the brake command the standstill supervision shall be re-initialised.

Clause 3.14.4.5 — If a cab is active, an indication shall be given to the driver showing when the Standstill

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.14.4.5, PDF p.154

3.14.4.5 If a cab is active, an indication shall be given to the driver showing when the Standstill Supervision is commanding the brakes.

Clause 3.15.1.1.1 — The RBC/RBC Handover principles are such that trains are able to pass from one RBC

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.1.1, PDF p.154

3.15.1.1.1 The RBC/RBC Handover principles are such that trains are able to pass from one RBC area to another automatically (without driver action).

Clause 3.15.1.1.2 — This is also granted when, due to a failure in the on-board radio communication

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.1.2, PDF p.154

3.15.1.1.2 This is also granted when, due to a failure in the on-board radio communication system, the on-board is no longer able to manage two communication sessions at once. Thereby, the behaviour of the RBCs is independent from such on-board degraded situation.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.154

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.15.1.1.3 — However, an RBC/RBC handover performed by a train with only one communication

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.1.3, PDF p.155

3.15.1.1.3 However, an RBC/RBC handover performed by a train with only one communication session available may result in performance penalties since it will not be able to “prepare” (session establishment, version determination, …) the expected supervision by the Accepting RBC until the on-board disconnects from the Handing Over RBC.

Clause 3.15.1.1.4 — In level 3, trains following the one with only one communication session available will

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.1.4, PDF p.155

3.15.1.1.4 In level 3, trains following the one with only one communication session available will always suffer performance penalties since no more position reports will be issued from the disconnection from the Handing Over RBC until the connection to the Accepting RBC.

Clause 3.15.1.2 — Handing Over RBC

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.2, PDF p.155

3.15.1.2 Handing Over RBC

Clause 3.15.1.2.1 — When the Handing Over RBC detects that a route is set for a train to enter another

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.2.1, PDF p.155

3.15.1.2.1 When the Handing Over RBC detects that a route is set for a train to enter another RBC area, it shall send: a) Intentionally deleted. b) To the Accepting RBC the following information:  The ETCS identity of the on-board equipment;  The border location that will be passed by the train when entering the Accepting RBC area;  Current mode of the on-board equipment;  For a leading engine, Train Data and Train Running Number;  The system versions supported by the on-board equipment;  Optionally, for a non-leading engine, the ETCS identity of the leading engine.

Clause 3.15.1.2.2 — The Handing Over RBC shall not send information to an on-board equipment

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.2.2, PDF p.155

3.15.1.2.2 The Handing Over RBC shall not send information to an on-board equipment concerning the route in advance of the border without receiving the corresponding information from the Accepting RBC.

Clause 3.15.1.2.3 — It shall be possible for the Handing Over RBC to request route related information from

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.2.3, PDF p.155

3.15.1.2.3 It shall be possible for the Handing Over RBC to request route related information from the Accepting RBC, limited to a maximum amount of data.

Clause 3.15.1.2.3.1 — Route related information is :

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.2.3.1, PDF p.155

3.15.1.2.3.1 Route related information is: a) Movement authorities b) Linking c) International static speed profiles d) Axle Load Speed profiles e) Gradients f) Temporary speed restrictions g) Mode profiles h) Intentionally deleted i) Track Conditions

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.155

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP j) Level Transition orders k) Intentionally deleted l) Route Suitability Data m) National Values n) Adhesion Factor o) Level Crossings p) Permitted Braking Distance Information

Clause 3.15.1.2.3.2 — Note: The amount of information to be sent between the RBCs is depending on the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.2.3.2, PDF p.156

3.15.1.2.3.2 Note: The amount of information to be sent between the RBCs is depending on the implementation trackside.

Clause 3.15.1.2.4 — Note: Route related information received from the Accepting RBC will be processed by

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.2.4, PDF p.156

3.15.1.2.4 Note: Route related information received from the Accepting RBC will be processed by the Handing Over RBC if possible.

Clause 3.15.1.2.6 — When the Handing Over RBC receives a position report and detects that the maximum

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.2.6, PDF p.156

3.15.1.2.6 When the Handing Over RBC receives a position report and detects that the maximum safe front end of the train has passed the border location, it shall inform the Accepting RBC.

Clause 3.15.1.2.6.1 — Note: This information might be needed to inform the signalman of the Accepting

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.2.6.1, PDF p.156

3.15.1.2.6.1 Note: This information might be needed to inform the signalman of the Accepting RBC that the train has entered the Accepting RBC area.

Clause 3.15.1.2.7 — When the Handing Over RBC receives a position report and detects that the minimum

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.2.7, PDF p.156

3.15.1.2.7 When the Handing Over RBC receives a position report and detects that the minimum safe rear end of the train has crossed the border, it shall send a disconnection order to the on-board equipment.

Clause 3.15.1.2.8 — When the Accepting RBC informs the Handing Over RBC that it has taken over the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.2.8, PDF p.156

3.15.1.2.8 When the Accepting RBC informs the Handing Over RBC that it has taken over the responsibility, the latter shall stop sending route related information to the on-board equipment.

Clause 3.15.1.2.9 — When the Handing Over RBC detects that the transition to the Accepting RBC has to

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.2.9, PDF p.156

3.15.1.2.9 When the Handing Over RBC detects that the transition to the Accepting RBC has to be cancelled, it shall send this cancellation information to the Accepting RBC (including the train identification).

Clause 3.15.1.2.9.1 — Note: For instance, the cancellation procedure can be triggered by:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.2.9.1, PDF p.156

3.15.1.2.9.1 Note: For instance, the cancellation procedure can be triggered by:  Change to a route which does no more include the border;  The sending of an “end of mission” information from the on-board equipment.

Clause 3.15.1.3 — On-board equipment

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.3, PDF p.156

3.15.1.3 On-board equipment

Clause 3.15.1.3.1 — When receiving an order to switch to another RBC at a given location, the on-board

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.3.1, PDF p.156

3.15.1.3.1 When receiving an order to switch to another RBC at a given location, the on-board equipment shall: a) Establish the communication session with the Accepting RBC;

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.156

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP b) Send a position report to the Handing Over RBC when the maximum safe front end of the train passes the given location; c) Send a position report to the Handing Over RBC when the minimum safe rear end of the train passes the given location;

Clause 3.15.1.3.2 — Exception to 3.15.1.3.1 a) (degraded situation): If the on-board equipment is able to

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.3.2, PDF p.157

3.15.1.3.2 Exception to 3.15.1.3.1 a) (degraded situation): If the on-board equipment is able to handle only one communication session at a given time, it shall wait until the session with the Handing over RBC is terminated due to crossing the border and then establish the session with the Accepting RBC.

Clause 3.15.1.3.3 — As soon as the on-board equipment has established the session with the Accepting

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.3.3, PDF p.157

3.15.1.3.3 As soon as the on-board equipment has established the session with the Accepting RBC, it shall send its Train Data unless it is in sleeping or non leading mode.

Clause 3.15.1.3.4 — When the on-board equipment is connected to both RBCs, it shall send its position

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.3.4, PDF p.157

3.15.1.3.4 When the on-board equipment is connected to both RBCs, it shall send its position reports to both of them with the use of the position report parameters valid for the Handing Over RBC.

Clause 3.15.1.3.4.1 — If the on-board equipment is connected to both RBCs, and it executes an End of

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.3.4.1, PDF p.157

3.15.1.3.4.1 If the on-board equipment is connected to both RBCs, and it executes an End of Mission, it shall execute the End of Mission procedure with both RBCs

Clause 3.15.1.3.5 — As soon as the on-board sends a position report directly to the Accepting RBC with its

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.3.5, PDF p.157

3.15.1.3.5 As soon as the on-board sends a position report directly to the Accepting RBC with its maximum safe front end having passed the border, it shall use information received from the Accepting RBC and only a disconnection order shall be accepted from the Handing Over RBC.

Clause 3.15.1.3.5.1 — Intentionally deleted.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.3.5.1, PDF p.157

3.15.1.3.5.1 Intentionally deleted.

Clause 3.15.1.3.6 — While both communication sessions are opened, if information is received from the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.3.6, PDF p.157

3.15.1.3.6 While both communication sessions are opened, if information is received from the Accepting RBC before a position report is sent to the Accepting RBC with the maximum safe front end having passed the border, this information shall be stored on- board. Exception: The acknowledgement of Train Data shall be immediately accepted by the on-board equipment.

Clause 3.15.1.3.6.1 — Note: for the exhaustive list of accepted/rejected information, please refer to

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.3.6.1, PDF p.157

3.15.1.3.6.1 Note: for the exhaustive list of accepted/rejected information, please refer to Chapter 4 Use of received information.

Clause 3.15.1.3.7 — When the train front end passes the announced border or when an order to execute

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.3.7, PDF p.157

3.15.1.3.7 When the train front end passes the announced border or when an order to execute the RBC transition immediately is received, the on-board shall substitute the current valid RBC ID/phone number with those of the Accepting RBC.

Clause 3.15.1.3.8 — After this substitution, the on-board shall however retain the RBC ID/phone number of

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.3.8, PDF p.157

3.15.1.3.8 After this substitution, the on-board shall however retain the RBC ID/phone number of the Handing Over RBC until at least one of the following conditions is fulfilled: a) Min safe rear end of the train has passed the border and the communication session with this Handing Over RBC has been terminated, b) The RBC transition order is deleted according to A.3.4 or 4.10.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.157

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.15.1.3.8.1 — Note: Even after the train front end has passed the border, the on-board may have

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.3.8.1, PDF p.158

3.15.1.3.8.1 Note: Even after the train front end has passed the border, the on-board may have to maintain the communication session with the Handing Over RBC (see 3.5.4) or re- establish it (see 3.5.3.4 f)) and needs therefore to remember the RBC ID/phone number of this RBC.

Clause 3.15.1.3.9 — In case the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment has reported that the train has passed

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.3.9, PDF p.158

3.15.1.3.9 In case the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment has reported that the train has passed with its min safe rear end the announced border and no order to terminate the session is received from the Handing Over RBC within a fixed waiting time (see Appendix A.3.1) from the time the position report was sent, it shall repeatedly send a position report with the fixed waiting time after each repetition, until the order to terminate the session is received, or the defined number of repetitions (see Appendix A.3.1) has been reached. If no reply is received within the fixed waiting time after the last repetition, the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall terminate the communication session with the Handing Over RBC.

Clause 3.15.1.4.1 — The Accepting RBC shall keep route related information sent to the Handing Over RBC

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.4.1, PDF p.158

3.15.1.4.1 The Accepting RBC shall keep route related information sent to the Handing Over RBC updated. In particular, this possibly includes temporary speed restrictions.

Clause 3.15.1.4.2 — As soon as the Accepting RBC receives from the on-board equipment a position report

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.4.2, PDF p.158

3.15.1.4.2 As soon as the Accepting RBC receives from the on-board equipment a position report and detects that the maximum safe front end of the train has passed the border, it shall inform the Handing Over RBC that it has taken over the responsibility.

Clause 3.15.1.4.3 — When the Accepting RBC receives Train Data from both the on-board equipment and

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.4.3, PDF p.158

3.15.1.4.3 When the Accepting RBC receives Train Data from both the on-board equipment and the Handing over RBC Train Data provided by the on-board equipment shall take precedence.

Clause 3.15.1.4.4 — If the Accepting RBC receives a cancellation information from the Handing Over RBC,

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.4.4, PDF p.158

3.15.1.4.4 If the Accepting RBC receives a cancellation information from the Handing Over RBC, it shall send an order to terminate the communication session to the corresponding on- board equipment (if already established).

Clause 3.15.1.4.5 — The Accepting RBC shall comply with the maximum amount of data contained in the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.4.5, PDF p.158

3.15.1.4.5 The Accepting RBC shall comply with the maximum amount of data contained in the last received route related information request from the Handing Over RBC.

Clause 3.15.1.5 — RBC/RBC message acknowledgement

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.5, PDF p.158

3.15.1.5 RBC/RBC message acknowledgement

Clause 3.15.1.5.1 — As soon as a consistent RBC/RBC message including the request for

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.5.1, PDF p.158

3.15.1.5.1 As soon as a consistent RBC/RBC message including the request for acknowledgement is received, the receiving RBC shall send an acknowledgement to the emitting RBC.

Clause 3.15.1.5.2 — The RBC/RBC message is consistent when all checks have been completed

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.1.5.2, PDF p.158

3.15.1.5.2 The RBC/RBC message is consistent when all checks have been completed successfully: a) It has passed the checks performed by the RBC/RBC Safe Communication Interface protocol (see SUBSET-098); b) Variables in the message do not have invalid values.

Clause 3.15.2 — Handling of Trains with Non Leading Engines

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.2, PDF p.159

3.15.2 Handling of Trains with Non Leading Engines

Clause 3.15.2.1 — It is possible to operate a train using more than one engine, each engine being under

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.2.1, PDF p.159

3.15.2.1 It is possible to operate a train using more than one engine, each engine being under the control of a driver.

Clause 3.15.2.2 — Only the leading engine is responsible for the train movement supervision functions.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.2.2, PDF p.159

3.15.2.2 Only the leading engine is responsible for the train movement supervision functions.

Clause 3.15.3.1 — ERTMS/ETCS allows Splitting and Joining using the normal supervision functions

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.3.1, PDF p.159

3.15.3.1 ERTMS/ETCS allows Splitting and Joining using the normal supervision functions available (e.g. On-sight, Shunting).

Clause 3.15.3.2 — Splitting only refers to the case that the two resulting trains contain at least one

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.3.2, PDF p.159

3.15.3.2 Splitting only refers to the case that the two resulting trains contain at least one ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment each.

Clause 3.15.3.2.1 — Note: This must be ensured by operational procedures.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.3.2.1, PDF p.159

3.15.3.2.1 Note: This must be ensured by operational procedures.

Clause 3.15.3.3 — ERTMS/ETCS is not responsible for providing information that a Splitting/Joining

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.3.3, PDF p.159

3.15.3.3 ERTMS/ETCS is not responsible for providing information that a Splitting/Joining operation has been correctly completed (technical aspect and/or operational aspect).

Clause 3.15.3.4 — Justification: ERTMS/ETCS is not able to provide this information. Splitting and Joining

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.3.4, PDF p.159

3.15.3.4 Justification: ERTMS/ETCS is not able to provide this information. Splitting and Joining requires the fulfilment of operating rules ensuring that a Splitting/Joining operation has been correctly completed (e.g. physical disconnection).

Clause 3.15.4 — Reversing of movement direction

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.4, PDF p.159

3.15.4 Reversing of movement direction

Clause 3.15.4.1 — It shall be possible to send in advance to an on-board equipment information about

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.4.1, PDF p.159

3.15.4.1 It shall be possible to send in advance to an on-board equipment information about areas, where initiation of reversing of movement direction is possible, i.e. change the direction of train movement without changing the train orientation.

Clause 3.15.4.1.1 — A new reversing area given from the trackside shall replace the one already available

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.4.1.1, PDF p.159

3.15.4.1.1 A new reversing area given from the trackside shall replace the one already available on-board.

Clause 3.15.4.2 — Together with start and end of reversing area, the following supervision information

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.4.2, PDF p.159

3.15.4.2 Together with start and end of reversing area, the following supervision information shall be sent: a) Maximum distance to run in the direction opposite to the orientation of the reversing area, the fixed reference location being the end location of the area where reversing of movement is permitted with which the maximum distance information is sent. b) Reversing mode speed limit allowed during reverse movement.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.160

Clause 3.15.4.4 — While at standstill with the front end of the train inside the indicated area, it shall be

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.4.4, PDF p.161

3.15.4.4 While at standstill with the front end of the train inside the indicated area, it shall be possible for the driver to reverse the direction of movement.

Clause 3.15.4.5 — The on-board equipment shall allow movement in the direction opposite to the train

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.4.5, PDF p.161

3.15.4.5 The on-board equipment shall allow movement in the direction opposite to the train orientation, supervising it according to distance and speed received.

Clause 3.15.4.6 — Note: level transitions and RBC/RBC handovers are not handled by the ERTMS/ETCS

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.4.6, PDF p.161

3.15.4.6 Note: level transitions and RBC/RBC handovers are not handled by the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment when in Reversing mode.

Clause 3.15.4.7 — When at standstill the on-board equipment shall inform the driver if the reversing of

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.4.7, PDF p.161

3.15.4.7 When at standstill the on-board equipment shall inform the driver if the reversing of movement is permitted.

Clause 3.15.4.8 — If the end location of the maximum distance to run in the opposite direction is passed

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.4.8, PDF p.161

3.15.4.8 If the end location of the maximum distance to run in the opposite direction is passed by the train front end, the emergency brake command shall be triggered.

Clause 3.15.5.1 — In a level 2/3 area, the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment is able to handle a track

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.5.1, PDF p.161

3.15.5.1 In a level 2/3 area, the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment is able to handle a track ahead free request given by the RBC.

Clause 3.15.5.2 — The track ahead free request from the RBC shall indicate to the on-board

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.5.2, PDF p.161

3.15.5.2 The track ahead free request from the RBC shall indicate to the on-board a) at which location the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall begin to display the request to the driver. b) at which location the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall stop to display the request to the driver (in case the driver did not acknowledge).

Clause 3.15.5.3 — As long as it is displayed, the driver has the possibility to acknowledge the track ahead

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.5.3, PDF p.161

3.15.5.3 As long as it is displayed, the driver has the possibility to acknowledge the track ahead free request (meaning the driver confirms that the track between the head of the train and the next signal or board marking signal position is free).

Clause 3.15.5.4 — When the driver acknowledges, the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall stop

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.5.4, PDF p.161

3.15.5.4 When the driver acknowledges, the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall stop displaying the request, and shall inform the RBC that the track ahead is free.

Clause 3.15.5.5 — There is no restrictive consequence by the on-board system if the driver does not

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.5.5, PDF p.161

3.15.5.5 There is no restrictive consequence by the on-board system if the driver does not acknowledge.

Clause 3.15.5.6 — A new track ahead free request shall replace the one previously received and stored.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.5.6, PDF p.161

3.15.5.6 A new track ahead free request shall replace the one previously received and stored.

Clause 3.15.7 — Tolerance of Big Metal Mass

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.7, PDF p.162

3.15.7 Tolerance of Big Metal Mass

Clause 3.15.7.1 — Big metal object in the track, exceeding the limits for big metal masses as defined in

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.7.1, PDF p.162

3.15.7.1 Big metal object in the track, exceeding the limits for big metal masses as defined in Subset-036, section 6.5.2 “Metal Masses in the Track” may trigger an alarm reporting a malfunction for the onboard balise transmission function.

Clause 3.15.7.2 — In Levels 0/NTC, the alarms which may be triggered by metal masses shall be ignored

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.7.2, PDF p.162

3.15.7.2 In Levels 0/NTC, the alarms which may be triggered by metal masses shall be ignored for a defined distance (see A.3.1). If the alarm persists for a longer distance the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall trigger a safety reaction.

Clause 3.15.7.3 — Justification: Ignoring the alarm for a defined distance eliminates the need to equip all

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.7.3, PDF p.162

3.15.7.3 Justification: Ignoring the alarm for a defined distance eliminates the need to equip all excessive big metal masses with track condition “Big Metal Mass” outside ETCS fitted areas.

Clause 3.15.8 — Cold Movement Detection

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.8, PDF p.162

3.15.8 Cold Movement Detection

Clause 3.15.8.1 — After being switched off (i.e. once in No Power mode), the ERTMS/ETCS on-board

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.8.1, PDF p.162

3.15.8.1 After being switched off (i.e. once in No Power mode), the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall be capable, if fitted with, to detect and record whether the engine has been moved or not, during a period of at least 72 hours.

Clause 3.15.8.2 — When powered on again, the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall use, if

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.8.2, PDF p.162

3.15.8.2 When powered on again, the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall use, if available, the memorised information about cold movement in order to update the status of information stored by on-board equipment (see chapter 4 section 4.11 for details).

Clause 3.15.8.3 — Note: information memorised by Cold Movement Detection function is considered as

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.8.3, PDF p.162

3.15.8.3 Note: information memorised by Cold Movement Detection function is considered as not available if: a) no Cold Movement Detection function is implemented in the ERTMS/ETCS on- board equipment, OR b) the Cold Movement Detection function has encountered a condition, during the No Power period, which prevents the use of the Cold Movement information (e.g. the battery ensuring the Cold Movement Detection function has run down during the No Power period).

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.162

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.15.9 — Virtual Balise Cover

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.9, PDF p.163

3.15.9 Virtual Balise Cover

Clause 3.15.9.1 — It shall be possible to set and remove from balise a Virtual Balise Cover (VBC). A VBC

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.9.1, PDF p.163

3.15.9.1 It shall be possible to set and remove from balise a Virtual Balise Cover (VBC). A VBC is defined by: a) A marker corresponding to balises to be ignored by the on-board together with the area (country or region) in which the VBC is applicable. The VBC marker and the country/region identity form the unique VBC identity. b) Its validity period.

Clause 3.15.9.2 — During a start of mission, the driver shall have the opportunity to set a new VBC, or to

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.9.2, PDF p.163

3.15.9.2 During a start of mission, the driver shall have the opportunity to set a new VBC, or to remove an existing one.

Clause 3.15.9.3 — As long as a VBC is stored on-board:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.9.3, PDF p.163

3.15.9.3 As long as a VBC is stored on-board: a) The ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall ignore any balise telegram that includes a VBC marker and a country/region identity that both match the VBC identity. b) No reaction shall be applied if errors in the reading of the rest of such balise telegram occur.

Clause 3.15.9.4 — If the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment receives from balise or from driver a new

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.9.4, PDF p.163

3.15.9.4 If the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment receives from balise or from driver a new VBC with the same VBC identity as an already stored VBC, the new VBC shall replace the previous one, including its validity period.

Clause 3.15.9.5 — A VBC shall be retained on-board when the on-board equipment is switched off (i.e.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.9.5, PDF p.163

3.15.9.5 A VBC shall be retained on-board when the on-board equipment is switched off (i.e. enters No Power mode) and shall remain applicable when powered on again. It shall be deleted when: a) it is ordered by trackside, or b) its validity period has elapsed, or c) it is removed by the driver (during Start of Mission), or d) a mismatch is detected between the country/region identity read from a balise group and the country/region identity of the VBC. Note: this means that the reception of a consistent balise group message is a necessary condition for deleting a VBC due to mismatching country/region identities.

Clause 3.15.10 — Advance display of route related information

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.10, PDF p.163

3.15.10 Advance display of route related information

Clause 3.15.10.1 — The ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall display an overview of the gradient

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.10.1, PDF p.163

3.15.10.1 The ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall display an overview of the gradient profile (as received from trackside), of the MRSP, of the track conditions (except the tunnel stopping areas), of the first Indication location, if any (only in Ceiling Speed monitoring), and of the EOA/LOA, with the remaining distances referred to the train front end position.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.163

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.15.10.2 — With regards to the MRSP, the track conditions and the EOA/LOA, the remaining

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.10.2, PDF p.164

3.15.10.2 With regards to the MRSP, the track conditions and the EOA/LOA, the remaining distances shall be computed taking into account the min safe, the estimated or the max safe train front end position depending on their respective supervision.

Clause 3.15.10.3 — With regards to the gradient profile, the remaining distances shall be computed taking

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.10.3, PDF p.164

3.15.10.3 With regards to the gradient profile, the remaining distances shall be computed taking into account the estimated train front end position.

Clause 3.15.10.3.1 — With regards to the first Indication location, the remaining distance shall be computed

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.10.3.1, PDF p.164

3.15.10.3.1 With regards to the first Indication location, the remaining distance shall be computed as specified in clauses 3.13.10.3.8 and 3.13.10.3.8.1.

Clause 3.15.10.4 — The overview of route related information shall be restricted to the elements contained

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.15.10.4, PDF p.164

3.15.10.4 The overview of route related information shall be restricted to the elements contained within the movement authority and up to the first target at zero speed, if any.

Clause 3.16.1 — Criteria of consistency

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.1, PDF p.164

3.16.1 Criteria of consistency

Clause 3.16.1.1 — The on-board shall not consider a message transmitted from the trackside if any of the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.1.1, PDF p.164

3.16.1.1 The on-board shall not consider a message transmitted from the trackside if any of the following criteria is not fulfilled. a) Correctness of the received message: the whole message shall be complete and respect the ETCS language; variables shall not have invalid values b) The message shall be received in due time. c) The message shall be received at the right expected location.

Clause 3.16.1.1.1 — Regarding a): a value of a variable is invalid when a spare value is used.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.1.1.1, PDF p.164

3.16.1.1.1 Regarding a): a value of a variable is invalid when a spare value is used.

Clause 3.16.2.1.1 — The information that is sent from a balise is called a balise telegram.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.1.1, PDF p.164

3.16.2.1.1 The information that is sent from a balise is called a balise telegram.

Clause 3.16.2.1.2 — The whole set of information (balise telegram or telegrams) coming from a balise

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.1.2, PDF p.164

3.16.2.1.2 The whole set of information (balise telegram or telegrams) coming from a balise group is called a balise group message.

Clause 3.16.2.1.2.1 — Note: In case of a balise group containing a single balise, telegram and message

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.1.2.1, PDF p.164

3.16.2.1.2.1 Note: In case of a balise group containing a single balise, telegram and message coincide.

Clause 3.16.2.1.3 — Intentionally deleted.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.1.3, PDF p.164

3.16.2.1.3 Intentionally deleted.

Clause 3.16.2.2.1 — If the on-board is not able to recognise whether a balise group is linked or unlinked (if

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.2.1, PDF p.164

3.16.2.2.1 If the on-board is not able to recognise whether a balise group is linked or unlinked (if none of the balises in the balise group can be read correctly), it shall consider it as unlinked.

Clause 3.16.2.2.2 — A balise within a balise group shall be regarded as missed if

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.2.2, PDF p.164

3.16.2.2.2 A balise within a balise group shall be regarded as missed if

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.164

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP a) No balise is found within the maximum distance between balises from the previous balise in the group. or b) A following balise within the group has been passed.

Clause 3.16.2.3 — Linking Consistency

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.3, PDF p.165

3.16.2.3 Linking Consistency

Clause 3.16.2.3.1 — If linking information is used the on-board shall react according to the linking reaction

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.3.1, PDF p.165

3.16.2.3.1 If linking information is used the on-board shall react according to the linking reaction information in the following cases: a) If the location reference of the expected balise group is found in rear of the expectation window b) If the location reference of the expected balise group is not found inside the expectation window (i.e. the end of the expectation window has been reached without having found the expected balise group) c) If inside the expectation window of the expected balise group another announced balise group, expected later, is found.

Clause 3.16.2.3.1.1 — The ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall reject the message from a balise

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.3.1.1, PDF p.165

3.16.2.3.1.1 The ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall reject the message from a balise group found with its location reference outside its expectation window.

Clause 3.16.2.3.2 — The on-board shall reject the message from the expected group and trip the train if the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.3.2, PDF p.165

3.16.2.3.2 The on-board shall reject the message from the expected group and trip the train if the balise group is passed in the unexpected direction.

Clause 3.16.2.3.2.1 — Exception: When the expected balise group is referred in the linking information

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.3.2.1, PDF p.165

3.16.2.3.2.1 Exception: When the expected balise group is referred in the linking information with a balise group with ID “unknown”, 3.4.4.4.2.1 shall apply.

Clause 3.16.2.3.3 — If the location reference balise of the group is duplicated and the on-board is only able

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.3.3, PDF p.165

3.16.2.3.3 If the location reference balise of the group is duplicated and the on-board is only able to correctly evaluate the duplicating one, the duplicating one shall be used as location reference instead.

Clause 3.16.2.3.4 — If the balise duplicating the location reference balise is used as location reference for

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.3.4, PDF p.165

3.16.2.3.4 If the balise duplicating the location reference balise is used as location reference for the group, and is found within the expectation window, no linking reaction shall be applied.

Clause 3.16.2.4 — Balise Group Message Consistency

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.4, PDF p.165

3.16.2.4 Balise Group Message Consistency

Clause 3.16.2.4.1 — If linking information is used, the on-board shall reject the message from a linked

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.4.1, PDF p.165

3.16.2.4.1 If linking information is used, the on-board shall reject the message from a linked balise group found in the expected location and react according to the linking reaction in the following cases: a) A balise is missed inside the group. b) A balise is detected but no telegram is decoded (e.g. wrong CRC,...). c) Variables in the balise group message have invalid values. d) Message counters do not match (see 3.16.2.4.7)

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.165

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.16.2.4.2 — Exception: Concerning a) and b) above, the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.4.2, PDF p.166

3.16.2.4.2 Exception: Concerning a) and b) above, the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall not reject the message and shall not apply the linking reaction if the balise not found, or not decoded, is duplicated within the balise group and the duplicating one is correctly read.

Clause 3.16.2.4.3 — If linking information is used, the on-board shall reject the message from a balise

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.4.3, PDF p.166

3.16.2.4.3 If linking information is used, the on-board shall reject the message from a balise group marked as linked but not included in the linking information. No reaction shall be applied, even if errors in the reading of the balise group occur.

Clause 3.16.2.4.4 — If no linking information is used, the on-board shall reject the message from a balise

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.4.4, PDF p.166

3.16.2.4.4 If no linking information is used, the on-board shall reject the message from a balise group marked as linked and command application of the service brake in the following cases: a) A balise is missed inside the group. b) A balise is detected, but no telegram is decoded (e.g. wrong CRC). c) Variables in the balise group message have invalid values. d) Message counters do not match (see 3.16.2.4.7)

Clause 3.16.2.4.4.1 — Exceptions: Concerning a) and b) of clause 3.16.2.4.4, the ERTMS/ETCS on-board

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.4.4.1, PDF p.166

3.16.2.4.4.1 Exceptions: Concerning a) and b) of clause 3.16.2.4.4, the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment: a) shall not reject the message and shall not command application of the service brake if the balise not found, or not decoded, is duplicated within the balise group, the duplicating one is correctly read and contains:  directional information while the orientation of the balise group can still be evaluated, or  only information valid for both directions, or  neither directional information nor information valid for both directions, or  only data to be used by applications outside ERTMS/ETCS, or  only data to be used by applications outside ERTMS/ETCS together with other information valid for both directions. b) shall not command application of the service brake if the telegram correctly read from another balise of the group contains the information “Inhibition of balise group message consistency reaction”.

Clause 3.16.2.4.4.2 — Concerning clause 3.16.2.4.4, if the service brake is applied, the location based

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.4.4.2, PDF p.166

3.16.2.4.4.2 Concerning clause 3.16.2.4.4, if the service brake is applied, the location based information stored on-board shall be shortened to the current position when the train has reached standstill. Refer to appendix A.3.4 for the exhaustive list of information, which shall be shortened.

Clause 3.16.2.4.4.3 — Concerning clause 3.16.2.4.4, if the service brake is applied, the driver shall be

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.4.4.3, PDF p.166

3.16.2.4.4.3 Concerning clause 3.16.2.4.4, if the service brake is applied, the driver shall be informed that this is due to a balise group message consistency problem.

Clause 3.16.2.4.5 — A message counter shall be attached to each balise telegram indicating which balise

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.4.5, PDF p.166

3.16.2.4.5 A message counter shall be attached to each balise telegram indicating which balise group message the telegram fits to.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.166

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.16.2.4.6 — Instead of a message counter corresponding to a given balise group message, it shall

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.4.6, PDF p.167

3.16.2.4.6 Instead of a message counter corresponding to a given balise group message, it shall be possible to identify a telegram as always fitting all possible messages of the group.

Clause 3.16.2.4.6.1 — It shall also be possible to identify a telegram as never fitting any message of the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.4.6.1, PDF p.167

3.16.2.4.6.1 It shall also be possible to identify a telegram as never fitting any message of the group.

Clause 3.16.2.4.7 — Comparing message counters of the received telegrams of a balise group message,

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.4.7, PDF p.167

3.16.2.4.7 Comparing message counters of the received telegrams of a balise group message, excluding the ones complying with 3.16.2.4.6 and the ones that are not used by the on- board to compose the message (see 3.16.2.4.8.2), if their values are not all identical, or at least one of them complies with 3.16.2.4.6.1, this shall be considered as a message consistency error.

Clause 3.16.2.4.7.1 — In case of single balise group, if the message counter of the received telegram

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.4.7.1, PDF p.167

3.16.2.4.7.1 In case of single balise group, if the message counter of the received telegram complies with 3.16.2.4.6.1, this shall also be considered as a message consistency error.

Clause 3.16.2.4.8 — It shall be possible to indicate failures in the system underlying the balise/loop/RIU

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.4.8, PDF p.167

3.16.2.4.8 It shall be possible to indicate failures in the system underlying the balise/loop/RIU (e.g. the Lineside Electronic Unit, LEU) by sending a balise telegram, a loop message or a RIU message including the information "default balise/loop/RIU information".

Clause 3.16.2.4.8.1 — If one (and only one) out of a pair of duplicated balise telegrams received by the on-

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.4.8.1, PDF p.167

3.16.2.4.8.1 If one (and only one) out of a pair of duplicated balise telegrams received by the on- board includes the information “default balise information”, the on-board shall ignore any other information included in this telegram and shall consider information from the telegram not containing “default balise information".

Clause 3.16.2.4.8.2 — When duplicated balises are both found and decoded correctly, and both, or none

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.4.8.2, PDF p.167

3.16.2.4.8.2 When duplicated balises are both found and decoded correctly, and both, or none of them, contain "default balise information", the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall compose the message using the telegram from the last received balise out of the pair.

Clause 3.16.2.4.9 — If a message has been received containing the information "default balise information",

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.4.9, PDF p.167

3.16.2.4.9 If a message has been received containing the information "default balise information", the driver shall be informed.

Clause 3.16.2.7 — RAMS related supervision functions

3.16.2.7 RAMS related supervision functions

Clause 3.16.2.7.1 — Mitigation of balise reception degradation

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.7.1, PDF p.168

3.16.2.7.1 Mitigation of balise reception degradation

Clause 3.16.2.7.1.1 — If 2 consecutive linked balise groups announced by linking are not detected and the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.7.1.1, PDF p.168

3.16.2.7.1.1 If 2 consecutive linked balise groups announced by linking are not detected and the end of the expectation window of the second balise group has been passed, the ERTMS/ETCS on-board shall command the service brake and the driver shall be informed. At standstill, the location based information stored on-board shall be shortened to the current position. Refer to appendix A.3.4 for the exhaustive list of information, which shall be shortened.

Clause 3.16.2.7.2 — Mitigation of balise cross-talk while expecting repositioning information

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.7.2, PDF p.168

3.16.2.7.2 Mitigation of balise cross-talk while expecting repositioning information

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.168

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.16.2.7.2.1 — If repositioning is announced and the expected repositioning balise group has been

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.7.2.1, PDF p.169

3.16.2.7.2.1 If repositioning is announced and the expected repositioning balise group has been found, the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall keep looking for a balise group that satisfies the same criteria as this previously expected and already found repositioning balise group, until one of the following events occurs: a) the on-board antenna leaves the expectation window of the repositioning balise group that was announced and already found b) a linked balise group that has been announced with known identity is found.

Clause 3.16.2.7.2.2 — If a second balise group is found that satisfies the same criteria as the previously

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.7.2.2, PDF p.169

3.16.2.7.2.2 If a second balise group is found that satisfies the same criteria as the previously expected and already found repositioning balise group, the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall command the service brake and the driver shall be informed. At standstill, the location based information stored on-board shall be shortened to the current position. Refer to appendix A.3.4 for the exhaustive list of information, which shall be shortened.

Clause 3.16.2.7.2.3 — Note: this function is independent from linking function, i.e. the rules related to

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.2.7.2.3, PDF p.169

3.16.2.7.2.3 Note: this function is independent from linking function, i.e. the rules related to linking always apply. This means that once a repositioning balise group has been found and if this latter contains new linking information, the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment will start expecting the first balise group announced in this new linking information in parallel with the monitoring specified in 3.16.2.7.2.1.

Clause 3.16.3.1.1 — A radio message is consistent when all checks have been completed successfully:

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.3.1.1, PDF p.169

3.16.3.1.1 A radio message is consistent when all checks have been completed successfully: a) Checks performed by Euroradio protocol have been passed (see Subset-037) b) Time stamps checks have been passed (see 3.16.3.3.3) c) Variables in the messages do not have in-valid values.

Clause 3.16.3.1.1.1 — The on-board shall reject a message transmitted from the trackside if the message

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.3.1.1.1, PDF p.169

3.16.3.1.1.1 The on-board shall reject a message transmitted from the trackside if the message is not consistent.

Clause 3.16.3.1.1.2 — The on-board shall inform the trackside if a not consistent message is received.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.3.1.1.2, PDF p.169

3.16.3.1.1.2 The on-board shall inform the trackside if a not consistent message is received.

Clause 3.16.3.1.2 — Emergency messages shall be transmitted either as high priority data or as normal

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.3.1.2, PDF p.169

3.16.3.1.2 Emergency messages shall be transmitted either as high priority data or as normal priority data.

Clause 3.16.3.1.3 — Other messages shall be sent as normal priority data.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.3.1.3, PDF p.169

3.16.3.1.3 Other messages shall be sent as normal priority data.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.3.1.3.1, PDF p.169

3.16.3.1.3.1 Messages shall only be accepted when received with the data priority for which they are specified.

Clause 3.16.3.1.4 — The chapters 3.16.3.2 to 3.16.3.5 define data consistency principles and

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.3.1.4, PDF p.169

3.16.3.1.4 The chapters 3.16.3.2 to 3.16.3.5 define data consistency principles and corresponding checks for data transmitted as normal priority data. For high priority data, the checks shall not apply.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.169

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.16.3.2.1 — The trackside shall always transmit its information with reference to the train time.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.3.2.1, PDF p.170

3.16.3.2.1 The trackside shall always transmit its information with reference to the train time.

Clause 3.16.3.2.2 — To time-stamp its messages, the trackside shall make a safe estimation of the on-

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.3.2.2, PDF p.170

3.16.3.2.2 To time-stamp its messages, the trackside shall make a safe estimation of the on- board time, based on the time-stamp of the received messages and the internal processing times. The estimation shall be made in such a way that the on-board time estimated by the trackside shall not be in advance of the real on-board time.

Clause 3.16.3.2.3 — Wrap around of the onboard timer can occur during a communication session and

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.3.2.3, PDF p.170

3.16.3.2.3 Wrap around of the onboard timer can occur during a communication session and shall have no impact on system behaviour.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.3.3.2, PDF p.170

3.16.3.3.2 There shall always be a time stamp increment between consecutive messages.

3.16.3.3.3 If the time stamp of the last received message is lower than or equal to the former one its content shall not be used.

Clause 3.16.3.3.3.1 — Only time stamps of messages received as normal priority data shall be used.

3.16.3.3.3.1 Only time stamps of messages received as normal priority data shall be used.

Clause 3.16.3.3.3.2 — Note: The supervision does not detect a lost message. This has to be assured by

3.16.3.3.3.2 Note: The supervision does not detect a lost message. This has to be assured by means of the “acknowledge” function.

3.16.3.4 Supervision of safe radio connection

Clause 3.16.3.4.1 — When the difference between the time stamp of the latest consistent received

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.3.4.1, PDF p.170

3.16.3.4.1 When the difference between the time stamp of the latest consistent received message and the current on-board time is greater than the T_NVCONTACT parameter (national value), the on-board shall apply the reaction required by trackside (see 3.16.3.4.2).

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.170

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.16.3.4.1.1 — After the on-board equipment has switched to L2 or 3 with no communication

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.3.4.1.1, PDF p.171

3.16.3.4.1.1 After the on-board equipment has switched to L2 or 3 with no communication session established, the current onboard time shall be compared with the on-board time at the moment of the level transition (instead of the time stamp of the latest consistent received message) until a new consistent message has been received.

Clause 3.16.3.4.1.2 — When an RBC/RBC handover has been announced, the current onboard time shall

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.3.4.1.2, PDF p.171

3.16.3.4.1.2 When an RBC/RBC handover has been announced, the current onboard time shall be compared with the time stamp of the latest consistent message from the Handing over RBC until the train considers the Accepting RBC as the supervising one (refer to 3.15.1.3.5). From then on the current onboard time shall be compared with the time stamp of the latest received consistent message from the Accepting RBC.

Clause 3.16.3.4.1.3 — As long as the train front end is inside an announced radio hole, the ERTMS/ETCS

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.16.3.4.1.3, PDF p.171

3.16.3.4.1.3 As long as the train front end is inside an announced radio hole, the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment shall stop the supervision of the safe radio connection. Afterwards, until a new consistent message has been received, the current onboard time shall be compared with the on-board time when the train front end left the radio hole (instead of the time stamp of the latest consistent received message). Clause 3.17.2 — Determination of the operated system version

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.17.2, PDF p.173

3.17.2 Determination of the operated system version

Clause 3.17.2.1 — The on-board equipment shall be able to operate with (i.e. shall support) any of the

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.17.2.1, PDF p.173

3.17.2.1 The on-board equipment shall be able to operate with (i.e. shall support) any of the ERTMS/ETCS system version numbers X included in the envelope of legally operated system versions, as defined in chapter 6.

Clause 3.17.2.1.1 — Within one of its supported system version numbers X, the on-board equipment shall

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.17.2.1.1, PDF p.173

3.17.2.1.1 Within one of its supported system version numbers X, the on-board equipment shall always operate the highest system version number Y defined in this release of the SRS, regardless of the system version number Y transmitted by the trackside.

Clause 3.17.2.2 — The on-board equipment shall operate with only one system version at a time, i.e. it

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.17.2.2, PDF p.173

3.17.2.2 The on-board equipment shall operate with only one system version at a time, i.e. it shall behave according to the whole set of requirements applicable to a system version (refer to chapter 6 in case the operated system version is older than the last one introduced in this release of the SRS).

Clause 3.17.2.3 — The on-board equipment shall determine the operated system version number X, in

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.17.2.3, PDF p.173

3.17.2.3 The on-board equipment shall determine the operated system version number X, in relation to non-RBC trackside constituents, as the system version number X transmitted by any balise, loop or RIU, if this system version number X is higher than the currently operated one.

Clause 3.17.2.4 — It shall be possible from balise group to order the on-board equipment to operate a

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.17.2.4, PDF p.173

3.17.2.4 It shall be possible from balise group to order the on-board equipment to operate a system version.

Clause 3.17.2.5 — On receiving the order to operate system version from balise group, the on-board

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.17.2.5, PDF p.173

3.17.2.5 On receiving the order to operate system version from balise group, the on-board equipment shall immediately operate the system version number X given in the order. After the order is executed, the requirement 3.17.2.3 shall be again applied for any further received balise telegram/loop message or any further contacted RIU.

Clause 3.6.0 — Chapter 3

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.173

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP

Clause 3.17.2.5.1 — Note: the system version order is to be used wherever it is necessary to enforce an

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.17.2.5.1, PDF p.174

3.17.2.5.1 Note: the system version order is to be used wherever it is necessary to enforce an operated system version number X lower than the currently operated one.

Clause 3.17.2.6 — If a mismatch has been detected between the country or region identifier read from a

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.17.2.6, PDF p.174

3.17.2.6 If a mismatch has been detected between the country or region identifier read from a balise/loop and the corresponding identifier(s) for which a set of national values is used onboard, the on-board equipment shall consider the system version number X transmitted by this balise/loop as the operated one and shall comply again with requirement 3.17.2.3.

Clause 3.17.2.7 — If the on-board equipment does not support the system version number X transmitted

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.17.2.7, PDF p.174

3.17.2.7 If the on-board equipment does not support the system version number X transmitted by a non-RBC trackside constituent or the one specified in the balise group order, it shall consider the operated system version as unchanged.

Clause 3.17.2.8 — In case of communication session established with an RBC, the system version

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.17.2.8, PDF p.174

3.17.2.8 In case of communication session established with an RBC, the system version number X of the RBC shall take precedence on the operated system version in relation to non-RBC constituents and on system version ordered from balise group; the operated system version number X shall be determined according to the following principles: a) if the on-board equipment is in level 0, NTC or 1 (e.g. entrance in level 2/3 area), the RBC system version number X shall be operated when the transition to level 2/3 is executed; b) if the on-board equipment is in level 2/3 (SoM procedure or order received from trackside), the RBC system version number X shall be operated immediately; c) in case of session established with an accepting RBC (RBC/RBC Handover), the accepting RBC system version number X shall be operated as soon as the engine has passed the RBC/RBC border location with its maximum safe front end; d) in case the on-board equipment switches from level 2/3 to another level (e.g. exit from a level 2/3 area), the system version control in relation to non-RBC constituents shall be again applied and the balise group orders shall be again considered; e) in case the engine passes the RBC/RBC border location with its maximum safe front end and no session is established with the accepting RBC, the system version control in relation to non-RBC constituents shall be again applied and the balise group orders shall be again considered.

Clause 3.17.2.9 — The system version currently operated when the on-board equipment is switched off

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.17.2.9, PDF p.174

3.17.2.9 The system version currently operated when the on-board equipment is switched off (i.e. enters No Power mode) shall be retained and re-used when powered on.

Clause 3.17.2.9.1 — If the on-board equipment loses the information (failure situation), the highest

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.17.2.9.1, PDF p.174

3.17.2.9.1 If the on-board equipment loses the information (failure situation), the highest supported system version shall be used.

Clause 3.17.3.5 — The on-board equipment shall check the ERTMS/ETCS system version number X

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.17.3.5, PDF p.175

3.17.3.5 The on-board equipment shall check the ERTMS/ETCS system version number X transmitted by any balise: a) In all levels, if this system version number X equals to 0, the balise information shall be ignored. b) In all levels, if this system version number X is different from 0 and lower than the lowest system version number X supported by the on-board equipment, it shall be able to interpret the balise information, to the extent defined for each type of information (see chapter 6 for detailed requirements). If the on-board is not able to interpret the information, this shall be considered as a message consistency error. c) In all levels, if this system version number X is amongst its supported ones, the on- board equipment shall be able to interpret the balise information. See chapter 6 for detailed requirements. d) In levels 1, 2 and 3, if this system version number X is greater than the highest version number X supported by the on-board equipment, the information from this balise shall be ignored, the train shall be tripped and an indication shall be given to the driver. e) In levels 0 and NTC, if this system version number X is greater than the highest version number X supported by the on-board equipment, the information from this balise shall be ignored and no reaction shall be applied.

Clause 3.17.3.6 — In level 1 the on-board equipment shall check the ERTMS/ETCS system version

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.17.3.6, PDF p.175

3.17.3.6 In level 1 the on-board equipment shall check the ERTMS/ETCS system version number X transmitted by any Euroloop found: a) if this system version number X is lower than the lowest system version number X supported by the on-board equipment, it shall be able to interpret the loop information, to the extent defined for each type of information (see chapter 6 for detailed requirements). If the on-board is not able to interpret the information, this shall be considered as a message consistency error. b) if this system version number X is amongst its supported ones, the on-board equipment shall be able to interpret the loop information. See chapter 6 for detailed requirements.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.6.0, PDF p.175

3.6.0 Chapter 3 13/05/2016 Principles ERA * UNISIG * EEIG ERTMS USERS GROUP c) If this system version number X is greater than the highest version number X supported by the on-board equipment, no reaction shall be applied and the information from this loop shall be ignored.

3.17.3.7 The on-board equipment shall check the ERTMS/ETCS system version number X transmitted the first time any RBC is contacted (including RBC hand over) or any RIU is contacted. Refer to section 3.5.3.7 d) for details.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.17.3.11, PDF p.176

3.17.3.11 For trackside information only differing by Y with regards to the highest system version number X supported by on-board, the on-board equipment shall not consider the reception of unknown packet/message as a message data consistency error (i.e. use of spare value for NID_PACKET or NID_MESSAGE) and shall ignore the content of the unknown packet/message in the following cases: a) unknown packet included in a balise telegram/loop message related to the higher system version; b) unknown radio message from an RBC or RIU operating with the higher system version; c) unknown packet from an RBC or RIU operating with the higher system version, included in a message in which one or more optional packet can be added according to the version operated by on-board.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.18.2.2, PDF p.177

3.18.2.2 Trains shall be supervised according to the National Values of the current infrastructure if they are available on-board.

Clause 3.18.2.3 — National Values are transmitted with the area(s) (country or region) in which they are

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.18.2.3, PDF p.177

3.18.2.3 National Values are transmitted with the area(s) (country or region) in which they are applicable. They shall become applicable at a defined location, or shall be applicable immediately.

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.18.2.4, PDF p.177

3.18.2.4 Evaluating a balise group message, the balise identity information referring to the country or region shall be used to ensure that correct National Values are used.

Clause 3.18.2.5 — For each National Value, the corresponding Default Value shall be used as fall back

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.18.2.5, PDF p.177

3.18.2.5 For each National Value, the corresponding Default Value shall be used as fall back value if:  the National Value is not available, or  a mismatch has been detected between the country or region identifier read from a balise group and the corresponding identifier(s) of the applicable set with which the National Value was received and stored.

Clause 3.18.2.6 — Note: even though the National Values are always transmitted as a single set for a

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.18.2.6, PDF p.177

3.18.2.6 Note: even though the National Values are always transmitted as a single set for a given system version, the content of a set depends on the system version, so that when a set of National Values is received or becomes applicable, or when passing a balise group, the on-board equipment may apply clause 3.18.2.5 for a subset of National Values

Clause 3.18.2.7 — The National Values currently applicable when the on-board equipment is switched off

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.18.2.7, PDF p.177

3.18.2.7 The National Values currently applicable when the on-board equipment is switched off (i.e. enters No Power mode) shall be retained and shall remain applicable when powered on.

Clause 3.18.2.7.1 — Justification: The aim of this requirement is to limit the number of balise groups

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.18.2.7.1, PDF p.177

3.18.2.7.1 Justification: The aim of this requirement is to limit the number of balise groups containing National Value information. Once a set of National Values has been received on-board, there is no need to re-load the information unless National Values change, the on-board equipment loses the information (failure situation), or the train enters an area requiring different National Values.

Clause 3.18.2.8 — The applicable set of National Values data shall be transmitted from the trackside on

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.18.2.8, PDF p.177

3.18.2.8 The applicable set of National Values data shall be transmitted from the trackside on transition between areas requiring a different set of National Values.

Clause 3.18.2.8.1 — When a new set of National Values becomes applicable its content shall always

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.18.2.8.1, PDF p.177

3.18.2.8.1 When a new set of National Values becomes applicable its content shall always overwrite the corresponding National Values currently applicable regardless of the country or region identifier(s).

Clause 3.18.2.9 — A previously received set of National Values which is not yet applicable shall be

Citation: SUBSET-026 v3.6.0, clause 3.18.2.9, PDF p.177

3.18.2.9 A previously received set of National Values which is not yet applicable shall be deleted if:  a new set of National Values is received, or  the ERTMS/ETCS on-board equipment is switched off (i.e., enters No Power mode).
